- v3.75 — The + button's travelling sheen now stays inside the glass (Ezra: "the little shine over the create button looks shit… make it so the shine effect is inside the button so you only see it over the button" — meaning the moving highlight, not the built-in glow). He diagnosed it exactly: the sheen is a full-size layer that slides ±62% across the button, and with nothing clipping it the bright band kept travelling past the rim, so you saw a rectangular streak floating outside the circle. One line fixes it — `overflow: hidden` on the button — because overflow respects border-radius, so the highlight now crosses the glass and vanishes at the edge like light moving over a real lens. Outer box-shadows are untouched (overflow only clips children), so the glow and the colour throw are exactly as they were. Also narrowed the band (38–62% instead of 30–62%) and dropped it from .30 to .26 opacity so it reads as a glint rather than a swipe. Same clipping applied to the editor's + FAB. Verified by freezing the animation mid-sweep and photographing it: the band terminates at the rim on both.
- v3.76 — The + button's sheen, done properly (Ezra: "just have it masked to the button then make it bigger than it needs to be so it doesn't go too small"). v3.75 masked it but left the highlight layer exactly the button's size, so once clipped, most of the sweep happened outside the circle and only a sliver was ever visible — the mask was right, the layer was too small. The layer is now 2× the button (124px over a 62px button, centred), so a full-width diagonal band is inside the mask for the whole crossing. Travel is ±40% of that oversized layer, which parks the band just off each side: measured across the sweep, the band centre moves −19px → 2px → 37px → 70px on a 62px button, i.e. it enters off one edge and leaves off the other. That also means no opacity fades are needed to hide the loop reset (the old version faded in and out mid-button, which is part of why it looked wrong). Band widened to 34–66% of the layer and brightened to .38 at its core so it reads as a proper glint. Verified by pausing the animation mid-sweep and photographing it.
- v3.77 — Keyboard shortcuts no longer drag you into a project (Ezra: "make the keyboard shortcuts thing that pops up not force you into a project if ur not in one"). Home's ⋯ → Shortcuts was literally `FM.home.close(); FM.shortcuts.toggle();` — reading the shortcut list closed the home screen and dropped you into whatever project was last open (and on a fresh install, into a newly created one). It now just opens the overlay, on top of home, and closing it leaves you exactly where you were; the overlay's z-index went 200 → 230 so it sits above the home screen and its chrome instead of tying with it. Making it reachable from a phone exposed a second problem worth fixing while it was on screen: the card had no height limit, so on a 380×820 screen the list overflowed the viewport — the "Shortcuts & tips" title was cut off above the top edge and Close sat past the bottom. The card is now bounded to 86vh with its own scroll and sized to the screen (its 360px min-width + 48px padding overflowed a 380px phone), and Close became a sticky full-width footer with its own opaque surface, because as a bare sticky button the list scrolled visibly underneath it. Verified in both themes and from both entry points (home and editor).
- v3.78 — The glint now finishes its motion instead of cutting out (Ezra: "the glint just turns off instead of fully doing the motion, making it look like it just cuts out"). Real geometry bug, mine: v3.76 sized the travel by the band's CENTRE and ignored the band's own width. At ±40% the centre landed 8px past the button edge — but the band is ~50px wide, so roughly a third of it was still lying on the glass when the loop reset and snapped it away. Exactly the cut-out he described. Travel is now ±100% of the layer (its full width each way), which puts the ENTIRE layer clear of the button at both ends: measured in button coordinates, the layer spans −155..−31 at the start of the cycle and 92..216 at the end, against a 0..62 button — nothing of it can be on the glass when the animation wraps, so the reset is invisible. Timing function went ease-in-out → linear as well: a glint that decelerates on its way out looks like it's stalling, not travelling. Net result per 7s cycle — 5.7s idle, a 1.26s sweep, of which 0.94s is actually crossing the glass: it enters at one edge, crosses, and leaves at the other.
- v3.79 — The full blend-mode set, reordered and renamed as ours (Ezra's five AM screenshots + "reorder the rows so it doesn't look like I was copying alight motion, put mask just below normal, and give them slightly different names"). 25 modes now, up from the 7 the picker actually exposed — the compositor already knew 17 native Canvas ops but the UI only ever listed ONE per family, so most of them were unreachable. NINE MODES CANVAS CANNOT DO were built from scratch in a new js/blend-modes.js: Linear Burn, Deepest/Brightest Colour, Gentle Overlay, Vivid Light, Linear Light, Pin Light, Minus, Ratio — each a tight per-pixel loop using the W3C alpha formula, hooked into drawLayer the same way mask blending already was (rasterise the layer as one plate with a neutral blend, then combine by hand), reading back only the plate's alpha bounds rather than the whole frame. A 9-agent workflow specced the maths per family, wrote the module, and computed ground truth in python independently of the implementation; the shipped renderer was then measured against it — 21 modes × 6 colour probes, worst channel delta 1 of 255. Three real bugs came out of that pass and are fixed: the manual hook sat BELOW the 3D-tilt branch, so tilting a Vivid Light layer one degree silently dropped it back to Normal (verified fixed: tilted vivid-light ≠ tilted normal); Brightest Colour compared luminance-weighted values while Deepest Colour compared a raw channel sum, so the pair disagreed about which of a pair was lighter vs darker on 17% of saturated colours; and Add was mapped to Canvas 'lighter', which is Porter-Duff PLUS, not Linear Dodge — correct only at full opacity, and out by up to 63 code values with the opacity slider moved (measured here: Add at 50% now 228,188,148 where the old path gave 230,210,150). The legacy 'add' id still maps to 'lighter' so existing projects render byte-identically; the picker offers the corrected one. ORDER AND NAMES ARE OURS: Basic → Cutout → Brighten → Deepen → Punch → Tint → Invert, with Cutout second because keeping or punching a hole is a constant-reach tool, not a footnote under the colour maths. Labels use Australian spelling and plain words for the same operations — Stencil / Punch Out, Brightest Colour, Deepest Colour, Colour Dodge, Colour Burn, Gentle Overlay, Colourise, Luminance, Minus, Ratio — while the standards the whole industry shares (Multiply, Screen, Overlay, Difference, Hue…) keep their names, because renaming those would only make the app harder to use. Every id underneath is unchanged, so projects, presets and AI ops are unaffected by what a row is called. Cost: native modes 0.15ms/frame, the per-pixel ones 2.5–5.6ms — real but well inside a 33ms frame, and only paid by layers actually using them.
- v3.80 — Shapes stay sharp when you zoom in (Ezra's side-by-side: an Alight Motion star with a razor edge vs ours smeared and soft at full zoom). NOT an anti-aliasing problem — the preview canvas was created at PROJECT resolution (canvas.width = P.width) and the zoom was a CSS transform on the wrapper, so the GPU was stretching a 1080px bitmap. At 4× on a phone that spreads 1080 pixels across ~4600 device pixels: every vector edge becomes a 4px smear, exactly as photographed. Alight Motion re-rasterises the vector at the zoomed size; now so do we. The preview's backing store is sized to the device pixels it actually occupies (CSS width × devicePixelRatio × zoom), and the compositor renders through a matching base transform, so shapes, text and drawings are re-rasterised rather than magnified. THE SAFE PART, because this is the highest-risk file in the app: the scale lives ON the canvas as `__fmRS`, not in a module variable. Comp-space scratch canvases are never stamped, so they resolve to 1 and every existing plate/effect/mask path behaves exactly as before; export and thumbnails render into unstamped canvases and are untouched by construction. All 55 `setTransform(1,0,0,1,0,0)` resets became `baseT(ctx)`, which restores whatever scale that particular canvas carries — one rule, no per-site judgement. Verified by rendering the same scene (star + blurred ellipse + a Vivid Light layer + text) at 1× and at 3×, downsampling the 3× and comparing: on shapes and the manual blend the flat interiors came back mean 0.008, max 4 — geometry is identical, every difference is edge quality. Adding text pushes the outliers to 51, which is glyph rasterisation genuinely improving at 3×, not geometry moving. Found and fixed one real bug on the way: drawManualBlendLayer assumed the target was project-sized, so on a supersampled canvas it blended the wrong region — its plate now matches the target's pixel grid (which also makes manual blends sharp). Measured on a simulated 390px preview at dpr 2: zoom 1× already had enough pixels and is unchanged, 2× now lands exactly 1.00 backing pixel per device pixel, and 4×/8× improve 2.4×. HONEST LIMIT: past ~2× zoom a 12MP budget caps it (a 1080×1920 comp at 4× would want a 33MP / 132MB canvas, which iOS will not give us), so extreme zoom is much better but not pixel-perfect; getting there needs viewport-cropped rendering — only the visible region at full res — which is a follow-up, not done here.
- v3.81 — Person and Woman redrawn as the international restroom pictogram (Ezra's reference image: "re design the male and female shapes to look identical to this"). The old pair were built from separate shoulder/elbow/wrist joint polygons — anatomically fussy and nothing like a sign. Both are now the classic construction: a detached head circle, ONE solid body, and two legs below, with the man's arms hanging flush to the torso and stepping in slightly at the wrist, and the woman's A-line dress leaving the shoulder at a shallow angle before the skirt kicks out to a square-cornered hem. Proportions are measured off the reference rather than eyeballed — head radius 0.088, shoulders at 0.205, hands at 0.600, crotch/hem at ~0.67/0.71, feet at 0.985 — and both figures are written as named constants deriving every point, so the two stay a matched pair when either is tweaked (they must read as a set in a sign). Two rounds of visual correction against the reference: the first pass domed the shoulders and flared the dress too early, which made the man chunky and the woman conical; shoulder round went 0.058 → 0.036, wrist round 0.050 → 0.032, and the dress's arm line now runs to 0.545 before the hem flare. Verified by drawing them through the app's own path builder at 520px AND through the real shape-layer render path.
- v3.82 — Viewport-cropped rendering: full sharpness at any zoom (the follow-up flagged in v3.80, "do it, if it will make things better"). v3.80 supersampled the WHOLE comp, which a pixel budget then capped — a 1080×1920 comp at 4× wanted 33MP and got clamped to 2.4×. But at 4× only about a sixteenth of the comp is on screen, so the fix is to paint just the visible slice at full device resolution: measured on a simulated 390×700 phone at 4× zoom, the visible region is 25%×25% of the frame, so with an 18% safety margin it renders 367×659 comp px — 3.9MP instead of 33MP, i.e. TRUE 1:1 device pixels for a ninth of the memory the capped version used. The canvas element is repositioned inside the comp wrapper to cover exactly that region (percentages of the wrap), while the wrapper keeps its comp-sized box — so selection handles, guides and hit-testing, which all work in comp space, are untouched. The base transform now carries a crop origin as well as a scale, and unstamped canvases still resolve to scale 1 / origin 0,0, so export, thumbnails and effect plates remain untouched by construction. Verified the crop transform is EXACT by rendering a 120×110 comp region directly and comparing it to the same window cut out of a full render: 0.4% of pixels differ, max 26, mean 0.009 — sub-perceptual antialiasing noise, not a shift (a one-pixel misalignment would show full-contrast diffs along every edge). AND THE IMPORTANT CATCH: this work exposed that v3.80 had silently broken direct manipulation. canvas-edit's eventToProject mapped a pointer through canvas.width, which assumed canvas pixels == project pixels — true before v3.80, false the moment the preview was supersampled, so a drag moved a layer by the supersample factor too far. Pointer mapping now goes via the canvas' own scale and crop origin. Proven by driving real pointer events through the drag path: a 30 CSS-px drag moves the layer 108 project px in both 1:1 and 2.5× supersampled mode, and at matched on-screen zoom the full-comp and cropped paths both move it 36.0 — cropping is invisible to interaction. Falls back to whole-comp rendering whenever zoom is under 1.35×, the crop would cover most of the frame anyway, or the layout can't be measured.
- v3.83 — Adaptive playback quality: smooth on a phone, full detail on a fast machine (Ezra: "alight motion is good at mitigating lag by lowering the playback quality… you can see a difference when you're still looking at one frame and when you press playback… I want it to run great on phones but also be pushed harder on a powerful computer"). He read it exactly right — we were pushing full quality every frame regardless of what the device could hold. Now the preview drops resolution WHILE PLAYING and snaps back to full the instant you pause, which is the visible "it sharpens when you stop" moment other editors have. It's adaptive rather than a fixed setting because that's what makes one build work on both ends: each played frame is timed (the render itself, not the rAF gap — that's the part we control), and the tier moves one step at a time through 1.0 / 0.8 / 0.62 / 0.48 / 0.36 / 0.28 with hysteresis and a ~0.4s cooldown so it settles instead of oscillating. Struggling above 72% of a frame's budget sheds pixels; sitting under 30% gives detail back, so a fast desktop climbs to 1.0 and effectively never leaves it while a phone sinks to a tier it can actually hold. This dropped straight into the render-scale plumbing from v3.80 — quality is just a smaller __fmRS — and it stacks with the v3.82 viewport crop, so a zoomed-in playback pays twice over. Settings → Playback quality: Auto (measures the machine), Smooth (pins it low for a slow device), Sharp (never trades quality). Verified deterministically: paused 1080×1920, playing on Smooth 670×1190 (0.62×, ~39% of the pixels), Sharp stays 1080×1920, Auto at tier 0 stays full, and pausing restores full every time. HONEST GAP: the adaptive stepping itself could not be exercised here — the preview pane is hidden in this environment and requestAnimationFrame is suspended, so no real playback frames run. Its inputs (frame cost measurement) and outputs (canvas resize per tier) are both verified; the loop between them needs a real device to watch.
- v3.84 — Tapping outside the Add sheet closes it and clears the selection (Ezra: "when you're in the add menu and tap anywhere else it closes it and de selects the clip"). There was already a global empty-tap→deselect handler, but it bailed out early in two ways that made this not work: `#add-sheet` and friends sit in a KEEP list so taps on the canvas/timeline never reached it, and it returned immediately when nothing was selected. The sheet is now handled first, before the KEEP check, because the canvas and the timeline are exactly where you tap when you've changed your mind. The + FAB is excluded — it already toggles, and closing here too would cancel its own tap. Drags are excluded as well, so scrubbing the timeline or dragging on the canvas doesn't dismiss the sheet mid-gesture. Verified by driving real pointer events: tap inside → stays open; tap the canvas → closes and deselects; tap the timeline → closes and deselects; drag outside → stays open.
- v3.85 — Favouriting from Recents actually works now, + Film Grain (Ezra: "make it so you can fave an effect from the recently used menu" and "add film grain effect"). ON THE ★: the feature already existed — the Recents page uses the same tile as everywhere else and the toggle logic was correct (verified: a programmatic click moved the effect into fm.fx.fav). The problem was purely physical: the star was a bare 13×13px glyph pinned in the tile corner with no padding, so on a phone you either missed it or hit the tile and added the effect instead. Padding plus a negative offset grows the hit area to 30×30 without moving the star or changing the layout, and it gains a shadow (it was invisible over bright thumbnails) and a press response. FILM GRAIN is deliberately not the existing Noise effect with a new name — flat per-pixel RGB noise reads as a broken sensor, so this models what film actually does: grain lives in the MIDTONES (a 4·L·(1−L) response, since silver halide has little to do in blacks and highlights clip), it CLUMPS via one hash per grain cell rather than per pixel, it is near-monochrome by default because dye clouds scatter luminance far more than colour, and it re-rolls every frame — static grain reads as dirt on the lens. Four controls: Amount, Grain size (1–6), Colour (0 = pure luminance), and In shadows (how much survives into the blacks). Measured all four claims rather than eyeballing: mid-grey std-dev 15.3 vs 1.8 near-white and 6.1 near-black (midtone-weighted), adjacent-pixel sameness 5% at size 1 vs 77% at size 4 (clumping), 88% of pixels re-rolled between frames (animated), R-vs-B spread 0.0 at colour 0% vs 18.1 at 100%, and amount 0 is a byte-exact passthrough. Featured list leads with it per the standing rule. NOTE FOR NEXT TIME: the first test run "failed" the clumping check because I passed params at the top level of the effect object — per-pixel effects read them from `fx.params`. The effect was right; the test was wrong.
- v3.86 — Drag-select on the Home screen, and the glass look is now "Liquid" (Ezra: "when you are selecting in the Home Screen you can do the drag thing that we have elsewhere to drag select a lot" + "make the name for the glass look option be cooler"). The gesture is deliberately the SAME one the timeline already uses on track heads, so the app feels like one thing: the selection a drag makes is always the span anchor→current card, which means dragging back the way you came un-selects what you passed instead of making you undo it by hand, and cards ticked before the gesture are never disturbed. Two ways in, matching the timeline: already selecting → a drag paints immediately; not selecting → HOLD a card for 380ms to enter select mode and start painting from there. Auto-scrolls when your finger nears the top or bottom of the list so a long run is reachable. It deliberately does NOT re-render mid-gesture — render() rebuilds the grid and would detach the card under your finger — so classes are painted straight onto the elements and the bar reconciles on release. The subtle part was touch: a drag on a card is also how you SCROLL the list, so `touch-action: none` is applied only while `body.hm-selecting` is set; outside select mode the list scrolls exactly as before. Verified with real pointer events: drag 1st→3rd selects 3, dragging back to the 2nd drops to 2, release keeps 2, a plain tap while selecting still toggles (2→3), a hold enters select mode with 1 ticked, and — the regression that would have mattered most — a plain tap outside select mode still opens the project it was on.
- v3.87 — Radial warps can finally happen somewhere other than the middle of the frame (first build round from the effect-depth audit). Twirl, Pinch/Bulge, Fisheye and Kaleidoscope were all welded to the frame centre and the frame diagonal — you could turn the amount up or down and nothing else, so a vortex could only ever sit dead centre and never on a face. Each now has Centre X / Centre Y, plus Radius (Lens size on Fisheye) and, on Kaleidoscope, a Rotation phase that makes the star spin — something it simply could not do before, and which keyframes for free like every other param. THE RULE THIS ROUND WAS BUILT AROUND: an existing project must render byte-for-byte as it did. Every new param's fallback is the exact constant the code hardcoded (centre 50% → W/2 and H/2, radius 100% → maxR), and the helpers short-circuit on those values rather than recomputing them, so no float drift can creep in. Verified: a legacy instance and one with explicit defaults are 0 differing bytes across all four effects, while centre 25/25 moves 19,170 bytes, radius 40% moves 10,928, kaleidoscope phase 45° moves 3,089 and a 45% lens moves 18,653. Also of note from the audit that produced this: 111 effects were audited and 105 want more control, and the ranking pass DISPROVED several agents' backwards-compatibility claims by brute-forcing the float maths — bumpmap's proposed 225° default computes to −0.5000000000000001, not the −0.5 it hardcodes, and a vignette falloff rewrite would not survive canvas gradient-stop quantisation. Those are flagged, not built.
- v3.88 — Six more effects stop being one-slider toys (round 2 of the depth audit). WAVE gets wavelength, phase and a cross-wave amount: phase is the one that matters, because a wave that cannot shift phase cannot TRAVEL — keyframe it 0→360 and the ripple runs across the frame, which was simply impossible before. The second axis keeps its old 46/38 ratio so one control moves both without changing the default look. RIPPLE and CURL get the same treatment plus a centre, and their phase is subtracted rather than added so a rising value sends the rings OUTWARD, the way water actually behaves. RAYS gets a light source position (−50%..150%, so the sun can sit off-frame), an intensity that was welded at 0.6, and a rotation — god rays from where the light actually is instead of a wheel pinned to the frame centre. BLOCK NOISE exposes the two numbers that define the glitch and were the only ones you could not touch: block size (6px) and rate (8Hz), plus an aspect so 0.15 gives tape-dropout bars. NOISE gets speed, grain size and colour — and speed 0 FREEZES the static, which is the single most useful thing it could not do (animated noise strobed under a keyframed opacity). Verified: all six render 0 differing bytes between a legacy instance and one with explicit defaults, every new control moves thousands of bytes, and frozen noise is byte-identical at t=0 and t=2s.
- v3.89 — The three glows and the two generators get real controls, and a bug in my own previous method got caught (round 3 of the depth audit). LIGHT GLOW / DARK GLOW / SOFT GLOW each gain Radius and Threshold — the two numbers that decide what actually blooms and how far it spreads, both previously welded shut (light 153/255 with a 6px box blur, dark 102/255, soft 90/255 scaled off the frame's short side). Threshold is the one that changes the feel: at the old fixed value only near-blowout highlights bloomed, so a moody shot with no bright pixels got nothing at all; drop it and the glow finds the midtones. CLOUDS gains Scale, Drift and a Tint colour, and drift is what makes it weather rather than wallpaper — the fractal actually moves through the frame over time instead of sitting still. IRIDESCENCE gains Scale, Bands and Speed, so the oil-slick sheen can travel across the surface. CAUGHT A BUG IN MY OWN WORK, WHICH IS THE REAL STORY HERE: light glow and dark glow read their new threshold inside the brightness loop while `var lgThr = …` sat BELOW that loop — `var` hoists, so the value was `undefined` during the loop, `undefined/100*255` is NaN, every `L > NaN` comparison is false, and the glow mask came out all zeros. Both effects rendered NOTHING — not even the amount slider did anything. It slipped through because the identity test compared a legacy instance against explicit defaults and both sides were equally broken, so "0 differing bytes" was a false pass. THE FIX TO THE METHOD: identity is now proven against the PREVIOUS COMMIT rather than against another run of the new code — `git show HEAD:js/compositor.js` is served alongside, both function bodies are extracted and run on the same synthetic RGBA buffer with no scene graph in the way, and old-vs-new must be 0 bytes. Re-ran every effect touched in v3.87 and v3.88 through it: rays, block noise, noise, all three glows, clouds, iridescence and all seven warps (wave, ripple, twirl, bulge, fisheye, kaleidoscope, curl) are 0 differing bytes against HEAD, and every new control moves thousands. Two things that looked like failures were my test being wrong, not the code: noise takes amount as 0–100 (I passed 0.6, i.e. half a level of grain), and iridescence's hue wraps with period 1.0, so testing speed 3 at t=0 vs t=2 compares phase 0 against phase 6 — the same hue by definition. Tested at 2.5 and it moves 20,626 bytes.
- v3.90 — Mirror gets a movable seam, Vignette a size, Hot Color six palettes and a range, Threshold softness and two colours (round 4 of the depth audit). MIRROR was welded to the exact half-frame: the reflection could only ever happen down the dead centre, so you could never put the seam on a face or an edge. It now takes a Seam % — and past the first reflection the strip MIRROR-TILES instead of stopping, because a seam at 30% would otherwise leave the far 40% of the frame empty, i.e. the effect would silently delete your content. At 50% the loop emits exactly the two drawImage calls it always did. VIGNETTE gets Size, the radius where the darkening starts. Worth knowing: the two vignette code paths have always disagreed — the per-pixel path starts at 35% and the media/video path at 45% — so each keeps its own fallback and no existing project moves, while a new vignette now carries 35 and both paths finally agree on one number. HOT COLOR was one fixed iron ramp; it now has six (Iron, White hot, Black hot, Arctic, Magma, Spectrum) plus Low/High to say which part of the tonal range the ramp is spread across, which is the difference between orange mush and an actual heat read. THRESHOLD gets Softness — a smoothstep instead of a hard binary cut, so edges stop aliasing — and a colour either side, so it becomes a two-tone poster tool rather than only black and white. Threshold also had its loop duplicated in two files' worth of code paths (per-layer and adjustment-layer); both now call one shared kernel, which is the only way they stay in step. Verified with the harness from v3.89: vignette and thermal are 0 differing bytes against the previous commit for a legacy instance AND for explicit schema defaults, and every new control moves 14k–27k bytes. Mirror was checked structurally instead of by diff, which is stronger — all four directions are pixel-exact symmetric about the seam (0 mismatched pairs out of 60) at both the default and a moved position, a legacy instance is identical to an explicit position 50, and nothing is dropped past the seam. Threshold: a legacy instance is still strictly binary (the only red value in the frame is 255), identical to explicit defaults, softness 60 yields 129 on mid-grey and 41 on a darker grey (a real ramp, not a constant), and both code paths return the same colour for the same input.
- v3.91 — Audio can now drive EFFECT parameters, not just move/scale/fade. This was flagged in EFFECTS-PLAN.md as the single highest-leverage change in the whole plan — worth more than any individual effect — because it doesn't add one feature, it makes the catalogue you already have beat-reactive. Audio → keyframes could previously only write to five transform props (scale, opacity, rotation, x, y). It can now bake onto any effect control: bass drives Twirl's twist, the kick drives Glitch amount, treble drives blur radius. Measured reach: 169 of the 174 effects have at least one drivable control, 365 controls in total. The Property dropdown rebuilds itself for whichever layer you target, with a group per effect on that layer, and it only offers controls that a continuous envelope can actually drive — a colour swatch or a segmented choice like Mirror's direction is excluded rather than offered and silently ignored. Range and unit come from the same registry schema the inspector's own slider uses, so picking Blur radius gives you px and 0–50, and Twirl Centre X gives you % and 0–100; the default range is "quiet = where the control sits right now, loud = the top of its range", so a bake never yanks the look somewhere unrecognisable on the first try. Values are clamped to the control's own domain, so a hot envelope can't push blur to 200px. Targets are stored as fx:<index>:<key> — the index, not an id, because makeInstance has never stamped ids and position in the effects array is the only stable handle. Verified: baking onto Blur radius wrote 28 keyframes that evaluate to 25.00 → 48.67 → 11.16 → 10.25 across the clip (it genuinely animates), every value landed inside 0–50, the dropdown groups render correctly, the unit and range fields update when you switch control, and both failure modes return 0 without throwing — a stale effect index and a colour param that can't be driven. Checked at 375px: the sheet still fits with the extra grouping, Apply/Cancel reachable.
- v3.92 — Five colour tools stop being single sliders, and Polar Coordinates gets the half of itself that was missing (round 5). POLAR COORDINATES could only ever wrap a rectangle into a circle; the INVERSE — unwrapping a circular image into a straight strip, a clock face into a timeline, a tunnel into a wall — simply had no way to be reached. Both directions now sit under one Direction control. Proved it is the true inverse rather than merely a different-looking map: running rect→polar and then polar→rect on 289 sample points returns every one of them to within 1px of where it started. GAMMA gets per-channel Red/Green/Blue trims that multiply the master, which is how you kill a colour cast — a single master curve can only make a cast lighter or darker, never remove it. COLOR TEMPERATURE gets the other axis of a white balance: Tint (green ↔ magenta), so a fluorescent green cast is finally correctable here, plus Keep brightness, which rescales each pixel back to its original luminance so a warm-up stops also being a brighten. Measured: mean luma 128.28 before, 135.09 after a +80 warm-up, 128.16 with Keep brightness at 100%. VIBRANCE gets the two guards that stop a vibrance pass looking wrong — Protect skin (orange-red hues) and Protect highlights (pixels near clipping, where extra saturation only shifts hue). Measured on swatches at amount 1.8: a skin tone gains 49 points of saturation normally and 5 with the guard at full, while blue sky (51 → 51) and green foliage (48 → 48) are untouched — it is genuinely selective, not a global weakening. GLOW gets Bloom, which stacks the drop-shadow 1–4 times: one pass is a halo, three is something that reads as light. At 1 it emits the exact same filter string it always did, character for character. Verified with the harness: gamma, temperature and vibrance are all 0 differing bytes against the previous commit for both a legacy instance and explicit schema defaults, polar coordinates' displacement field is identical at mode 0 across every sampled point, and every new control moves the render.
- v3.93 — Spectral Map gets the third of its spectrum that was unreachable, plus depth on Exposure, Colorize and Duotone (round 6). SPECTRAL MAP had a real bug hiding behind its hardcoded numbers: the HSV wedge chain stopped at 300°, with everything above folded into the 240–300 formula. Because the sweep was welded to 260°, that branch could never be reached — so the bug was invisible until the span became adjustable. It now has the missing 300–360 branch, and Spectrum span, Hue offset and Saturation. Proved the new branch is real, not just present: a near-black input at span 360 now maps to 68,0,10 — red-dominant, which is what that wedge should be — where the old final else would have produced a blue-dominant magenta. EXPOSURE was a bare multiply that hard-clips, so pushing a stop up smashed highlights into flat white and there was no way to touch the black point at all (multiplying never moves black). It gains Black point and Highlight rolloff, the latter a soft shoulder that is asymptotic to 255 rather than clipping. COLORIZE had its shadow floor hardcoded at 0.25, fixing the contrast of every wash, and could only replace the image — never sit over it. It gains Shadow lift and a Blend mode (Tone / Multiply / Screen / Overlay). DUOTONE's luma→colour ramp was a straight line, so the handover between shadow and highlight colour was fixed and every duotone landed in the same place; it gains Balance to move that handover and Contrast to widen or flatten the ramp. Duotone was also the second effect this week found holding two copies of the same loop (per-layer and adjustment-layer), which is exactly how the two drift apart — both now call one shared kernel, same as threshold in v3.90. Verified: exposure, colorize and spectral map are 0 differing bytes against the previous commit for legacy instances and explicit defaults; duotone was checked against an independently hand-computed reference of the original ramp (133.9, 85.0, 87.4 vs a rendered 134, 85, 87) and both its code paths return identical pixels at defaults AND with balance engaged. One thing worth recording because it nearly shipped: rewriting these catalogue entries by hand, I typed exposure's default as 0.5 when it has always been 0.8, and dropped its ' EV' unit and colorize's colour label. Diffing my rewrite against the previous commit's entries caught all three — a new instance would otherwise have quietly come out at the wrong exposure.
- v3.94 — Tint can split-tone, Posterize can choose where its bands fall, Gradient Map can move its handover and dither away its banding (round 7). TINT washed the whole tonal range at once, so split-toning — cool shadows against warm highlights, the single most common reason to reach for a tint — was impossible, and tinting toward a dark colour also crushed the image because the brightness came along for the ride. It gains Range (All / Shadows / Midtones / Highlights) and Keep brightness, which rescales each pixel back to its original luminance so the tint is purely a colour move. Two Tints on one layer now give you a real split-tone. POSTERIZE could only choose how MANY bands, never where they fall or how hard it lands: it gains Band spacing (quantise in a gamma-warped space, so bands cluster into the shadows where stepping actually shows), Quantise = Luma only (bands the tone while the hue survives, instead of collapsing into a colour clash), and Mix to fade it back. GRADIENT MAP joined its two colours with a straight line, so the handover point was fixed, and on video the ramp banded with nothing to break it up — it gains Midpoint and Dither (an 8×8 ordered matrix). Tint and Posterize were the third and fourth effects found holding two copies of the same loop across the per-layer and adjustment-layer paths; both now share one kernel, matching threshold (v3.90) and duotone (v3.93). Verified against independently computed references rather than only against the previous commit: tint's legacy output matches the original formula's 114.0/22.8/45.6 to within a rounding step, posterize's matches its own quantiser exactly, and in both cases the per-layer and adjustment-layer paths return identical pixels. Gradient map is 0 differing bytes against HEAD for legacy and explicit defaults. The dither claim was measured rather than asserted, and the first attempt to measure it was a bad test — a perfectly smooth 96px ramp has a unique value per column, so there was no banding to break. Re-run on a 12-band ramp: 12 flat plateaus become 92 distinct steps at 60% dither. Also caught before shipping: I retyped posterize's Levels maximum as 12 when it has always been 16.
- v3.95 — Six shapes redrawn from Ezra's reference images (thumbs-up, pointing hand, envelope, key, car, check). THE POINTING HAND was the big one: it pointed SIDEWAYS. The whole silhouette has been rebuilt vertically — index finger straight up with a rounded tip, three curled fingers scalloping down the outside of the fist, a round palm bottom and a thumb reaching left, which is what the reference actually shows. THUMBS-UP keeps its separate cuff bar but the cuff is now a proper rounded bar with a clear gap, the thumb sweeps up to a broad round tip, and the four curled fingers are a scalloped edge. That last part was a real lesson: my first attempt cut actual notches between the knuckles, and at icon size they rendered as sawtooth spikes — alternating out/in smooth points reads as fingers, explicit notches read as a saw. The same fix was needed on the pointing hand. THE ENVELOPE was wrong in a more interesting way: cutting the flap V out of the top edge (the obvious approach, and what the old shape did) produces a chevron or a shopping bag, because the eye reads the large notch as the silhouette. The flap has to be a thin GROOVE cut across a full rectangular body — then it reads as an envelope instantly. THE KEY was diagonal with a solid bow; it is now vertical with a real ring (a hole punched in the bow) and teeth stepping out of the left edge. THE CAR gained window holes, wheel arches and solid tyres instead of a flat silhouette with two circles stuck on. CHECK is now a tapered brush tick with rounded caps — built as a centreline offset by a half-width that shrinks toward the top-right — rather than a constant-width chevron that read as a UI glyph. Two pieces of shared machinery came out of this and will pay for themselves: rrectS (a rounded rectangle: a smooth point AT a corner curves THROUGH it and cuts the corner off, so each corner needs its two edge points plus a smooth 45° point), and holeS, which makes a sub-path into a hole. holeS deliberately checks the shoelace sign rather than just reversing the points — blind reversal is what made the envelope's first groove silently fill in solid, because those points already ran the other way. Verified by rendering all six at 260px and comparing against the references, then re-rendering after each fix; the last pass caught the V poking through the envelope's rounded corners and a white crescent where the car's arches cleared the tyres.
- v3.96 — The six shapes are now TRACED from Ezra's references instead of hand-plotted, and ten new shapes join the library. Ezra's verdict on the v3.95 hand-drawn versions was "complete dogshit", and he was right — I was placing points by eye against professionally drawn icons, which is a losing game no matter how many passes I do. So the references get traced properly: threshold the bitmap, find connected components, follow the boundary by chaining unit edges (a Moore-neighbour walk kept terminating on its own seed), average around the closed loop to kill the pixel staircase, simplify with Ramer-Douglas-Peucker, then flag each vertex smooth ONLY where the path genuinely turns gently — so the check keeps its sharp point and the key keeps its teeth while every curve stays a curve. Three things that mattered: taking only the largest ink blob silently dropped the thumbs-up CUFF and reduced the envelope to its flap triangle, because both references are several separate blobs — so the tracer takes the N largest and normalises across all of them together. Holes are found as background regions that don't touch the border, which gets the key's ring and the car's windows for free, and they're wound opposite to the outline (all traced loops come out the same way round, so they must be flipped or they fill in solid). And normalising had to STRETCH to fill the box rather than letterbox, with the reference's true aspect handed to SHAPE_ASPECT instead — letterboxing there and then applying the aspect multiplier pads the shape twice and renders it small inside its own box. TEN NEW SHAPES: Squircle (a real superellipse — the Apple corner, where curvature stays continuous instead of snapping from a straight edge into an arc), Cross, Map pin, Lock, Gear, Crown, Eye, Music note, Starburst and Clock. The cross is a plus rotated 45° rather than an X plotted from corners, because corner-plotted arms come out uneven the moment the box isn't square. IMPORTANT, and written into BEFORE-PUBLISHING.md: the six traced shapes are derivatives of specific stock drawings (two of the references still carried Dreamstime and Shutterstock watermarks). The forms themselves aren't protectable and the risk is narrow, but they need redrawing from scratch before anything ships publicly. The reference images were deliberately not committed.
- v3.97 — Liquid Glass effect, a Smoothing control for Apple corners, and adding an effect now lands you on its controls. LIQUID GLASS is a new effect (Ezra: "add a glass (like apple glass) overlay option as an effect... so it doesn't clash with the others"). It makes the LAYER look like a pane of frosted glass rather than pasting a glass sprite over the frame, and it's four things stacked, because none of them alone reads as glass: a frost blur that is then clipped back to the layer's own crisp alpha (the blur bleeds past the silhouette otherwise), a little of the sharp image left showing through (Clarity), a diagonal specular sheen, and a two-sided bevel — light rim on the lit side, dark rim opposite — built by subtracting an offset copy of the layer's own alpha from itself, which works for any silhouette rather than assuming a rectangle. Controls: Amount, Frost, Clarity, Sheen, Edge, Tint + tint colour, and Light angle. Worth knowing for the see-through look: pair it with Copy Background on a full-frame shape, since the effect frosts the layer it's on, and Copy Background is what puts the scene behind it INTO that layer. APPLE CORNERS ALREADY EXISTED — it's the Corner setting on Rounded Corners and has been the default since it shipped, which is why Ezra couldn't tell whether the button was doing anything. What it was missing is a way to feel it, so it now has Smoothing: the superellipse exponent, where 50 lands on exactly the constant it always used (2 + 50×0.06 = 5) so nothing existing moves. Measured on a 180px box at radius 60: a plain arc starts 18px in from the box corner, the Apple corner 8px in, smoothing 10 gives 14px (nearly circular) and smoothing 100 gives 5px (nearly square) — 2,592 pixels differ between the two styles, so it was always working, just quietly. Also fixed there: the corner polygon was a flat 16 segments per quadrant, which at a 400px radius is a 39px chord and visibly faceted; segments now scale with radius, and anything up to 96px (including the default 80) still resolves to 16, so those renders don't move. ADDING AN EFFECT now opens its editor (Ezra: "currently it just closes the layer"). The code already marked the new effect expanded, but that only decides WHICH row is open — it does nothing when the inspector has fallen back to the category grid, which is exactly what it does after a refresh. It now navigates to Effects explicitly and scrolls the new row into view, since on a phone it usually lands below the fold.
- v3.98 — Tiles closes the gap properly, in both modes (Ezra: "the tiles effect still doesn't close the gap fully and work as it should, work extra hard on that"). There were THREE separate causes, which is why it kept almost working. (1) THE BIG ONE: Extend mode spaces its copies by the layer's measured bounding box, but it was being handed the FAST bounding box — a scan of a 4× downsample, which the code's own comment elsewhere describes as having "~12px slack". Any slack in that rectangle is transparent margin baked into every copy, so it becomes a gutter between them, and no gap setting can remove it because the gap slider isn't what's creating it. Tiles now gets an exact, per-pixel bounding box (like Rounded Corners already did, and for the same reason), and the fixed ±2px inset that was trying to compensate for the slack is gone — it couldn't work, since the error varies with the content. (2) Grid mode divided the frame as W/n, which is fractional for most sizes: 1920÷7 isn't a whole number, so every cell landed on a fractional pixel, canvas resampled the edge column, and the soft result read as a seam. Cell edges now snap to whole pixels and are derived from rounded boundaries, so cell k ends exactly where cell k+1 begins. (3) Even with all that exact, a hairline remained, and the arithmetic explains it: the content's own antialiased boundary column is about 50% alpha, and two of those butted together composite to 75%, not solid. So at gap 0 copies now overlap by TWO pixels — one is not enough (measured: one pixel of overlap gave alpha 192, two gives 255), which puts a fully opaque column under the soft one. Verified by counting seam pixels (interior pixels that aren't fully opaque while both neighbours have coverage): Extend at gap 0 is now 0 seams horizontally and vertically, 0 with mirror on, 0 with an ellipse instead of a rectangle, and Grid is 0 at counts 3, 5 and 7 on a 400×300 frame that divides evenly by none of them. The gap slider still opens real gaps when you actually want them, which was the other half of "work as it should". Note this DOES change existing projects that use Tiles — that is the point, they were rendering the gutter.
- v3.99 — Mask is now an entry in the effect browser, and its own button is gone (Ezra: "make the mask effect an effect in the effects menu and get rid of it having its own button like it is rn"). Mask appears under Matte / Mask / Key alongside Chroma Key and Luma Key, so everything that shapes a layer is reached the same way — one door instead of two. It is deliberately NOT a real effect: the browser intercepts the id and pushes a pen mask onto layer.masks, and nothing is ever added to layer.effects, which matters because a stray effect object of a type the compositor doesn't route would sit there rendering nothing forever. Picking it drops you straight into the drawing tool, exactly as the old button did — a mask you then have to go and find isn't a mask that's been added. It refuses politely on layer types that can't be masked (camera, null, group) rather than adding something inert. The masks list itself stays where it is, under the effect stack, for enabling, inverting, feathering and deleting what's already there; the empty-state hint now points at + Add Effect so there's no dead end where the button used to be. Verified end to end through the real browser UI: clicking Mask produced exactly one mask with the full contract shape (id, enabled, mode, feather, opacity, invert, closed, path), layer.effects stayed empty, and grepping confirms no "+ Add mask" button remains anywhere in the source.
- v4.00 — RGB Split can finally tear in a direction, and Pixelate's blocks stop being square (round 8). RGB SPLIT was locked to the horizontal axis with green pinned at zero, so a vertical or diagonal glitch tear was impossible and real lens fringing — which grows toward the frame edge — could not be described at all. It gains Angle, Toward edges (scales the offset with distance from centre, the way a lens actually fringes), and Green shift so the third channel can move. All three had to be written into BOTH code paths, since RGB Split runs per-layer and again inside the adjustment-layer grade. At angle 0 / radial 0 / green 0 the sampling reduces to the original left-right offsets, and both paths short-circuit to the exact original lines rather than to a general expression that happens to agree. PIXELATE blocks were always square and always hard-edged: it gains Block aspect (25–400%, so 25 gives wide CRT-style cells) and an Edges choice of Blocks or Soft, where Soft simply leaves the upscale interpolated for a low-res look rather than a mosaic. Verified on both paths independently: legacy instances are 0 differing bytes against explicit schema defaults on the per-layer path AND inside an adjustment layer, and every new control moves thousands of bytes. One test I threw away rather than report: I first tried proving the two RGB Split paths render identically, which they don't and shouldn't — the adjustment path grades the composited frame with the background in it, while the per-layer path only ever sees the layer's own plate, so a spatial effect that samples neighbouring pixels legitimately differs between them. Also caught before shipping, and this is the third time: rewriting the catalogue entry by hand I typed Pixelate's block-size maximum as 64 when it has always been 80. Diffing every rewritten entry against the previous commit is now just part of the routine.
- v4.04 — Rounded Corners becomes "Rounded Corners / Apple style", and the Apple option is a tick box that visibly takes over (Ezra: "the option for squircle should be a tick box that overrides everything else, make it obvious it's overriding the other sliders"). The two-button Rounded / Apple picker is gone. In its place, first in the panel, is a TICK BOX called "Apple corners" — deliberately a tick and not a segment, because a segmented control reads as "pick one of these two" while a tick reads as "this takes over", which is exactly what it now does. Ticked, the whole layer becomes ONE continuous superellipse across its full bounds: the app-icon shape, where the curve never stops turning and there is no straight-edge-into-arc junction to size. That is why it ignores Radius rather than reinterpreting it — a squircle's corner is a property of the whole outline, not a radius you dial in. The Radius slider below it dims to 38%, stops responding, and carries a pill reading OVERRIDDEN BY APPLE CORNERS, because a greyed-out control with no explanation just reads as broken. Untick and it is the plain rounded rectangle it always was, with Radius live again. THE SMOOTHING SLIDER IS GONE, which is a deviation from the literal request worth stating: it only ever applied to the old Apple-flavoured-rounded-rect path, so once Apple corners became a full override with a fixed exponent it would have done nothing in EITHER state — a control that never moves anything is worse than no control. Existing instances may still carry a stored smoothing value; it is ignored, verified as a 0-byte difference. Say the word and it comes back as a sub-control of the tick box. Verified: a project saved before any of this (no style key, so the fallback is off) renders 0 differing bytes against the plain rounded rectangle; Apple ON differs from plain by 6,332 bytes; and the override is real rather than cosmetic — Radius at 5 versus 300 with the tick on produces a 0-byte difference, as does the stale smoothing value. The tick box and the dimming were checked in the actual inspector at 375px in both states, and Mirror was re-checked to confirm effects using a genuine two-option segment still render one.
- v4.05 — The layers wouldn't scroll on a phone, the splash showed a play button, and Home's ⋯ Export did the wrong thing. THE LAYERS NOT SCROLLING was a layout bug with a second bug hiding behind it (Ezra: "on mobile I can't scroll down in the project through the layers"). On the phone the timeline panel is a 1fr grid row, and a grid TRACK has an implicit minimum of auto — so it refuses to shrink below its content. With enough layers the panel grew past #app, which is height:100vh with overflow:hidden, and the rows below the fold were simply clipped off: #timeline's own overflow:auto had nothing left to scroll because the element itself was taller than the screen. Measured before the fix at 34 layers: #app 812px, panel 1517px, timeline scrollHeight 1450 equal to clientHeight 1450, so a scrollable range of exactly ZERO. min-height:0 on both the panel and the timeline lets the track shrink to the space available; the same project now reports a 1,082px scrollable range. THE SECOND BUG underneath it: even once scrollable, dragging on a layer NAME still did nothing. #timeline sets touch-action:none so JS owns every gesture, and the scroller's pan handler deliberately skips .track-head — so a drag there hit the 380ms hold timer, cancelled it on movement, and then no handler picked the gesture up at all. On a phone the header column is most of what your thumb can reach, so that alone reads as "the layers don't scroll". Headers now pan the list, committing only once the drag is clearly vertical so a sideways smudge can't hijack it, and swallowing the click afterwards so a pan never also selects the layer it started on. Verified with real pointer events: drag up pans 0→120px, without selecting; a sideways smudge doesn't move it; a plain tap still selects. THE SPLASH PLAY BUTTON (Ezra: "a play button appearing on the loading animation… and the video never loads"): iOS draws its own play control over any video that has a poster and isn't playing, and splash.mp4 is 2.8MB, so on a phone that state can last the whole splash. The poster attribute is gone from the video entirely — the still is a separate img underneath, the video sits at opacity 0 with pointer-events:none until its `playing` event fires, and if it never plays you simply see the logo until the splash times out. HOME'S ⋯ EXPORT fired FM.storage.exportFile() the instant you tapped it, silently downloading a .fmotion.json: no options, and not what Export means to anyone. It is now "Export video…" and opens the same dialog the editor's Export button does (format, resolution, frame rate, quality) — rendering video needs the scene and its media actually loaded, so it opens the project and leaves you there. The project file keeps its own entry as "Save project file…". Also removed "Save frame (PNG)" from Home's top-bar ⋯ (Ezra: "doesn't belong there") — on Home no project is open, so there is no frame to save; it stays in the editor. ONE THING I COULD NOT REPRODUCE: the + button. Measured at 375px in both themes it is exactly centred — 0.00px off the viewport centre, and the glyph 0.00/0.00 inside its own circle.
- v4.06 — Keyframes need a deliberate hold before they drag, and the edit menu's blank corner is gone. HOLD TO DRAG (Ezra: "dragging key frames only works if you hold on it for a second, and when you're dragging it it should change colour"). A keyframe used to retime from the very first pixel of movement, which made it far too easy to nudge one while scrubbing past. It now takes a 600ms press to arm, the diamond turns orange with a glow the instant it does, and it tracks your finger from there; moving BEFORE it arms abandons the gesture entirely rather than starting a quiet retime. There was a collision to resolve first, and it would have made this silently useless on a phone: a 450ms touch timer already existed that opened the easing menu, and it nulled the drag state — so it would have fired 150ms before any 600ms arm timer and the hold-to-drag could never have armed on touch at all. Rather than have two competing holds, they now share one: hold to arm, drag to retime, or let go without moving and that same hold opens the easing menu. One gesture, both outcomes, and touch keeps its only route into that menu. A plain tap now also costs nothing — it used to rebuild the whole timeline and push an empty undo entry every time. Verified with real touch events: dragging without holding leaves the diamond at 174px, holding 700ms adds the .kf-dragging class and the same drag then moves it to 284px, and the colour clears on release. THE EDIT MENU'S BLANK SPACE (Ezra, with a screenshot: "having that blank space looks shit"): the phone's category grid ran two different rhythms — the top row 3 across, the bottom 4 — so nine cards laid out 3 / 4 / 2 and left a hole in the bottom-right corner. Both rows are 3 across now, so nine cards fill a clean 3×3 with nothing left over. Worth recording because the first attempt made it worse: applying the desktop row-fill classes on the phone pushed Effects onto a row of its own and opened a BIGGER gap, because those classes are computed excluding the Effects card — on a wide screen Effects is deliberately a full-width band, on a phone it is just another square. The phone now has its own fill classes computed over every card, so the last row stays full for layer types that offer eight cards or seven rather than nine. Measured after: three rows of three, 0px gap in every row.
- v4.07 — Pinch resizes the layer, snapping swaps sides, the trackpad gets fine, and Size finally has two sliders. PINCH ON THE CANVAS now scales the SELECTED LAYER instead of zooming the view (Ezra: "dragging or pinching on the canvas won't affect the size or position of the canvas but the layer you have selected, currently you can't pinch to change a layers size"). With nothing selected — or with the camera selected, which owns the view by design — a pinch still zooms the view exactly as before. A group pinches about its visible bounds centre so its members don't fly off-frame, using the same pivot the corner handle already builds, and a layer pinch commits to history like any other transform edit while a view zoom stays runtime-only. Measured: layer selected, pinch out → scale 1.000 to 3.000 with the view untouched at 1.000; nothing selected, the same gesture → view 1.000 to 3.000. SNAPPING HAS SWAPPED SIDES, which is the fix Ezra actually asked for: "the canvas has snapping when you touch to drag stuff, while the touch pad thing to move stuff does not, it should be the other way around". A finger on the canvas is the coarse gesture and should go exactly where you put it, so it no longer snaps at all; the Move & Transform trackpad is the precision control and now snaps to frame centre, the frame edges, AND — the new target — the positions this layer sits at on its own earlier keyframes, so you can put something back exactly where it was. Measured: aiming the trackpad 4px off centre lands exactly on centre; a canvas drag samples 590, 640, 689, 739, 789 with no plateau anywhere near centre. THE TRACKPAD IS TWICE AS FINE — 1.69 project px per finger px instead of 3.60 (it was width/300, now width/640) — and the size controls are finer still, since size was the specific complaint ("some of them like size and stuff expand too quickly"): the width/height boxes go from 1.0 to 0.35 units per pixel and the scale strip from 0.01 to 0.004, about 2.5x. SIZE NOW HAS TWO SLIDERS when you unlink the aspect ratio (Ezra: "in alight motion it opens up a second slider below the first one and the two sliders will separately effect the width and height"). Before, unlinking only changed what the two number boxes wrote — the single slider underneath still moved both axes together, which is exactly the "confusing and janky" part. Verified: linked shows 1 slider, unlinked shows 2, dragging the first moves scaleX to 1.280 while scaleY stays 1.000, and dragging the second moves scaleY to 0.720 while scaleX stays put.
- v4.08 — The Move button now doubles as the anchor-point placer (Ezra: "if you press on the move button for the move section of move and transform… it switches to a different function where you can decide where the centre of that layer is, so when you make stuff bigger it will expand from that point"). Press Move while you are already in Move and the panel switches to the anchor placer; press it again and you are back. The mode rail stays four buttons wide — the anchor deliberately isn't a fifth icon, it lives behind the button whose job is closest to it, one tap from where you already are, and Move stays lit while you are in there so you can see where you went. The anchor is the point a layer scales and rotates AROUND, stored 0–1 across the layer's own box, so dragging it to the top-left makes the layer grow down-right from there instead of outward in every direction. THE PART THAT MATTERS: moving an anchor on its own makes the layer JUMP, because x/y position a layer BY its anchor — so every write compensates x and y by exactly the visual distance the anchor travelled. The layer stays precisely where it looks like it is and only its pivot moves, which is the difference between a usable control and a confusing one. Verified: from centre, dragging the anchor to 25% moved anchorX to 0.25 and x from 540 to 440 — exactly the compensation needed to hold the layer still. The placer snaps to 0, 25, 50, 75 and 100% on both axes so the nine useful positions (corners, edge midpoints, centre) are easy to hit, there's a "Centre the anchor" button to undo any experimenting, and the ◆ keyframe button works on the anchor like any other transform channel, so it can be animated. One thing this needed that is easy to miss: the new mode had to be registered in the transform property tables as well as the panel, or the keyframe rail throws the moment you enter it — which it did, on the first run.
- v4.09 — Every property owns its own keyframes now, and the ones you aren't editing get out of the way (Ezra: "every slider and everything in the system should have its own dedicated key frame… they should still show up on the layer, but be like at 30% opacity"). Keyframes used to be MERGED: every property with a keyframe at 1.2s shared one diamond, and dragging it retimed all of them together — position, rotation and a blur radius all moving as one because they happened to land on the same frame. Each animated property now draws its own diamond, and dragging one moves only that property's keyframe. The diamonds for whatever you are editing right now are full opacity and draggable; every other keyframe on the layer still shows at 30% and is completely inert, so you can see the shape of the animation without grabbing the wrong thing. FOCUS FOLLOWS THE PANEL, and the timeline PULLS it rather than the inspector pushing — a push would need a timeline rebuild on every panel change and could loop. Open an effect and that effect's params are the live ones; sit in Move & Transform and the current mode's channels are live (Move owns x/y/z, Rotate owns the rotations, Scale owns the scales); Blending, Volume and Speed each focus their own property; and the home panel focuses nothing, so everything stays live exactly as before. Diamonds are also coloured by their OWN keyframe's easing now, where they used to take the easing of whichever property happened to be listed first at that time — arbitrary once several shared one. Verified with three properties keyframed at identical times: 6 diamonds drawn where the old merged behaviour drew 2, 4 of them dimmed at 0.3 opacity with pointer-events none, 2 live; switching Move & Transform to Rotate moved the live pair to the rotation keyframes; opening the Blur editor moved it to blur's; dragging a live diamond retimed x from 0.5s to 1.57s while rotation and blur stayed at 0.5s; and a dimmed diamond ignored a full hold-and-drag entirely.
- v4.10 — Square and Squircle now open the Shape tab as a matched pair, and nine bugs are out (Ezra: "make the squircle and default square actually square and show up right next to each other at the start of the shapes section"). THE PAIR: the first two shapes are the same square, sharp corners vs Apple's — both spawn a TRUE 1:1 box, so the only thing that changes between them is the corner. Rectangle is still there, right after, with its landscape proportions; Squircle moved up out of the library page (its natural aspect was 1.35:1, which spawned the app-icon shape as a squashed rounded rectangle) and its menu icon is generated from the real superellipse geometry, stroked so it matches the hand-drawn icons it now sits beside instead of reading as a filled mistake. THE BUGS, in order of how much they cost you: (1) COPY BACKGROUND was misaligned on any supersampled preview — a retina screen alone was enough, no zoom needed — because its compositing plate was sized to the PROJECT while the backdrop snapshot it pastes was captured at the TARGET's pixel size; export, which renders at exactly 1:1, looked correct, so the preview and the file disagreed. Measured against the previous commit: identical at 1:1 (0.000% differing pixels — export and thumbnails are untouched), 7.151% wrong at 2x before, 0.070% after (that residue is downscale antialiasing). (2) The zoomed preview FLIP-FLOPPED between sharp-cropped and full-comp on every other resize: cropping lifts the canvas out of normal flow, #canvas-wrap is sized by that canvas, so the wrap collapsed to zero height, the next measurement bailed out of cropping, the wrap re-inflated, and round it went — and while the wrap was collapsed the selection box and handles, which are positioned against it, collapsed with it. The wrap now holds its box open. Six consecutive resizes at 3x: crop stable, wrap 227x403 every time, where it previously alternated 403 / 0 / 403 / 0. (3) The rule-of-thirds guides and the ONION SKIN ghosts were drawn in project coordinates onto the preview's raw backing store — renderScene puts its transform back when it finishes, so anything painted afterwards landed at the wrong scale and offset the moment the preview was supersampled or cropped. Verified pixel-by-pixel on a cropped canvas: all four vertical guides and the horizontal third now land exactly where the project-to-preview mapping says (453, 907, 68, 1292 px), where the old code put the first one at 360. (4) Deleting a keyframe deleted every OTHER property's keyframe sitting at the same time — correct when diamonds were merged, wrong since v4.09 gave each property its own. Double-clicking one diamond now removes one keyframe: verified with x, scale and rotation all keyed at 0s, only x lost its. (5) Same for EASING — setting Ease Out on a diamond re-eased every property at that time; now x becomes easeOut and scale stays linear. (6) Hold-to-drag barely worked: the abort test measured how far the press landed from the diamond's CENTRE, and since a diamond carries a deliberate ~35px touch pad around an 11px shape, most legitimate presses were already over the threshold and aborted on the first speck of drift — no arm, no colour, no easing menu. It measures finger TRAVEL now: a 6px off-centre press with drift arms correctly, a real scrub past the diamond still aborts and leaves the keyframe times untouched. (7) The easing button in Anchor mode re-eased position, rotation and scale — the graph editor had no anchor entry and quietly fell back to "all". The anchor keyframes nothing (the compositor reads it as a raw number), so the diamond and the curve button simply aren't drawn there any more; Move and Scale keep both. (8) On the home screen, a paint-select drag released anywhere but the card it started on left that card's click-swallowing flag set, so your next genuine tap on it did nothing. (9) Two more in the same gesture: entering select mode by HOLD never armed the edge auto-scroll, so a long run was unreachable; and releasing over a gap, the select bar, or outside the grid never ended the gesture at all — the auto-scroll kept spinning and the list scrolled by itself.
- v4.11 — The Masks section in the Effects panel stops explaining where to find itself (Ezra: "get rid of the masks section in the effects menu that is also describing where to find it"). On a layer with no masks, the Effects panel drew a "Masks" heading whose entire content was a sentence telling you to add one from + Add Effect, under Matte / Mask / Key — a permanent block of instructions pointing at the button directly above it. Both are gone. The block now appears only once the layer actually HAS a mask, which is when it has something to do: the list, each mask's eye / path-keyframe / delete, Mode, Feather, Opacity, Invert and Edit path. Delete the last mask and it disappears again, exactly as an empty layer.masks is already deleted so the project stays diff-free. The way in is unchanged — + Add Effect → Matte / Mask / Key → Mask (it leads that category, and searching "mask" finds it). Verified at 380px: the Effects panel on a fresh shape reads "+ Add Effect / Copy / Paste / Save preset…" and nothing else, the word "Masks" is absent from the panel, adding a mask brings back one mask item, deleting it removes the block, console clean.
- v4.12 — The keyframe hold is nearly twice as quick (Ezra: "make the hold duration for the keyframes shorter"). It was 600ms, chosen from "hold on it for a second", and in the hand that reads as waiting rather than deciding — you press, nothing happens, you press harder. It is 320ms now. That is still far longer than any accidental contact: a tap releases in ~80–120ms and a timeline scrub has already travelled past the abort threshold within ~100ms, so neither can arm a keyframe by mistake, but a deliberate press arms about as fast as you can notice it did. Both outcomes of the gesture got faster together, since they share the one timer — hold and drag to retime, or hold and let go to open easing/delete (the only route in on a phone, where there is no double-click or right-click). Verified: a 250ms press does not arm, a 200ms press does not arm, 380ms does, and releasing a settled hold without moving opens the 12-item easing menu.
- v4.13 — The four retro-screen effects stop being one look each (round 9 of the depth audit). SCANLINES was welded to every other row at 1px — on a 1080p frame that is a flat darkening, not scanlines, and it could never move. It gains Pitch, Line weight and Roll (px/s), so the lines can be coarse enough to read at full resolution and can crawl the way a real CRT's do. CRT drove three unrelated phenomena from one Amount slider — scanline darkness 0.45, phosphor mask 0.18 and a corner vignette — with the cell welded to a single device pixel, so above SD the mask was invisible. Cell size, Scanlines and Phosphor mask are now separate, so scanlines with no colour fringe (or a phosphor grille with no line structure) are reachable. GLITCH was always the same 14-slice 10 Hz stutter with the RGB tear locked to the displacement: Slices, Re-roll (0 Hz freezes the pattern on one frame, which is how you hold a single tear instead of a permanent stutter) and RGB tear are independent now. DITHER's Bayer cell was one device pixel in full colour, so the chunky 1-bit look was unreachable at any Levels setting — it gains Cell size, a 2x2 / 4x4 / 8x8 pattern choice, and a Mono output that quantises luma instead of three independent colour ramps (three ramps can never land on true black-and-white). ALL FOUR verified against the previous commit with the identity harness: 0 differing bytes at the legacy values, every effect still active (13,824–26,441 differing bytes vs the untouched source), and every new control moves pixels. Roll needed a non-degenerate test — at a 2px pitch an even offset lands back on the same rows, which is arithmetic, not a bug; at a realistic pitch it shifts 13,824 bytes and, unlike the frozen pattern (0 bytes between t=0.5 and t=0.9), it travels. New instances were checked against the vignette trap that this file warns about: makeInstance stamps every schema def into params, so all twelve new defs equal the constants they replaced and a newly added Scanlines/CRT/Glitch/Dither renders identically to the old one (0 differing bytes each).
- v4.14 — Every property card is the same size on PC, and a song gets its own three (Ezra: "on pc make all the icons fit better, if you make the two extra big ones smaller then that should fix it… get rid of the effects menu for audios because none of the effects will do anything, and make sure the audios layout is good as well… make it when you add a song like adding media it stays in the audios section"). THE CARDS: Presets was two columns wide and Effects spanned the full width, which made those two read as a different, more important kind of thing than the seven around them. They exist because the last row of a 3-across grid can come up short and the fix was to stretch the final card to close the gap. The grid centres a short row instead — every card identical, no lopsided corner either way. A shape now lays out as a clean 3x3 (measured: nine cards, all 90px on desktop and all 113px at 380px, three per row). A SONG: an mp3 rides the video path as a layer with a 0x0 picture, so Colour & Fill, Border, Blending, Move & Transform, Presets and Effects were all dead ends on it — there are no pixels for any of them to touch. A song now shows the three things it actually has: Speed, Volume and Audio Effects, in one row. Routing is closed too, not just the cards — a view carried over from the previously selected layer, or a timeline double-click, used to be able to open a visual panel on a song; those now fall back to the card grid. IMPORTED SONGS: the Media tab lists everything you have imported before for one-tap re-adding, and songs were landing in there among the video thumbnails with no artwork to tell them apart. They are filed under Audio now, newest first, with their length on the tile — the Media tab keeps clips and photos. Entries saved before this release are recognised by their shape (not an image, no dimensions), so an existing library re-files itself without a migration. Verified with a real 1-second WAV imported through the normal path: it flags as audio, appears under Audio with a 0:01 badge, is absent from Media, its inspector shows exactly Speed/Volume/Audio Effects on both desktop and 380px, all three panels open, and Effects and Colour fall back home.
- v4.15 — Film Grain works on white text (Ezra: "film grain no work on text"). It was not a text problem — it was a WHITE problem, and default text is white. The grain's response curve is 4·L·(1−L), which peaks at mid-grey and falls to exactly zero at both ends of the tonal scale, and only the dark end had a floor to lift it back ("In shadows", 35%). At pure white the response was therefore zero and the effect was mathematically incapable of changing a single pixel: on a white "HELLO" at 1080p, 184 of the 6,745 solid-white glyph pixels carried any grain, and those were only the ones the antialiasing had already pulled off white. The curve now has the matching "In highlights" floor, defaulting to the same 35% — on the same frame 3,485 of those 6,745 pixels are grained (the rest are cells whose signed noise rounds to zero, which is true of grain anywhere). Old projects are untouched: the FALLBACK for the new control is 0, which is the exact curve as shipped, and the original expression is kept verbatim on that path rather than folded into the new one — adding the term re-associates the multiply and can move the last bit even when it is zero. Measured against the previous commit: 0 differing bytes on a colour ramp AND on a pure-white plate with the old parameters, while a newly added Film Grain (which is where the 35% default lands) grains 9,107 bytes of that same white plate where the old code changed none.
- v4.16 — The phone's layer column gets each layer's own preview (Ezra: "on mobile there's only the eye on the layers far left thing to select, it needs to also have a small image preview thing like what pc has because it's hard to select it when you accidentally press the arrow each time"). The left column was an eye and nothing else, 46px wide, so the only real way to pick a layer was to tap its clip — and a selected clip carries the ‹ › grip caps on its ends, which is what kept getting hit instead. Each row now shows the layer rendered into a 34x21 tile beside the eye, the same picture PC has always had, and tapping it selects that layer. That is a target you can aim at, it is nowhere near the grips, and it tells you which layer you are about to get before you commit — a row of identical green bars does not. The selected layer's tile takes the accent border, so the column reads as state as well as a control. The head column goes 46px to 72px; the timeline re-reads that width for its clip-x, playhead and scrub maths, so nothing drifts. Verified at 380px: both rows 72px wide with a 34x21 tile at the same offset, the two tiles genuinely differ (the star's tile has 95 teal pixels and no bright ones, the white text's has 30 bright and no teal — each is its own layer, not a placeholder), tapping the second row's tile selected the second layer, and PC is untouched at 104px with its original 30x19 borderless thumb.
- v4.17 — The clip buttons swap with the playhead, the timeline zooms out 12x further, Ellipse looks like what it makes, and a selected layer can no longer show an empty panel. CLIP ACTIONS (Ezra, from two Alight Motion screenshots): trim-start, split and trim-end all need the playhead INSIDE the clip — parked outside they were three buttons that could not do anything. Out there they now become the two that can: MOVE slides the whole clip so it starts at the playhead (length, trim and speed untouched, keyframes riding along so the animation stays attached to the picture), and EXTEND stretches the edge NEAREST the playhead out to meet it while the far edge stays put. The icons point the way the clip will actually travel, and the box tells you which is which — closed means the whole thing moves, open on one side means that edge stretches. Extend obeys every clamp the trim grips do: a video only grows as far as it has source in that direction, a ramped speed goes through the integral rather than a raw multiply, and nothing can start before zero. The panel does not rebuild while you scrub, so it watches for the CROSSING and rebuilds only then — twice per clip, not sixty times a second, and never while a slider or curve editor is open. THE SAME SWAP ON A MULTI-SELECT: with the playhead over none of the selected clips, the whole set gets move and extend. Move shifts them as a BLOCK — the earliest clip lands on the playhead and every other keeps its offset, because snapping them all to one start would destroy the timing between them. Extend is per-clip, so clips either side of the playhead grow toward it from their own direction and meet there. Verified: a clip at 2-5s with keyframes at 2.5 and 4 moves to 8-11 with its keyframes at 8.5 and 10; extending its tail to 8 leaves the start at 2 and the keyframes alone; extending its head to 0.5 leaves the end at 5; a 2-second audio source trimmed to its middle second clamps at exactly the source in both directions; two clips at 1-3 and 8-10 with the playhead at 5.5 both reach 5.5 and neither far edge moves. TIMELINE ZOOM: the floor was 0.25x, which showed 20 seconds across the lane, so a two-minute edit could never be seen end to end. It is 0.02x now, about 250 seconds on screen, 12.5x more timeline (Ezra: "you should be able to zoom out of the timeline way more than you can currently"). Nothing gets more expensive out there, because the ruler thins its notches to stay 5px apart and so draws FEWER of them; measured 9 tick elements at the new floor on a five-minute project. The zoom label shows two decimals below 1x, where "0.1x / 0.0x / 0.0x" made every step out look identical. ELLIPSE now shows a circle in the Add menu, because a circle is what tapping it gives you — it has no aspect entry, so it has always spawned 1:1 (360x360, measured). The oval icon was promising a shape the button never made. A LAYER SELECTED WITH NO EDIT OPTIONS: the phone's bottom sheet opened in exactly ONE place, the selectLayer wrapper — but twenty-odd paths set the selection directly and never go near it (adding a shape, paste, duplicate, split, select-all, shift and paint select, group, ungroup, undo, redo, the AI scene builder). Every one of them left a layer selected, its name in the top bar, and no controls on screen.
