(1) WHITE FLASH on launching the home-screen shortcut: that was iOS, not the app — a standalone web app with no matching apple-touch-startup-image gets a blank WHITE launch screen while the page boots, and the media query has to match the device metrics exactly or it falls back to white. Added the 12 modern iPhone sizes, each solid #000 so the launch screen and the splash video's own ground are one continuous black (~1KB each, 92KB total), plus an inline html{background:#000} as the earliest possible paint. (2) THE HOME SCREEN FLASHING through the intro was v4.92's own dissolve: the home was fully built and painted underneath, so the moment the splash began thinning you saw a finished project list ghosting through a logo animation that was still moving — and then every element snapped to opacity 0 to start its entrance. The content is now held back from the first frame (.hm-preintro), so the dissolve reveals only the background blooming in and the cards arrive after. (3) The editor's + is Ezra's faceted glass CROSS render, keyed off its white background by flooding in from the edges rather than a global luminance key (the glass has its own blown-out facets — a global key punches holes in them), unpremultiplied so the cut edge carries no white halo, 4x-upscaled and sharpened before keying so the alpha lands on real gradient instead of JPEG blocks. Editor only, by his instruction — the home Create button keeps the orb, so the two + buttons are deliberately different objects now. Three knock-ons from it being a cross and not a disc: border-radius back to 0 (50% would clip the arm tips), the ambient glow moves from box-shadow to drop-shadow (box-shadow follows the border box, which for a cross is a square with visible corners — and the reason the orb couldn't use a wide drop-shadow, that it floods the + cut-out, doesn't apply to a solid cross), and 64px rather than 58 because a cross covers ~44% of its box against a disc's 79%.
- v4.94 — the box around the glass cross, and seating it properly. The box was the sheen painting UNMASKED: the mask was written as the `mask: url(x) center / contain no-repeat` shorthand, and the size-after-slash form is the fragile part of that shorthand in WebKit — when it fails the whole declaration goes with it, so the highlight covered the full square button. Chrome masked it correctly the entire time, which is exactly why it only ever showed on Ezra's phone, and why the orb never showed it either: border-radius:50% + overflow:hidden clip the orb's sheen to a circle whether the mask works or not, so the same latent bug was invisible there. Fixed three ways: mask LONGHANDS (what's tested is what's used), plus a clip-path traced from the artwork's own alpha as a guarantee that does not involve masks at all — verified to contain 100% of the render with 0 pixels outside while covering 53% of the button box, so the worst case if masking ever fails again is a plus-shaped highlight, never a rectangle. Confirmed by forcibly disabling the mask and screenshotting: a faint plus, no square. Then glow and depth, per Ezra ("so it doesn't look plastered on"): the filter chain is ordered tight-to-wide on purpose — the specular rim FIRST so its hairline comes off the crisp alpha rather than a blurred silhouette, then the cyan light the glass throws, then a tight dark contact shadow right underneath, then a long soft one that lifts it off the timeline. Pressing it pulls the shadows in and dims the glow rather than just darkening, so it reads as moving toward the surface.
- v4.95 — the box, actually fixed. v4.94 changed nothing because it fixed the wrong thing: the sheen was never involved. Diagnosed out of the pixels in Ezra's screenshot instead of guessed at — inside the box the yellow clip fits ~16% cyan plus ~16% black composited over it, which is precisely the glow pass and the two shadow passes of the FAB's own filter, and the interior is a soft blob darkest near the cross bounded by a HARD step exactly on the 64px border box. A soft centre ending on a straight edge is a shadow being CLIPPED, not a square being painted. The cause is CSS paint order: filter is applied BEFORE the element's clip, so `overflow: hidden` on that button slices the glow off dead straight at the border box. WebKit enforces that order and Chrome does not, which is the whole reason this only ever appeared on his phone and never in my checks — I was looking at the one browser that ignores the clip. The orb hid the same bug twice over: its clip is a circle concentric with a circular render, and its wide ambient glow is a box-shadow, which overflow never touches. overflow is now removed from the FAB (it was doing nothing — the sheen is inset:0 and cannot paint outside the button regardless) and declared `visible` explicitly so a later rule can't reintroduce it. Locked in with a regression test, mutation-checked: putting `overflow:hidden` back turns it red with the exact diagnosis, removing it turns it green. Also fixed the runner fetching tests.js from cache — it had been reporting green against a version of the suite that no longer existed on disk, which is how the new test appeared to "pass" before it had ever run. 22/22.
- v4.96 — PC transport: play and the playhead share one centre, view options moves to the far right. The misalignment was a real bug, not a style choice: #tl-centerline is absolutely positioned inside #timeline-panel and was pinned with `left: 50vw`, which measures from the PANEL's left edge rather than the screen's. Identical while the panel spans the viewport — phone, and the classic desktop layout — but in Studio the panel begins after the rail and the inspector, so at 1440px wide the playhead sat at x=1126 while the play button and the timecode pill sat at 923. Now `left: 50%`, and timeline.js's recomputePad() derives its scroll offset from that same panel width instead of window.innerWidth, so the line and the content scrolling under it come off one measurement and cannot drift apart. Measured after: playhead = play = timecode = the panel's centre, in classic (all 720), in Studio (all 923) and on a 380px phone (all 190). Then the row itself, per Ezra ("you can move in all the buttons so they fit, they dont need to keep the same spacing anymore, and you can move the view options button to the far right"): on desktop it becomes a 1fr/auto/1fr grid, so the flanking columns are equal BY CONSTRUCTION and play is dead centre no matter how many buttons sit on either side — which is what makes it safe to lift the view button out of the row entirely and pin it to the panel's right edge. The 780px width cap is dropped there too, so the cluster is sized by its own contents and hugs play instead of stretching. The phone keeps the old edge-to-edge spread, verified unchanged. Locked in with a regression test asserting the RELATIONSHIP (playhead centre == play centre) in both desktop layouts rather than a pixel value, so it can't be satisfied by hard-coding an offset that only fits one; mutation-checked by restoring `left: 50vw`, which fails it with "studio: playhead is 180px off the play button" while classic still passes — exactly the shape of the bug that shipped. 23/23.
- v4.97 — two corrections. (1) CENTRED TO THE SCREEN, not the timeline. Ezra: "i meant i want the play head and button centred to the screen not the timeline." Neither previous position was screen centre: the original `left: 50vw` on #tl-centerline landed at panelLeft + half the viewport (1126px on a 1440 screen, because the line is absolutely positioned inside #timeline-panel so a viewport unit measures from the PANEL's edge), and v4.96's panel-centre landed at 923. timeline.js now measures the panel's left once, publishes it as --tl-panel-left, and derives the content's scroll offset from the same number; CSS subtracts it from 50vw for the line and pads the transport's right edge by it so the grid's centre column lands on 50vw too. One measurement, three consumers, so they cannot drift. The timecode pill follows as well — it is the playhead's readout and was floating 200px from the line it reports. A ResizeObserver on the panel catches a layout switch, which moves the panel sideways without firing a window resize. Measured at 1440: classic and Studio both put playhead = play = timecode = 720 = screen centre, with the view button still hard right at 1426 and the leftmost control at 576, well clear of the inspector band. (2) The FreeMotion wordmark gets a GLOW, not a glint: "i meant like a glow that shines the area around it brighter type thing." The travelling highlight is replaced by a soft radial pool behind the letters on `screen` blend, deliberately unmasked so it spills onto the bar and actually brightens the area, plus a masked bloom on the glyphs themselves so the light visibly comes FROM the wordmark; both breathe on one shared 7s cycle so they read as a single source, with nothing translating. That needed .hm-brand's overflow:hidden removed — a clip on the box the glow lives in cuts it off at the glyphs, which is the same mistake overflow:hidden was making on the + button's glow. Regression test asserts three separate things (the line is always on screen centre; the row never overflows the panel; play IS centred whenever the cluster fits), each mutation-verified to fail on its own. In the course of that a comment I had written about why the width cap exists turned out to be wrong — grid's auto minimum already prevents the overflow I claimed it was preventing — so the comment is corrected rather than left as a plausible-sounding falsehood. 23/23.
- v4.98/v4.99 — the Add-menu consolidation, the car's wheels, and four smaller ones. (1) ADD MENU: Elements is the first tab now, so Shape is no longer what opens by default, and it absorbed the whole top row — Text, Captions, Freehand Drawing and Vector Drawing are MOVED there, not copied (Ezra: "i want them moved not duplicated"), so the rail is gone and the panel got a row of its height back. The Elements search field is gone with it: it now holds nine fixed entries all visible at once, so it was searching a list you could already see whole — the real search lives in the Custom elements browser (renamed from "Browse elements") over the pile that needs one. "Save selection as element…" is gone too, and for a reason worth recording: it acts on a SELECTION, and this menu only ever appears when nothing is selected, so the one state it needed was the one state it could never be in. The panel grid went 4 columns to 5 with trimmed cards, because nine entries at four columns was three rows and Custom elements fell off the bottom. (2) THE CAR'S WHEELS, which Ezra was right to hate: the arch and the tyre were not concentric and the tyre was too small for its arch, so each wheel read as a thin dark eyebrow floating above a small dark dot. Both wheels are rebuilt around one circle — arch R=0.184, tyre R=0.154 (a uniform 0.030 gap ring all the way round), hub R=0.0625, centres at y=0.845 so the tyre bottoms land on the baseline at 0.999. The traced arch notches in the outer path were replaced with arcs concentric to their wheels, and the tyre/hub rings were re-radiused by reusing their existing angular sequences — which preserves the winding by construction, rather than by me reasoning about clockwise-ness under nonzero fill and getting it backwards. (3) The Studio export button's icon was jammed against the left edge of its own button: the label is hidden with font-size:0 rather than removed, so the dead text node still held a flex slot and the default flex-start pushed the icon aside — measured 13.5px off centre, now 0. (4) A Settings cog on the PC editor: FM.settings was reachable only from the home screen on desktop, including the Classic/Studio toggle itself, which you had to leave your project to reach. (5) "Save frame (PNG)" moved out of the ⋯ menu into Export ▸ Format ▸ "This frame (PNG)" — it IS an export, just of one frame — and out of the mobile ⋯ too, so it lives in exactly one place. First of the ⋯ menu's entries to find a real home. (6) A hidden layer's clip is now visibly dimmed on the timeline with a dashed rim and an italic name; before, the only tell was the struck-through eye in the track head, which is not where you look when you are wondering why nothing is rendering.
- v5.00 — the + button regraded, from a 9-agent judged pass. Ezra: "the plus button on mobile that we just added still feels flat… could be just that it doesnt mark with the rest of the themes but idk." He was measurably right on both counts: the cross sat at saturation 0.149 and hue 214° (blue-violet) against its sibling orb's 0.467 / 191° and the app accent's 197°, with a luminance spread of 0.479 to the orb's 0.607 — a third of the colour and noticeably less internal contrast than the button it sits next to. Five independent treatments (hue-alignment, local contrast, relighting, chromatic dispersion, bright-rim/dark-core), each rendered at the size it actually ships at and on both a dark panel and a bright clip; three judges with different biases (small-size legibility, material honesty, does-it-belong-in-this-app) scored them and the consensus winner was grafted with the runner-up's directional key light, which was the one flaw all three named. Shipped: saturation 0.488, hue 196° (accent is 197), spread 0.678. Alpha channel byte-identical to the source, so the silhouette, the clip-path guarantee and the sheen mask all still line up.
- v5.01 — the two clip buttons moved to the playhead. Ezra drew an arrow from the inspector's "move clip to playhead" / "extend clip to playhead" pair down to two marks flanking the centre line: "i feel its close the where you will be dragging so itll be easy to click, one on each side." They now straddle #tl-centerline in the ruler band, so the GAP between them is the playhead — they frame the line rather than sitting near it. Positioned off the same `calc(50vw - var(--tl-panel-left))` expression the line itself uses, so they track it in every layout without their own offset maths. They are contextual, not static: shown only while a clip is selected AND the playhead is off it (FM.clipPlayheadSide), which is the same condition that made them appear in the inspector, and each one's icon and tooltip flip with the side so they always point the way the clip will actually travel. Desktop only — the phone keeps them in the inspector, because a floating pair on a 375px timeline would sit on top of the clips, and its layout is off-limits. One home per platform, never two at once. Verified by clicking them: extend took a 0-2s clip to 0-4s with the playhead at 4, and move slid a 0-2s clip to 2.5-4.5s with the playhead at 4.5 — end meets the playhead, length untouched.
- v5.02 — hold the jump buttons for playback speed. Ezra: "if you hold those down, it'll change the play speed… when you hold down one of the buttons, it switches it to a different button that is, like, a plus indicator… and you can spam tap it to make the speed go up. And then once you hold on it again, it switches it back." So it is a MODE, not hold-to-repeat: a 480ms hold on either jump button flips BOTH — left to −, right to + — and they stay flipped until you hold again. Both flip together rather than only the one you held, because a + you can tap ten times with no − beside it is a trap. The ladder is 0.1 · 0.25 · 0.5 · 0.75 · 1 · 1.25 · 1.5 · 2 · 3 · 4 · 6 · 8, deliberately finer than the ⋯ menu's 0.25/0.5/1/2/4 — that list was built for a menu where each pick costs a trip through two levels, so big jumps made sense; here one tap is one step and doubling overshoots immediately. A rate chip appears beside the timecode, shown whenever playback isn't at 1× (not only in speed mode), because a project silently left at 0.5× reads as a slow render rather than a setting. The skip glyphs are captured from the DOM at init rather than hardcoded here: they are a line+polygon pair in index.html, and a hand-copied duplicate would have silently stopped matching the moment either was touched. Verified end to end by driving the real pointer events: a plain tap still jumps and does not touch the rate, a hold morphs both and shows the chip, three + taps went 1 → 1.25 → 1.5 → 2, two − taps came back to 1.25, a second hold restored both buttons with byte-identical icon markup.
- v5.03 — the view-options popup gains the transport extras. Ezra: "add the playback speed buttons in the menu that pops up when you press on the view options button, along side loop playback, mark export start and mark export end, clear export marks, and zoom timeline in buttons - the zoom timeline in buttons should be an arrow point up and an arrow pointing down - if you hold them in they max zoom or max zoom out." A second group under a divider: a −/1×/+ rate stepper on the same ladder the transport's speed mode uses (so the two controls can never disagree), a loop toggle that lights when armed, mark-in / mark-out / clear for the export region, and the two zoom arrows. Timeline zoom is hold-to-JUMP rather than hold-to-repeat: the range is 0.02–12, and a repeat fast enough to cross that is far too fast to stop anywhere useful — Ezra asked for "max zoom or max zoom out", not "keep going while I hold". The arrows dim at the ends of the range and Clear dims with no marks set; dimmed rather than disabled, because a truly disabled button gives no feedback at all when you press it. Both new stepper groups stack vertically like the canvas-zoom group above them — laid out as rows first, which pushed "− 1.25× +" straight out of the bar's left edge. Everything still ALSO lives in the ⋯ menu: Ezra asked to empty that menu gradually, so nothing has been deleted from it yet. Verified by clicking every control: rate 1 → 1.5 → 1.25 with the label tracking, loop toggling and lighting, marks set at 1s and 3s then cleared, a zoom step 1 → 1.35, and a hold on the down arrow landing exactly on the 0.02 minimum with the arrow dimming there. No console errors.
- v5.04 — Select works on the Templates and Elements tabs. Ezra: "Need the select option in templates and elements sections." It was projects-only, and every part of it was hardwired to FM.projects — which was safe only because Select could not be entered anywhere else. Four things had to move together or Select would have looked broken (or been dangerous): the guard that killed select mode on a tab change is gone, but the SELECTION is cleared on a tab hop while the MODE stays on, because ids only mean anything within their own list; templateCard and elementCard route through one shared selectify() helper so they behave identically to projectCard rather than approximately (tick, outline, click-to-toggle, and ⋯ suppressed — the check owns that corner and two overlapping controls in one corner on a phone is a coin flip about which you hit); the select bar resolves per tab, with the right store, the right noun in the confirm, and Duplicate simply absent on templates and elements because neither store has one and a button that throws is worse than a button that isn't there; and shownIds is filled on all six render paths. That last one was the trap: Select-all used to fall back to FM.projects.list() when shownIds was empty, so opening Select on the Templates tab without filling it would have ticked the PROJECT list and handed those ids to Delete. The fallback is gone — an empty shownIds now means the grid is genuinely empty, and selecting all of nothing is nothing. Verified against real data: Select-all on Templates counted 3 templates rather than the 5 projects, the confirm read "Delete 3 templates", and afterwards templates were 0 with all 5 projects and every element untouched; ticking two of three elements deleted exactly those two. Regression test added and mutation-checked both ways. Also fixed a real crash found while testing: FM.settings.open() schedules a rAF that touches `scrim`, which close() nulls — an open/close in the same frame threw "Cannot read properties of null". Pre-existing, but the new PC settings cog made it reachable back-to-back.
- v5.05 — shape icons back to full size. My own regression from v4.98: trimming the Elements grid's cards was written as `.addmenu--panel .addmenu-page .addmenu-card .addmenu-ic svg`, four class selectors, which outranks the shape grid's own two-class `.addmenu-card--ico` rule — so it also shrank every shape tile from 34px to 19px. Ezra: "all of the shape icons have gone small." Scoped with `:not(.addmenu-card--ico)` so the trim only ever touches the labelled cards it was written for. Measured after: shape tab back to 34px icons in 6 columns, Elements still 19px in 5. A pure-CSS specificity accident with no JS involved — the kind no behavioural test would ever notice — so it is now covered by a test that measures both card kinds and asserts the shape icon is the bigger of the two; mutation-checked by re-introducing the exact offending rule, which fails it with "shape tile icons are 19px".
- v5.06 — the project-bricking group cycle is closed. BUG-HUNT.md's one CRITICAL finding, and the only one that could destroy a user's work with no route back: "Select All → Group Selection" while editing a group swept the OPEN group into its own new child, so G.parent === G2 while G2.parent === G. FM.groupBounds was the single parent walk in the codebase without a cycle guard (every other one carries `seen` or a hop cap), so it recursed until the stack blew — and because the cycle lived in FM.scene.layers, the autosave persisted it. After a reload FM.storage.load() threw before it could hand control to the home screen: the project could not be opened, closed OR deleted, and it took every other project down with it. Reproduced first, in the running app, against a detached scene with every persistence path stubbed so the repro could never reach real data — two groups pointing at each other, groupSelection and groupBounds both throwing RangeError. Three guards, because the trigger is a symptom rather than the disease: (1) FM.selectAll is now scoped to the open group's subtree, which is what it always should have been — selecting layers you cannot see also meant Ctrl+A then Delete inside a group deleted the whole document; (2) FM.groupSelection refuses any member that is an ancestor of the group being made, so any FUTURE path that offers one is the same non-event; (3) FM.groupBounds and FM.groupDescendants get the `seen` guard the rest of the codebase already had, so a cycle from anywhere degrades instead of killing the app. Fix (2) also required the member re-parent test to look at the MEMBER set rather than the raw selection — a layer can now be selected and still be refused, and its children were checking the wrong list, which left the new group empty. Mutation-tested one guard at a time: each of the three, reverted alone, turns the new test red with a different message. That exercise also caught a bug in the test itself — a stale groupContext carried between cases meant case 2 was silently asserting nothing.
- v5.07 — editor keyboard shortcuts can no longer reach the project hidden under a full-screen overlay. BUG-HUNT.md, high severity, and the second data-loss route in the list: the global keydown handler bailed out for modifier combos and for editable targets, and for nothing else. The home / project browser is a fixed, full-screen overlay, but opening it only PAUSES playback — the last project stays loaded in FM.scene with its selection intact. So every bare-key editor shortcut still landed on a document the user could not see. Backspace is the one that hurts: it is the habitual "go back" key, it is exactly where focus sits after the back button, and it ran deleteSelected() → commit() → autosave(), so the layer was gone next time they opened that project with nothing on screen to explain it. Space was quieter but as odd — playback starting behind an opaque overlay, audio arriving from nowhere. The same hole covered the effects browsers, the export dialog, the canvas dialog and Settings, whose buttons are not INPUT/SELECT/TEXTAREA and so never counted as "editable". One guard, placed above the modifier chain so ⌘Z/⌘D/⌘V are covered too, with Escape deliberately still allowed through because that is how several of these overlays close. Tested per branch — home reported open, a dialog open, and everything closed — because the app boots with home up, which would otherwise have masked the control leg and made a broken test look green; home's isOpen is stubbed rather than really toggled, since home.open() rebuilds the list and can write a thumbnail. Mutation-checked: dropping the guard fails it on the home leg.
- v5.08 — the filmstrip bitmap leak is closed. BUG-HUNT.md, high severity. FM.buildClipStrip decodes 8 ImageBitmaps per video clip for the timeline bar and caches them on the media record; the matching releaser FM.clearClipStrip existed and had ZERO call sites — a grep over the whole repo returned only its own definition. Every teardown path released the neighbouring frame cache and skipped this one, and deleteLayer/deleteSelected deliberately KEEP the media record alive so undo can restore the clip, which means the bitmaps stayed reachable rather than merely uncollected: pinned by a live reference, no GC could take them. Two halves to the fix. First, the releaser is now called everywhere clearFrameCache already was — project reset, deleteSelected, deleteLayer, replaceMediaWith. Second, the decode is capped: every consumer of stripFrames draws into a 32px-tall canvas, so a full-resolution decode was paying for ~99.9% of pixels it then threw away — 8 frames of 1080p is ~66MB of native surface per clip, ~265MB at 4K, and that memory lives outside the JS heap where it applies no GC pressure at all and simply accumulates until iOS jetsams the tab. Now decoded at 64px tall (2x the tile, so the draw is still supersampled), which is well under 1MB per clip and makes the leak harmless even if a future teardown path forgets. One subtlety worth the words: the releaser used to park stripFrames at null, but both the build guard and the timeline's "should I build?" test are `=== undefined` — null means "built, came back empty, never retry". Releasing to null would have made every undone delete come back with a permanently blank clip bar, so it deletes the key instead. Both halves mutation-checked: unwiring deleteLayer fails with "closed 0 of 3", uncapping the decode fails with "decoded at 1920x1080".
- v5.09 — undo now actually reaches the live audio-effect chain. BUG-HUNT.md, high severity, and a quiet one: preview and export silently disagreed. buildAudioFxChain captures each effect instance BY REFERENCE and its applyAt reads inst.params on every frame forever after, but sync() decided whether to rebuild from a signature that is deliberately structure-only — type and enabled, because "param values ride applyAt", which is correct and is what keeps a slider drag from churning the graph. That is only safe while the instance OBJECTS are stable, and history.restore() does `FM.scene.layers = JSON.parse(str).layers`, which replaces every one of them with a fresh object of identical shape. Byte-identical signature, completely different objects: sync early-returned and the chain kept driving itself from the orphaned pre-undo instances. So the undo was inaudible, and worse, from that moment the inspector edited the new object while the chain read the old one — every further drag on that effect did nothing in preview until you added, removed or disabled something, while export (which builds a fresh chain from the live scene) rendered the value you could not hear. The cache key is identity-aware now as well as structure-aware, compared over the whole audioFx array so it never has to re-derive buildAudioFxChain's own filter. Also fixes the sibling case of reordering two effects of the same type, which produces an identical signature in a different order. Tested through a small exposed predicate rather than a real graph — a MediaElementSource needs a playing <video> — and the test pins the behaviour on both sides: an in-place param edit must NOT invalidate (or every slider drag would click), a JSON round-trip of the instances must. Mutation-checked by dropping the identity half.
- v5.10 — an adjustment layer grades the WHOLE frame again at every preview scale. BUG-HUNT.md, high severity, and the most visible thing on the list: applyAdjustment allocated its plate at PROJECT size and never stamped it, so baseT resolved to the identity and the frame snapshot was an unscaled 1:1 blit — but the preview canvas is not project-sized. resizeCanvas() sets canvas.width = P.width * s for the adaptive playback tier, which drops to 0.28 on a phone, so the graded snapshot occupied only the top-left corner of the plate and was then blitted BACK through baseT, shrinking it again: the grade ended up covering rs² of the visible frame. Measured at 100% → 64% → 38% → 13% across the tiers, with the top-left corner always graded and the bottom-right never. It snapped back to correct the instant playback stopped and the tier returned to 1, and export was always right, so preview and export disagreed exactly while you were trying to judge a grade — and the same fault hit the zoomed preview, where __fmRS goes up to 6 with a non-zero crop offset. The plate now lives on the TARGET's pixel grid and carries its __fmRS/__fmOX/__fmOY stamps, which is the rule drawManualBlendLayer and the Copy Background snapshot already followed; the pixel pass, the pixelate down/up-scale and the final blit all work in those device pixels, and the blit is done at identity because the plate is no longer in project space. Two details worth naming: pixelate derives its block COUNT from the visible area in project units (cw / rs) so blocks keep their real size as the tier moves and stay right on a cropped preview, and a pen mask — which makes an adjustment layer a LOCAL grade — is built in project pixels, so it is drawn through baseT onto the plate rather than at 0,0. Mutation-checked, and instructively: reverting the plate SIZE alone did not fail the test, because a project-sized plate blitted at identity still lands correctly. The defect is the blit, and reverting that fails at 39% for tier 0.62 — rs² to the percentage point.
- v5.11 — the Export and Canvas dialogs are reachable on a phone again. BUG-HUNT.md, high severity. The three app modals shared one z-index of 50, written before any of today's phone chrome existed — the inspector sheet is 55, the AI panel 58, toasts 60, the + button 61, the add sheet 63 — and neither #app nor #main creates a stacking context, so the dialog and the sheet were competing in the ROOT stacking context and the sheet won. Reproduced live at 380x720 in the one state that triggers it: multi-select two layers, which is what keeps the phone Export button visible AND holds the inspector sheet open at 52vh. The export card spanned y=163..557 while the opaque sheet covered y=500..720, and elementFromPoint at the centre of both "Export MP4" and "Cancel" came back as the sheet's close handle — a dialog with no reachable buttons, where tapping Export dismissed the sheet instead. Same for Cancel on a running export, and for the canvas-size dialog. Raised to 100, which clears every piece of chrome and still sits under the things that must stay on top (home screen 200, settings scrim 220, shortcuts 230, context menu 300 — and home's own "Export video…" opens the project first, so it never shows this dialog over the home screen). Verified after the change at the same size: both buttons hit-test to themselves, and the canvas dialog's too. The regression test reads the STYLESHEET rather than computed style, because every one of those chrome z-indexes lives inside a phone media query and would compute to auto at the test runner's width — passing against anything. Writing it turned up a second trap worth recording: a CSSStyleRule now carries its own (usually empty) cssRules list for CSS nesting, so the obvious `if (r.cssRules) recurse; else read;` walker skips every plain rule in the sheet.
- v5.12 — the wordmark glow, rebuilt. Ezra, on the v4.97 version: "The glow for free motion text looks shit, work hard on it with multiple agents to make sure it looks good." He was right, and the diagnosis is simple once it is measured: that glow was a radial-gradient in a box inset -18% / -75%, far taller than wide with its corners cut off, so it read as a grey-white rectangular fog with a findable edge — and on top of the glyphs sat a flat rgba(198,240,255,.5) wash on `screen`, which lifts every pixel by the same amount and is exactly how a glass render becomes a pale silhouette. Against the bare artwork it DARKENED the letters' contrast while adding a stain behind them. So five candidates were designed independently against the real home screen — each agent rendered its own work headlessly and iterated on what it saw, rather than reasoning about CSS — and three judges scored them on different lenses: the brief, visual craft, and whether it belongs to this app. They picked three different winners, which is the useful outcome: all three "what should it steal" notes pointed at the same three parts, and the measurements settled it. The letterform-bloom candidate lit the surroundings best (+19.5 just past the word) but measured −20 / −11 / −10 ON the glyph columns: real light, chalky grey letters. The backlit-glass candidate put +35 on the glyphs but its light was gone within 10px. What shipped is the backlit-glass structure (every lit layer is the artwork's own alpha, blurred — not one gradient-in-a-box, which is what guarantees no straight edge can appear), with the ambient pushed out to 30/44px so it reaches like the first one, the on-letter pass pulled back so the wordmark stops being the brightest thing on the bar, and a 0.5px specular edge from the restraint candidate to re-sharpen what the inner bloom softens. Final measurement against the wordmark with no glow at all: glyphs 40.8 → 61.3 at phone width and 55.9 → 92.9 at desktop, the bar immediately beside it 32.6 → 35.0, and 60px out and beyond IDENTICAL to the nine-tenths of a pixel — lit, not fogged, with no spill down the bar and no effect on anything else. Two mechanics the whole thing depends on, both recorded in the CSS: filter is applied BEFORE an element's own mask (so a masked element cannot be blurred in place — each step is a filtered parent over a masked child), and a child's filter composes with its parent's (blur 8 inside blur 3 is 8.5, not 8). Phone radii come down with the type, which is 20px there rather than 24px. One thing the harness taught me: the backdrop's 48s drift must be KILLED for a comparison shot, not paused — paused stops it wherever it happens to be, and two captures of the identical page disagreed by ~10 levels of green across the whole bar, which reads exactly like a candidate dimming the backdrop. I nearly filed that as a compositing bug before bisecting it.
- v5.13 — a single overshooting keyframe can no longer switch off every filter on a layer. BUG-HUNT.md, high severity, and the nastiest kind of bug: it bakes into the export exactly as previewed, so there is nothing to tell you it is a bug rather than your own keyframes. effectFilter concatenated raw evalProp results into a CSS filter list with no domain clamp. blur, brightness, contrast, saturate, grayscale, sepia, invert and drop-shadow's radius all require a NON-NEGATIVE value; one out-of-domain function makes the whole list a parse error, and assigning an invalid string to ctx.filter is silently IGNORED — the context keeps whatever it had, which after save+baseT is 'none'. So one negative frame dropped EVERY filter effect on that layer, not just the one that went negative. Getting there takes a single click: effect params get the same 8-preset easing editor as transforms, and the built-in Overshoot curve peaks at ~1.096, so on a DECREASING keyframe pair it undershoots the target by ~10% of the span — below zero whenever the end keyframe is 0, which is exactly what "fade brightness to 0" and "blur out to sharp" are. Elastic undershoots by 35%. Measured on a white shape: brightness keyframed 1→0 with Overshoot rendered correctly at t=0.6 and then FULL WHITE for every frame from t=0.8 to t=1.9 of a 2s fade. Every non-negative-domain value is clamped at the point it is stringified now, non-finite values fall back to their default, and the colour-grade panel's saturate gets the same treatment since it is a second way in. hue-rotate is deliberately left alone — it is the one function here that legally takes any sign, and the test asserts −90deg survives. Mutation-checked on the brightness clamp.
- v5.14 — a keyframed anchor point can no longer make a layer vanish permanently. BUG-HUNT.md, high severity, and the only remaining item that destroys work with no visible cause. The compositor reads transform.anchorX/anchorY as RAW NUMBERS in eight places, never through evalProp — that is deliberate, and the inspector withholds the ◆ button for the anchor precisely because of it. Nothing enforced the contract, though: ai-ops listed anchorX/anchorY in the keyframeable transform set and ai-manifest actively advertised them to the model, so asking the AI panel to "animate the anchor so it grows from the corner" wrote `transform.anchorX = {kf:[…]}` straight onto the layer. `-sw * {kf:[…]}` is NaN, the whole traced path is NaN, and the layer draws nothing — no exception thrown, nothing on screen to explain it, and the {kf} object serialises into the save, so the layer stays gone across reloads. An imported project could carry the same object. Fixed at both ends: every anchor read in the compositor goes through one guarded helper that falls back to 0.5 for anything non-finite (two of the eight sites already had a typeof guard, and even those did not catch NaN), and the AI op path now refuses to keyframe the anchor while still allowing setProp, with the manifest reworded to match. Mutation-checked — and the mutation run earned its keep for the third time tonight: the first version of the test counted lit pixels by ALPHA, but the scene paints an opaque background, so every pixel had alpha 255 whether the layer drew or not. It reported 76800 of 76800 both ways and could not have failed. It counts the shape's own colour now: 10800 pixels with a plain anchor, 0 with a keyframed one before the fix.
- v5.15 — duplicating, importing or templating a project no longer silently breaks its layer-referencing effects. BUG-HUNT.md, high severity. reIdLayers mints fresh ids and remapped exactly two classes of cross-layer reference — l.parent, and behaviors' targetId/sourceId — but never effects[].params.source, which is the layer id every effect declared `layer: true` writes: Luma Matte, Compound Blur, Match Grade, Displacement Map, Polar Displacement. After the re-id that id pointed at a layer which does not exist in the new scene; the compositor's lookup returns undefined and falls straight through to drawing the layer PLAIN — the full uncut rectangle instead of the matte, no blur map, no grade, and Displacement quietly falls back to displacing by its own luma. No error, no toast, and the dead reference is autosaved into the copy, so the duplicate renders differently from the original and stays that way. One function covers Duplicate project, .fmotion.json import, template use/insert and element insert, so all four were affected. karaokeOf was the same unremapped class and is fixed with it. The two IN-PROJECT paths (duplicate a layer's subtree, and paste) needed the same addition but a different rule, and getting that backwards would have been its own bug: there, a source pointing OUTSIDE the copied set must be left alone, because that layer is still in the scene and both the original and the copy legitimately matte off it — only a reference to a batch-mate follows the copy, and only a reference to something that no longer exists is cleared. reIdLayers is exposed as FM.storage._reIdLayers so the remap can be asserted directly rather than by duplicating a real project into IndexedDB. Mutation-checked. Also made the shape-icon test self-sufficient while I was in there: it required "nothing selected" as unstated setup and failed twice tonight on leftover state, so it now deselects and restores the selection itself.
- v5.16 — the home screen no longer comes back invisible after a refresh. Ezra, live: "im having a glitch where when i exit a project nothing loads, i can still press on the screen and load projects but they just arent visibly there… it happens if i refresh while in a project." My own regression from v4.92. The splash plays ONCE PER SESSION — the boot script bails at `sessionStorage.getItem('fm.splashed')` — and on that early return it never removes #splash's `hidden` class, never wires dismiss(), and never dispatches fm:splash-dismiss. But armIntro asked "does #splash EXIST", not "is a splash actually up". The element is still sitting there on every repeat load, so armIntro took the "wait for it" branch: .hm-preintro went on, which is `opacity: 0` for every child of #home-screen, and it then waited for an event nothing would ever send. The 6s backstop eventually cleared it, so the symptom was a home screen that was invisible but still fully clickable for six seconds — exactly what he described. Refreshing INSIDE a project is what exposes it, because home is not opened at boot, so armIntro runs for the first time on the way back OUT, long after the splash slot has passed. Reproduced first (root=[hm-preintro], .hm-top opacity 0, five cards present and clickable), then fixed by asking the real question: a splash counts as "up" only if the element exists AND is not `hidden` AND is not already mid-`splash-out` (that last one matters too — during the dissolve the event has already fired, so a listener added then waits just as forever). Verified against his exact sequence: open a project, reload, exit → root=[], every opacity 1, cards visible immediately. Mutation-checked. The predicate is its own exposed function so the test drives the REAL condition in each DOM state instead of asserting against a copy of it — the first cut of this test did exactly that and would have passed against the broken code. Worth recording separately: this was ALSO the moment I found that a plain reload can serve a stale index.html from the HTTP cache, so the page kept loading home.js?v=106 while the server had v=107 — one "verified fixed" reading earlier in the session was against the old bundle and had to be thrown out. Cache-bust the document itself when verifying, not just the assets.
- v5.17 — every popup menu toggles now. Ezra: "When you tap on something that opens up the menu like the icon that opens up the select and duplicate menu, tapping on it again should close it, currently it just infinitely reopens." One tap fires two events: the pointerdown lands outside the open menu and closes it, then the click runs the trigger's own handler, which calls show() and opens it straight back up. Fixed once inside FM.contextMenu rather than in each of the dozen triggers, since the next trigger anyone adds would otherwise arrive with the same bug. The whole difficulty is telling "tapped the SAME trigger" (toggle shut) from "tapped a DIFFERENT trigger while a menu was open" (close the old, open the new) — from inside show() those look identical. The answer is to remember which element OPENED the menu that was just dismissed and compare THAT against the element being clicked now, captured by a capture-phase document listener that runs before the trigger's own handler. My first attempt compared the dismisser against the clicker, which are the same element on every tap ever, so a second trigger swallowed its own menu — caught by testing that case explicitly rather than only the happy path. Two more fell out of that: the document listeners were being registered lazily inside ensure(), which first runs DURING the click that opens the first menu, so the very first trigger of a session was never recorded and only toggled from the second menu onwards; and the "did this tap just close a menu" window was 60ms, which the parent picker misses because it builds a thumbnail for every layer before calling show(). Listeners now register at load, the window is 400ms, and a stale opener is cleared whenever a tap lands with nothing showing, so a much later tap on the same button can never toggle shut a menu that was not open. Verified by driving real pointerdown/pointerup/click sequences through five cases; mutation-checked.
- v5.18 — pinch now twists as well as scales. Ezra: "When pinching to zoom you should be able to pitch and twist to change the angle as well." The two-finger gesture on the canvas already resized the selected layer; it now also rotates it by the change in angle of the line between the fingers. The whole difficulty is that the two must not bleed into each other: fingers ALWAYS rotate a few degrees while they pinch, so without a guard every resize would quietly skew the layer. The twist therefore has to travel 7° before it engages at all, and once latched the threshold is subtracted from the delta so the angle starts moving from zero rather than jumping by seven the instant it catches. Finger identity matters too — vpPtrs is a Map and iterates in insertion order, so the two fingers keep the same roles between the gesture's start and every move; swapping them would flip the measured angle by 180° mid-twist. Verified by driving real touch pointer streams both ways: a 40° twist at constant separation produced exactly 33° of rotation with the scale unmoved at 1.000, and a straight spread doubled the scale with rotation at precisely 0. Mutation-checked.
- v5.19 — freehand strokes are drawn curves now, not faceted polylines. Ezra, for the third time: "Free hand draw is still buggy and looks like shit, I've asked Claude to fix twice now." Driving it and LOOKING at the result found it immediately: the stroke was committed as its raw samples, one hard corner at every one of them. The path format has supported "curve through this point" all along — a third element, [u,v,1], which traceShapePath/buildSubPath honour — and nothing was ever setting it; worse, FM.addPathLayer explicitly dropped the third element while normalising, so even a caller that set it would have lost it. Two passes now: Ramer-Douglas-Peucker simplification (a stroke samples every 2.5 device pixels, so a quick arc arrives as a few hundred points whose jitter IS the visible wobble), then every surviving interior point marked smooth, with the two ends left hard so the stroke starts and stops crisply rather than hooking. Epsilon 2.2 project pixels, tuned by eye — 1.1 still left angular kinks and past ~2.5 it starts cutting the corners off deliberate shapes. Worth recording how this was verified, because the first attempt proved nothing: the harness drew a mathematically perfect sine curve, which has nothing to smooth, and the before/after images were pixel-identical. It draws a densely-sampled stroke with hand tremor on top now, and the difference is plain — angular kinks before, flowing curvature after. Still outstanding on this screen and NOT fixed here: drawing mode does not appear to give the canvas any extra room in the Studio layout, which is a separate complaint from the same area.
- v5.20 — adding a camera no longer throws the whole scene's depth out. "Make sure camera actually works properly" was vague, so six agents audited it by driving it; the depth-parallax one came back with this, measured to under half a pixel. Parallax was driven by the camera's ABSOLUTE position rather than its displacement from the frame centre, so every layer with z != 0 picked up a constant offset of centre*(1 - pscale) the instant a camera existed — before anyone touched it. The vanishing point sat at the frame's bottom-right corner (2cx, 2cy) instead of the middle. At the app's default 1080x1920 with a camera at rest, a z=-1000 layer jumped 190px left and 338px up, z=+1000 jumped 111 right and 198 down, and it scaled with FOV — which the inspector's own hint encourages people to open up. What was already RIGHT, and had to stay right: the parallax rate, order and magnitude were exact (near moves more than far, sub-pixel agreement with -pan*F/(F+z)), and the stash is correctly scoped to the layer loop so a no-camera render is untouched. So the fix is one subtraction, not a rewrite: stash the camera's offset from (cx, cy). Verified with the auditor's own harness — rest offsets went from -53…+53 to exactly 0 at every depth, while a 40px pan still parallaxes -53 / -45.5 / -40 / -35.5 / -32 / -27 across z = -120…240, unchanged. The test asserts BOTH halves, because the obvious wrong fix is to drop the parallax altogether and that would pass the first half on its own. Mutation-checked.
- v5.21 — the camera spans the comp, and there is only ever one of it. Two more from the camera audit, both measured by driving the app. FIRST: the camera clip's length was frozen at creation — `duration: P.duration || 5` takes a one-time snapshot — while autoFitDuration keeps growing the comp to the furthest clip end. So the normal order of work (add camera, then add a longer clip) left a camera that simply stopped applying partway through: measured as an 80px jump on a 320px-wide frame plus a 2x size change between two ADJACENT frames, with nothing on screen to explain it. The camera now follows the comp length, and — the other half of the same idea — it no longer counts toward auto-fit, because a camera is not content and should never be the thing holding the timeline open past the end of the real footage. SECOND: the single-camera invariant was enforced in duplicateLayer and nowhere else, so Cmd-C then Cmd-V on the camera produced a second one, and the composite drives the view from the first camera it finds — which need not be the one you are editing. Guarded in paste, and in element insert too, where a saved element carrying a camera could add unlimited extras. Both halves tested.
- v5.22 — dragging a rotated camera moves the scene the way you actually dragged. From the camera audit, and the most confusing of the lot to use: the campan drag divided the finger delta by the camera's zoom but never un-rotated it, while the composite applies ctx.rotate(rot) to the whole scene. So the scene came out off by exactly the camera's angle. Measured live at 1080x1920 with the same 281px rightward drag: rot=0 moved the scene (+281, 0), correct; rot=45 moved it diagonally (+198, +198); rot=90 sent it straight DOWN; rot=180 sent it BACKWARDS. The parented-layer branch a dozen lines below already un-rotates a world-space finger delta into its parent's frame — the camera simply never did the same. Now it does, and all four angles move the scene right by the same 331px for the same rightward drag. Asserted through the composite's own relation rather than by eye: the screen displacement of a scene point is R(rot) applied to the negated camera delta, and that has to come back out as the drag at every angle. Mutation-checked (at 45° the unrotated version gives (69,69) instead of horizontal). Still open from the same audit and NOT fixed here: the wheel-zoom anchor has the same missing un-rotation, and the camera plate is allocated at project size and never stamped with the render-scale, which is why a camera scene ignores the adaptive quality tier and softens when you zoom — the same class of bug as the adjustment-layer plate fixed in v5.10.
- v5.23 — on a phone you can now press-hold ANY clip to move it, selected or not. Ezra: "On mobile you can only drag clips on the timeline if you have them selected, you should be able to drag clips by holding down on them without selecting." The hold-to-move path was gated on `FM.scene.selectedId === layer.id`, which made moving a clip a two-gesture job — tap it, wait for the inspector sheet, then press and hold — when the hold on its own is already unambiguous. The gate is gone; grabbing a clip that was not selected now selects it as part of the grab, so what you are dragging is visibly the thing you grabbed (that rebuild is safe here because the pointer capture lives on the timeline's inner element, which survives a rebuild, not on the clip element, which does not). What the gate was incidentally protecting still holds, and is the half worth testing: a finger that is still TRAVELLING is a scrub, not a grab, so a quick horizontal drag across clips moves the playhead and leaves every clip where it is — the hold only converts once the finger has been still for ~150ms. Verified by driving real touch pointer streams: holding an unselected clip moved it from 1.0s to 1.65s and selected it while the previously-selected clip stayed put, and a quick drag over the same clip left it at exactly its original start. Mutation-checked.
- v5.24 — the home ⋯ menu is gone; its two entries live in the settings cog beside it. Ezra: "Put the options that show up in the three dots that are in the home menu specifically inside the menus settings cog menu." The ⋯ held exactly two things — Import project file… and Shortcuts — and sat immediately next to a settings cog, which is two front doors to the same cupboard with the ⋯ being the emptier one. Both are app-level rather than project-level, so Settings is where they belonged: a new action-row kind (a row whose control performs something instead of storing a preference) carries them, and each closes the panel before acting so you are not left looking at Settings over a file picker. They work identically from Home or from inside a project. With nothing left in it the ⋯ button is deleted outright rather than left as an empty affordance, which also gives the phone's very tight top row back a 38px control — the row is now wordmark · search · Select · cog. Two loose ends tidied while there: the two injected buttons anchored themselves with insertBefore(…, the ⋯), which after the removal was insertBefore(…, null) and happened to do the right thing by accident, so they say appendChild now; and the .hm-more style rule is removed rather than left as dead CSS. Verified by driving it: no .hm-more in the DOM, both rows present in Settings, and clicking Show closes the panel and opens the shortcuts overlay.
- v5.25 — trim and split moved up beside the playhead. Ezra, with an arrow drawn from the inspector's row of three to the ruler band: "move these buttons here too, only when you have a layer selected, and also make sure the delete fully left and delete fully right are both on the left side and then the split is on the right side." Done exactly that way: both trims in the left group, the split alone on the right. It also happens to read correctly — the trims act on the material to either side of the line, the split acts at the line itself. The group hangs off the same `calc(50vw - var(--tl-panel-left))` anchor the centre line and the nudge pair use, so all three agree about where the playhead is in every layout, and it straddles the line the same way (the GAP is the line, not a gutter beside it). The nicest part fell out of the logic rather than being designed: this group appears only while the playhead is INSIDE the selected clip, which is exactly when the existing nudge pair is hidden — trimming or splitting somewhere the clip isn't means nothing — so precisely one of the two groups is ever on screen and they never fight for the same 24px. Desktop only, like the nudge pair; the phone keeps them in the inspector, and on desktop the inspector's copies are hidden so these are MOVED, not duplicated (he has called that out before). Verified by driving it: at t=3 inside a 1→5s clip the trims render at x 660..725 and the split at 751..781 with the nudge pair hidden, at t=8 the pair swaps back, and trim-end at t=3 cut the clip's duration from 4 to 2.
- v5.26 — the three export-mark buttons read as one control. Ezra: "add a dark layer behind these three buttons to signify theyre grouped." Mark-in, mark-out and clear were three loose siblings in the view rail, indistinguishable from the unrelated icons above and below them; they now share a rounded backing with an inset hairline, 42x122 around three 38px buttons. Deliberately DARKER than the bar rather than lighter: every other lit surface in that rail means "armed" — .vb-btn.on and the accent-soft wash both do — so a lighter panel would have read as "marks are set" when they are not. Inset rather than raised, for the same reason. Measured in place rather than eyeballed: the backing is 4px wider than the buttons on each axis and the group holds exactly the three intended children.
- v5.27 — the layers button finally does something. Ezra: "also make the layers button work, the one that weve had for ages that does nothing" — it toasted "coming soon". With one clip selected it now cycles three ways: tap once to hide every other layer, again to bring them all back with this one drawn on TOP of them, again to return to normal. The button lights and its tooltip says which state you are in, and picking a different clip drops it so you can never be left staring at a filtered scene wondering where everything went. The important half is the half he spelled out — "make sure you dont actually make it move on the timeline at all, this shouldnt change anything but just be its own little tool to help you visualise stuff" — so it is a VIEW state (FM.isolate) read by the compositor's layer loop and written nowhere else: no layer.visible, no reordering, no history entry, no autosave. The regression test proves that directly rather than trusting it: after arming every mode, layer order and visibility are byte-identical and the history/autosave counters are still zero. Mode 2 deliberately does nothing for a layer inside a group unit — those members composite as one flattened unit at the group's z-slot, so lifting one member out would change what the group IS rather than just what order it draws in. Verified with a scene where the two shapes only partly overlap, because with one fully covering the other the three modes render identically and the test would have proved nothing: mode 1 removes the other layer entirely, mode 2 keeps it and wins the overlap. Mutation-checked.
- v5.28 — the phone Add sheet's ✕ is gone, and the tiles get the width back. Ezra: "On mobile I want you to get rid of that stupid X button that's there for some reason and takes up heaps of needed space." He is right about the cost: it reserved an 88px rail on a 375px screen — very nearly a quarter of the width — to duplicate something you can already do four other ways. Tapping anywhere outside the sheet closes it (the tap handler's own comment says "so you never have to find the ✕"), the + toggles it, the grab handle closes it, and the sheet swipes down. Removing the button leaves the rail empty, and the `.addmenu-side:empty { display: none }` rule that was added back in v4.98 for exactly this situation collapses it. Measured at 380px: the tile grid went from ~266px to 354px of 380, a third more room, and the tiles are visibly bigger for it. Both close paths re-verified after the change — the + still toggles the sheet shut and a tap outside still dismisses it — because removing the only affordance you can SEE from a control is only safe if the invisible ones genuinely work. The dead .addmenu-close rule and the 88px rail reservation are removed rather than left behind.
- v5.29 — the phone view rail slides. Ezra: "On mobile the view row is kinda ruined, needs to not be crammed in and be slide-able up and down." The rail is pinned to the CANVAS's height and has been carrying far more since v5.03 — fit, grid, layers, camera, canvas zoom, playback rate, loop, the three export marks, timeline zoom. Measured at 380x820 it is a 316px box holding 566px of controls, so 252px of them were unreachable: in his screenshot the mark-out button is sliced in half by the canvas edge and everything past it is simply gone. It scrolls now, with the scrollbar hidden (a visible bar inside a 46px rail is the crammed look being fixed) and overscroll-behavior: contain so a flick that reaches the end does not turn into a page scroll or a scrub on the timeline underneath. Deliberately NOT solved by shrinking the buttons: "not be crammed in" was the other half of the ask, so every control keeps its 38px touch target and you slide to the far ones instead. The test asserts both halves. Fixing this also exposed a flaw in v5.23's test: it selected a layer before grabbing a different one, which only works at desktop width — on a phone, selecting a layer SOLOS its timeline row, so the unselected clip has no row to grab at all. It now runs with nothing selected, which is both the real phone scenario for that feature and width-agnostic; the suite is green at 380 and at 1440.
- v5.30 — the empty tabs no longer look broken. The one finding the screen-polish sweep actually got to verify before its agents hit a session limit, and it is the first thing a new user sees on two of the three home tabs. The empty state was a single line of 13px italic --text-faint pinned to the TOP of the tab body: measured at 380x820, a 26px paragraph followed by 517px of dead background; at 1440x900, one 11px line above a 611px void — 99.1% of the body empty. `grid-column: 1 / -1` was the tell, vestigial from when .hm-grid was a grid; it became a flex column and the block was never re-centred. Now a mark, a bold title and one short line, centred off .hm-scroll — the element that actually has the height — rather than with a min-height calc, which would double-count the top bar, the pill row and the 110px FAB reserve that .hm-scroll's padding already provides, and could put a scrollbar on a page with nothing to scroll. :has() scopes the centring to the empty case so a populated tab is untouched. The italic --text-faint is gone too (#59647a is about 4:1 on this backdrop, and italic body copy at 13px is the worst of both), and the Elements string — five lines of explainer — is trimmed to the sentence that says what an element actually IS, since centring five lines of small italic would not have helped anyone.
- v5.31 — the first of BUG-HUNT.md's largest block, and the measuring stick for the rest of it. The preview canvas is rendered at P.width × s for the adaptive playback tier — as low as 0.28 — so any effect whose parameter is a LENGTH has to multiply by that scale, or it draws the same number of PLATE pixels, which is a far bigger thing in project terms. drawPixelEffect has always passed the scale as a sixth argument, with a comment saying precisely that; only 4 of 67 effects were taking it. Rather than "fix 41 effects" from the hunt's list, I measured all 177 registered effects the same way the hunt did — the fraction of the frame each one changes should not depend on render scale — and ranked them by how far off 1.0 that ratio drifts between 1:1 and 0.36. Stroke was the worst credible offender at 3.89 (3.9% of the frame at 1:1, 15% at 0.36 — the +154% the hunt reported), and it is now 1.52. The residual is not a bug that can be removed: a 4px outline on a 0.36 plate rounds to 1px and integer dilation cannot do better than that. The ranked list of the rest is recorded in the task so the next pass is evidence-led rather than a scan of the source — border (5.14, and notable because it already ACCEPTS the scale argument, so it is taking it and using it wrongly), hextiles 8.45, contourlines 7.84, vhstape 5.55, mattefringe 3.63, tiltshift 2.55, scanlines 2.20, box3d 2.19, rasterextrude 2.09, mosaic 2.02. The regression test measures the ratio rather than the pixels, so it is a template every one of those can reuse; mutation-checked, and it reports the real 3.89 when the scaling is removed.
- v5.32 — three more of the plate-scale family, and a correction to how the family is being measured. mosaic (block size), mattefringe (edge width) and hextiles (tile size) all take their parameter in PROJECT pixels and were applying it in PLATE pixels, so on a reduced-quality preview the blocks, fringe and tiles were all far coarser than the export. Measured with meaningful parameters on a shape with real edge detail: mosaic 2.02 → 1.14, stroke (v5.31) now measures 1.13, mattefringe 1.09. hextiles improved 8.45 → 2.15 but is NOT fixed — something else inside it is still plate-relative, most likely the row offset or grid origin rather than the tile size, and it is left alone rather than half-fixed on a guess. The correction matters more than the three fixes: my first ranking measured every effect at DEFAULT parameters on a flat rectangle, and that put mattefringe at 3.63 and hextiles at 8.45 when each was changing under 1% of the frame — the ratio was almost entirely edge antialiasing, not the bug. Re-probed properly, mattefringe measures 1.09 and always did; my "fix" for it changed nothing real. It also cleared `border`, which the first pass ranked worst of all at 5.14 and which turns out to scale correctly already. So the ranked list in the task is rewritten with the honest method: meaningful parameter values, a star rather than a rectangle, and a coverage floor of 5% before a ratio is allowed to mean anything. The test carries that floor as an assertion, so a future probe that measures nothing fails loudly instead of passing quietly. Mutation-checked on mosaic (2.48 with the scaling removed).
- v5.33 — the car, redrawn and judged. Ezra, third time: "fix the car shape again, but use other agents to hold you accountable coz you keep doing shit." So the accountability was built into the process rather than promised: a harness (tests/_shape.html) that renders any library shape at 520px AND at the 34px add-menu size with a ?poly= override, so five candidates could be drawn at once without anyone touching compositor.js; five designers each rendering and criticising their OWN output over four or more rounds; then three judges scoring the PICTURES on three lenses — silhouette, drawing craft, and icon legibility. The winner took all three at 8/8/8 and the shape it replaces scored 2/2/2 from every judge, which is the part worth reporting: the exercise did not just produce a different car, it confirmed the old one was as bad as he kept saying. One judge diagnosed it better than I had: its ink box was 57x58px, literally square, and a car in profile is a wide shape — so it read as a bubble-van blob with rings printed on top, tyres crossing the floor line, and a V-notch bitten out of the front bumper. The new one is a polyline of named landmarks (rocker, cowl, beltline, tailgate shoulder, arch lip) with a true circular fillet at every corner, so panels stay straight and each curve is a radius someone chose; the points carry MANUAL bezier handles, which FM.pointCtrl already supported, so the arcs are real circles rather than Catmull-Rom approximations. The regression test pins the properties that were actually broken rather than the look: the body must be at least 1.4x wider than tall, at least three sub-paths must wind against the body (or the windows and hubs fill in solid), the two wheels must be the same size and on one centre line, and the tyres must reach below the body's underside while staying inside its width — i.e. be in arches rather than discs on a slab. Known weakness kept on the record: the tyre-to-arch gap is 0.023, sub-pixel below ~60px, so at the 34px icon the arches close up; the icon judge still ranked it first of six.
- v5.34 — camera focus blur is symmetric about the focus plane again. From the camera audit, two of its findings and one cause: the blur radius was divided by the layer's perspective scale. That reads like pre-compensating for the scale applied a moment later, but ctx.filter is applied in DEVICE space and is not touched by the current transform — the same fact that makes plateScale necessary everywhere else in this file — so the division did not cancel anything. It made the blur depend on depth a SECOND time, on top of camDefocus which already measures distance from the focus plane and is perfectly symmetric. The result: anything BEHIND the focus plane was smeared away (the audit measured 13.4px) while anything in FRONT of it barely blurred at all (1.2px), so a rack focus only worked in one direction. Measured here with a rectangle at z = ±260 either side of the plane: 29px vs 64px before, 58px vs 59px after — a ratio of 1.02 — and a layer sitting ON the plane still renders with a ramp of exactly 0, i.e. properly sharp. Verified by putting the old division back and watching the ratio return to 2.21. One thing this cost me first: my initial probe built the camera's focus object by hand as {enabled, d, r, s} and measured NO blur at all, which looked like the fix had broken it. Those are the INTERNAL names cameraLens emits; the layer's own fields are {enabled, distance, dof, blur}, and a focus object missing them silently means "no blur" — which is itself one of the audit's open findings. The test asserts the blur actually engages before it compares the two sides, so that failure mode reports itself instead of passing as a suspiciously perfect 1.0.
- v5.35 — a camera no longer stops blend modes and adjustment layers seeing the background. Two HIGH findings from the camera audit with one cause, and Ezra made the design call that decided the shape of the fix: "the background is usually one solid colour so would you even notice the panning? Keep the background unaffected." So the background stays FIXED on screen, which is why it was being painted on the real canvas rather than into the camera's plate in the first place. The trouble was that painting it only there left the plate transparent where the background should be, so a difference blend had nothing to blend against and an adjustment layer had nothing to grade — both silently stopped working the moment a camera existed, and neither failure said anything. It now goes into the plate as well, INVERSE-MAPPED: the quad filled is the screen rectangle pushed back through the camera's transform (p = cam + Rᵀ·((screen − C)/zoom)), so once the composite applies that transform the fill lands exactly over the frame and does not move when the camera pans. Same colour as the real-canvas fill underneath, so there is nothing new to see — the point is entirely what the layers above can now blend and grade against. Measured all three properties, because fixing any one alone is easy and wrong: the background is byte-identical at rest and after a 70px pan; a difference blend renders 207,159,63 both with and without a camera, where before it stayed pure white with one; and an adjustment layer inverts the background 48,96,192 → 207,159,63 instead of leaving it alone. Mutation-checked. One near-miss worth recording: my first blend probe set `layer.blend`, which is not the field — it is `layer.blendMode` — so the blend never applied and the result looked like the fix had failed. The test now asserts the blend applies WITHOUT a camera before comparing the two, so a dud probe reports itself.
- v5.36 — a camera scene is rendered on the preview's own pixel grid. The last of the camera audit's structural findings, and the same class as the adjustment-layer plate fixed in v5.10: the camera composites through an offscreen that was allocated at exactly P.width x P.height and never stamped with __fmRS/__fmOX/__fmOY. Two consequences, both invisible as bugs and both real. First, plateScale() read an undefined scale on that plate and returned 1, so INSIDE a camera scene the adaptive playback quality tier did nothing at all — the whole point of the tier is to render fewer pixels while the playhead moves, and with a camera present it was still rendering every one of them. Second, a zoomed preview could not supersample: the plate was project-sized and had to be UPSCALED to the canvas, so the picture went soft the moment you zoomed in and stopped matching the export. Measured at a 2x zoomed preview: a hard edge came out as a 2px ramp with a camera against 0px without; now both are 0. The plate is sized to the target's grid and carries its stamps, and the blit hands back at project size so the surrounding baseT and camera transform still map it exactly as before. Capped at 2x, because beyond that a supersampled plate on a 1080x1920 project costs tens of megapixels for detail nobody can see. Verified there is no regression at the reduced tier either — a 14px blur measures the same size in project terms at 1.0 and at 0.36, with and without a camera. Mutation-checked by pinning the plate scale back to 1, which returns the 2px soft edge.
- v5.37 — the OPEN badge on a project card says OPEN over any thumbnail. Ezra: "since the text is just a cutout, sometimes it's hard to read it saying open on certain backgrounds, so make it a colour that stands out." The artwork was built the same way as the + FAB — one luminance key punched the black backdrop out of the render — and that key took the LETTERING with it, so the word was a hole and simply wore whatever project thumbnail sat behind it. On a pale or busy card it stopped reading as a word at all, and even when it was legible it changed colour from card to card. Fixed in the asset rather than in CSS, because a colour behind a cut-out needs the pill's exact silhouette and the pill is not a plain pill (there is a sparkle off the corner). The letters are found by flood-filling the transparent region INWARD from the border: whatever transparency that flood cannot reach is interior, i.e. the lettering, which leaves the pill's own soft outer edge untouched. Each of those 2004 pixels is recomposited as glass OVER ink, so a letter edge at 40% alpha lands 40% of the glass over the ink instead of stair-stepping. Ink is #071319 — near-black, biased to the glass's own teal rather than a flat #000. Black rather than white on measurement, not taste: 90% of the glass immediately around the letters sits below 0.40 luminance and only 1% is specular-bright, so ink beats white by 5.9:1 against 3.5:1 at the median. Checked at the real 33x22 through the real filter chain over white, a pale photo, a busy midtone, black and a hard split — identical word every time. Guarded by a test that reads the shipped PNG and fails if any interior pixel is see-through again, which is exactly how this comes back; mutation-checked against the old art (1173 see-through pixels). Also lifted the note on --glass-edge-sm: it had to stay sub-pixel only because drop-shadow follows alpha and bloomed into the counters of the O and P, and with the letters inked the pill outline is the only alpha edge left.
- v5.37 — fixed a regression test that was crying wolf. The splash/preintro guard asserted "#home-screen is not in .hm-preintro RIGHT NOW", which is a question about time answered with a single snapshot: on a cold session the splash legitimately holds preintro for ~2.4s, so the test read red on every fresh load and green only once the splash had already played that session — it had been quietly red in headless the whole time. Sampled the real thing every 100ms from boot to tell the two apart: preintro at 100ms with the splash up, splash-out at 2300ms, intro at 2400ms, clean by 4400ms — the app was never stuck. The assertion now encodes the invariant the bug actually violated (preintro may only be up WHILE a splash is up) with an 0.8s grace for the handover frame, against the 6s of blank screen that was reported. Mutation-checked by putting the pre-v5.16 element-existence bug back into splashIsUp(), which turns it red immediately.
- v5.38 — the wordmark has a glint instead of an outline. Ezra: "the FreeMotion text's glow makes it hard to read, I didn't want a bright outline I wanted a glint like the sun, not edge glow… tone it down a bit on the edge glow and more focus on that shine." That is a real defect and not a preference, and the cause is structural: every emitter in the lockup traces the ARTWORK'S ALPHA, so all of them are rim light by construction. The rim had ended up brighter than the glass it surrounded, so the eye read the outline rather than the letterform, and at the 20px phone size it was thick enough to start filling the counters. The whole emitter stack comes down to roughly a third of its alpha, and the light it loses goes back on the GLASS rather than around it, as two things. The internal sheen becomes a rake — a radial originating off the top-left corner instead of a top-to-bottom ramp — so the word brightens toward one light source and has a direction to it, where a ramp lifts every letter equally and reads as the same even glow. Then one tight specular catch on the F's top-left corner, which is where the artwork already carries a bright facet, so the highlight has geometry to justify it. Pure white on `screen`, masked by the artwork's alpha so it can only ever land on the glass and can never add to the rim, sized in percent of the box so it tracks the type from 24px down to 20px without a second set of numbers. Measured on the real home screen: halo spill onto the bar past the word +0.0148 → +0.0003 luminance, and the darkest fifth inside the word — its counters and the gaps between letters, which is what fog fills first — 0.0148 → 0.0081, taking gap-to-peak contrast from 33:1 to 49:1. Judged as pictures as well as numbers, five candidates at 1100px and at 380px, and the two that put a big glint ACROSS a letter blew that letter into a white blob: a sun glint has to be a point, which is the thing worth remembering here.
- v5.39 — copy and paste one effect, across projects. Ezra: "in the three dots for each effect, add options to copy effect and paste effect." Copy sits under the effect's ⋯ next to Duplicate; Paste appears only when there is something on the clipboard and NAMES it ("Paste Gaussian Blur") rather than saying a bare "Paste effect" — an effect stack is a column of near-identical rows, and a generic label gives you no way to know what is about to land on it. It pastes directly below the effect whose menu you opened, which is the one place you can point at. The clipboard is localStorage, not a variable, because the actual reason to copy an effect is usually to put it on a layer in a DIFFERENT project, and a page-lifetime clipboard is empty exactly when you get there. Copies carry keyframes for free: an animated parameter IS a channel object sitting in fx.params[key], so the deep clone takes the animation with it — the same reason Duplicate clones instead of building a fresh default instance. Two things the clipboard has to refuse: a stored effect whose type this build no longer knows (older build, renamed effect) reads as an EMPTY clipboard rather than pasting a row that renders nothing and cannot be edited; and the copy runs through jsonReplacer so it cannot carry the runtime _expanded flag, which would otherwise make every pasted effect arrive with its editor already open and shove the stack around. Tested through the real ⋯ menu rather than by calling the clipboard directly — the whole feature is two menu entries, and a working clipboard behind a menu that never offers it is the failure worth catching. Mutation-checked three ways: dropping the jsonReplacer, dropping the registry check, and renaming the menu entry each turn it red. One thing that cost me a run: my first probe animated a param called __probe, and jsonReplacer strips leading-underscore keys by design, so the test reported lost keyframes when nothing was wrong. It now animates the effect's real parameter and asserts FM.isAnimated agrees before trusting the result.
- v5.40 — text wraps at a border you drag. Ezra: "when you edit text, you should be able to drag the border of the text to decide when the text wraps and stops going on to the right."
