Text had no wrapping AT ALL before this: four separate places did String(text).split('\n'), so a long line simply ran off the frame and the only way to break it was to type a newline yourself. All four now come through one FM.textLines(ctx, layer, text) — the draw pass, the alpha/footprint pass and layerSize — so the picture and the selection box cannot disagree about where the lines break, which is the failure that would make the handle you drag stop matching the border you see. layer.wrapWidth is that border in project px; 0 means no wrapping, exactly as before. Two side handles appear on the selection box for text layers only, shaped as BARS rather than the corner squares because they do a different job — the corners scale the layer, and a side handle that stretched the glyphs is what every editor gets shouted at for. The drag projects the finger onto the layer's own x axis (so a rotated layer's column still tracks the finger), divides out the layer's scale so the stored number is in the same project px the renderer measures in, and doubles it, because the text lays out about its centre and one handle moves both borders — which is what keeps the column centred under the anchor instead of walking sideways as you drag. Double-click a handle to turn wrapping off again: a wrap you can only ever tighten is a trap once undo has scrolled past. Three things worth recording. Wrapping runs AFTER applyTextEffects, since Count Up and Randomizer change the string and wrapping the pre-effect text would break the lines somewhere different on every frame. A word wider than the whole column is chopped by character — not typographically nice, but the alternative is a line quietly sticking out past the border that was just dragged, which is the thing being fixed — and that chop has to be reachable for a word that STARTS a line, not only one that lands mid-line, which is the bug the greedy loop had on the first pass. And the line cache holds two slots rather than one, because the draw pass and layerSize call in with different letter-spacing and a single slot would miss on every call and cache nothing. Verified by measuring the drawn ink, not just the helper: 320px wide unwrapped against a 140px column, height up by four lines, and layerSize agreeing with the column to within half a pixel. Mutation-checked three ways — restoring the old split, removing the over-long-word chop, and letting layerSize report the widest line again all turn it red.
- v5.41 — the mobile text editor stops fighting the keyboard. Ezra: "when you're in the text edit screen on mobile and you are typing it is glitchy and doesn't let you edit the other options on screen… and also pushes the screen down." Three separate faults, found by faking a 336px keyboard in the harness — headless Chrome has no software keyboard, so without shrinking the visual viewport by hand every capture shows the easy case and the bug is invisible. FIRST, the selection box detached from the text. Opening the keyboard shrinks the visual viewport, the editor answers by re-padding #stage, and that resizes the canvas — but a padding change fires no window resize, so nothing told the box, and it sat wherever the canvas used to be while the text was somewhere else entirely. That is most of what "glitchy" means here. It now updates from onViewport, SYNCHRONOUSLY rather than on the next frame: update() reads getBoundingClientRect(), which flushes the padding written a line earlier, so it already measures the new layout — and a rAF there silently never runs wherever rAF is throttled, which is exactly what happened the first time and hung the whole test suite instead of failing one test. SECOND, the lift was a guess: gap + a hardcoded 118px, where the dock's real height moves with the safe-area inset and the field's line count. It measures the dock now, and CLAMPS the result so at least 120px of preview always survives — on a short screen (landscape, a small device, a split view) keyboard + dock exceeded the stage outright and the preview collapsed to 0x0, no picture at all while typing, which is worse than a preview partly behind the keyboard. THIRD, the toolbar only guarded `mousedown` against stealing focus. On iOS the blur is already under way by the time a synthetic mousedown arrives, so every tap on Align / Font / Size / Colour dismissed the keyboard and re-flowed the screen — the "doesn't let you edit the other options" half. It guards pointerdown too now, and so do the popovers, with native input/select/textarea excluded because preventing pointerdown on a range input stops the thumb dragging and on a select stops the picker opening. Mutation-checked all three. One part I could NOT verify here and am flagging as such: for "pushes the screen down" I also put the document scroll back to 0 on every visual-viewport event, because iOS scrolls the page to reveal a focused field and body{overflow:hidden} does not stop it — that is the standard fix, but this machine has no iOS to prove it on, so it is reasoned rather than measured.
- v5.42 — keyframes are inert outlines until you open the editor that owns them. Ezra, with Alight Motion screenshots: on first tapping a layer "they're clearly showing you where they are but you can't move them yet or hover over them or anything at all"; then, going into Move & Transform and specifically the position editor, that property's keyframes "become clear and highlighted when you go over them, whilst the others stay as outlines." The rule was already half here — a keyframe belonging to the panel you have open was live and the rest were dimmed — but the DEFAULT was backwards: with no property editor focused, kfFocusProps returns null and null meant "everything is live", so merely selecting a clip armed every diamond on the layer for dragging. That is the state his first screenshot is of, and the one he wants disarmed. One character's worth of logic, and then the states had to actually look like states. Idle is now a true OUTLINE — transparent fill, a 1.5px border in the easing's own colour so the shape of the animation is still legible at a glance — rather than the old 30% opacity solid, which said "not this one" clearly enough but had stopped saying "here is a keyframe" at all. It stays fully inert, touch pad included, so it cannot be hovered, dragged or long-pressed. Live keyframes keep the solid per-easing fill, and the live one the PLAYHEAD is sitting on grows to 15px with a white ring — grown and ringed rather than recoloured, so it still tells you which easing it is. That last state is toggled from updatePlayhead rather than at rebuild time, since the playhead moves without the timeline rebuilding, with half a frame of tolerance because a keyframe's time and FM.time are both floats and an exact compare would flicker. Mutation-checked three ways: putting the old nothing-focused-arms-everything rule back, letting idle diamonds take pointer events again, and dropping the playhead highlight each turn it red.
- v5.43 — Z position editing on the move pad. Ezra, from an AM screenshot: "it's showing what the z position editing looks like and I would like you to add z position editing as well, like how it is in alight motion, you just tap on z and then it switches to this version." Z already existed as a channel — the camera's parallax, focus and fog all read it — but the only way to change it was to scrub the little number, which is a poor control for a value that ranges into the thousands. It is a SUB-MODE of the move pad rather than a fifth button on the mode rail, exactly as AM has it, because Z is still position. The switch is the LABEL under each value, not the value itself: tapping the number already opens the type-in editor and taking that over would have traded one thing for another. The labels became small pills so they are real touch targets, the active one carries the accent, and the values follow — an axis the pad is not editing reads as a plain number, or all three stay accent-coloured and the readouts contradict the chip that says which is live. In Z mode the pad is vertical-only with a chevron above and below, and the sign is chosen so the pad drags the OBJECT rather than the number: pushing DOWN pushes the layer back into the scene (z grows = further away, the same direction the fog and focus maths already read it) so it shrinks under a camera, and pulling UP brings it toward you and it grows. Gain is double the X/Y pad's, since depth ranges much wider than a frame. Two things worth recording. My first pass ended the Z branch with `return center`, which would have skipped the mode rail built below it — it is an `else` now. And the first mutation run reported the "leaving the panel must not strand you in Z mode" assertion as passing when the reset was removed, because the test had already tapped back to X before checking; it taps back to Z first now, and then the mutation turns it red as it should.
- v5.44 — points snap to the other points in the shape editor, and two quick drags stop deleting one. Ezra: "the shape editor point editor thing doesn't have grid snapping to the other points." Snapped in the shape's OWN local units rather than on screen, because lining two points up should mean they share an edge of the geometry, and that has to go on meaning the same thing when the layer is rotated or skewed. One local unit spans the whole shape, so the 7px stickiness is divided back through the layer's size and scale per axis — a flat number in local units would be hair-fine on a big shape and magnetic on a small one. The guide is drawn back THROUGH the same local→canvas map as the points, so on a rotated layer it comes out slanted with the shape, which is where the alignment actually is; it is extended past the shape's box so it reads as a rule rather than as another edge of the outline, and it ticks the haptics when it CATCHES rather than every frame it holds. Two bugs came out of building it, both caught by the suite rather than by reading. First, I named the snap helper `nearest` — and this file already had a nearest(e) that hit-tests the pointer against the points. A second function declaration of the same name in the same scope silently replaces the first, so every tap in the editor started throwing, and the regression test for "a missed tap never escapes to the canvas" went red immediately. Second, and this one is a real bug that predates the feature: dragging a point and then grabbing it again within 350ms counted as a DOUBLE TAP and deleted it, because only taps were meant to latch but nothing cleared the latch after a drag. Two ordinary adjustments in a row and the point was gone. A drag now clears it. Mutation-checked three ways — removing the snap, making the threshold infinite, and removing the latch clear all turn it red.
- v5.45 — the text box stops looking mangled. Ezra: "Edit text menu is broken." Nothing was thrown and every control worked — I drove the real route (open the phone inspector, tap the Edit Text tile) and the editor came up with all six toolbar buttons, no console errors. What was broken was the PICTURE, and it was mine: the wrap handles added in v5.40 were a fixed 26px tall sitting ON the border, so on a one-line caption they were TALLER than the box itself and clamped over the first and last glyph — and their 14px touch pads then swallowed taps meant for the letters underneath. They were also drawn inside the focused text editor, on top of the very text being typed, where they have no job at all. Now: 3px wide instead of 4, positioned fully outside the border with clearance rather than straddling it, height proportional to the box (56%, floored at 10px and capped at 30px) so a handle can never be taller than the thing it is a handle for, and hidden outright while body.text-editing is set. Guarded by a test that measures the real rects — clearance from the box edge, and handle height against box height — and mutation-checked by putting each of the three back. The first version of that test was too lenient: it asked only that the handle not OVERLAP, which a bar ending exactly on the border satisfies while still sitting on the glyph, so putting the old position back did not turn it red. It asks for real clearance now. Also swept up while in there: the category grid's tile handler sets the inspector view directly instead of going through openCategory, so it was skipping the _mtAxis reset — tapping Move & Transform from the grid could land you in the v5.43 Z sub-mode left over from last time.
- v5.46 — the Add sheet stops jumping, and its tab row fills. Ezra: "I want the top row of all the addable stuff to take up the whole row neatly, also I want every tab in the add section on mobile to be the same height, currently some are lower than others and it makes going between them all feel jumpy and shit." This is the third item on his list — the one I failed at three times earlier and parked — and it went in cleanly this time because I measured it first instead of designing at it. The numbers before: five tabs pinned at a fixed 66px plus gaps came to 358px inside a 354px row, so the row OVERFLOWED by 4px and could be nudged sideways, reading as loose rather than deliberate; and the sheet stood 209px tall on Media / Audio / Template against 356px on Shape, so the whole panel moved 147px under your thumb every time you changed tab. Two causes, both simple once named. The tabs were `flex: 0 0 auto`, so they never shared the row — now `flex: 1 1 0` with `min-width: 0` divides whatever width there is, which is exactly full at any screen size and can never scroll. And nothing in the sheet ever STATED a height, so it was content-sized and every tab got its own — now the body is `clamp(170px, 32vh, 260px)` and scrolls inside that. The clamp is what the earlier attempts were missing: they tried to make the inner content FILL its parent, and a parent that is itself sized by its children cannot also size them, so the panel collapsed to 1px every time. A number breaks the circle. Verified at 320x568, 375x667 and 430x932: tab row 100% full with zero overflow at all three, and 0px of movement between tabs at all three, with the body adapting 182 / 213 / 260px. Mutation-checked three ways — restoring the fixed tab width, restoring the content-sized body, and making the stated height clip instead of scroll all turn it red. NOT done, deliberately: the "icons scale to fill the empty space" half of the original ask. A thin tab like Audio (one card) now shows real empty space under it, which is the honest consequence of a stable height, and stretching one card to 260px would look worse than the gap does. That is a taste call for Ezra rather than something to guess at.
- v5.47 — the easing editor becomes three graph FAMILIES, each with its own presets and its own grab points. Ezra, with six AM screenshots: "it currently treats the bounce graphs as just another option, but how it works in alight motion is that there's three types of graphs — cubic bezier easing, bounce, and steps, and each section has its own presets… For bounce the three different presets actually change the points you can grab and adjust… Just get the base down and we can tweak later." The blocker was never the UI, it was the data: an easing could only be a cubic bezier (four numbers, two handles) or one of two FIXED functions. `bounce` and `elastic` had NO parameters at all, so there was literally nothing to grab — a bounce you cannot tune is a preset, not a graph. So a keyframe can now carry `ez = { fam, preset, p }`, a new js/eases.js owns the six parameterised presets (Bounce, Elastic, Cyclic, Random / Steps, Elastic Steps), and evalProp resolves `ez` ahead of the existing chain. Backwards compatibility is load-bearing and tested both directions: easeApply returns null for an absent or unknown ez so an old project — or an import naming a preset this build has never heard of — falls straight through to the code that has always been there, and writing an `ez` also leaves a plain `e: 'easeInOut'` behind so a reader that ignores the field gets a sane curve instead of a linear one. Each preset declares three things — fn, defaults, and `points` (where each handle SITS for a given parameter bag, and what a drag does to it) — so the editor never branches on a preset name: it draws `points` and calls `drag`. Adding a preset is an entry in that file, not a change here. Two real bugs came out of verifying rather than eyeballing. A parameter sweep over every corner of every range caught elastic returning 1.002 instead of 1 at high amplitude — scaling the sine breaks BOTH ends, not just the start, and a keyframe you do not land on exactly is a layer that jumps; both residuals are now subtracted along complementary ramps. And the first render had every curve leaving the top of the box, because these families overshoot on purpose (bounce peaks near 1.9, elastic near 3.7) and the graph was pinned to 0..1 — it auto-fits its vertical range now, taking the handles into account so they cannot be stranded off-canvas, and dashes in where 0 and 1 actually are so you can still read how far past the keyframes the curve goes. One thing only the picture caught: elastic's period was clamped to a CSS-ish 0.12..0.9, which made its constant ten times too big — it rang ~33 times inside one segment and read as a smear. It uses the classic easeOutElastic scale now (1..8, default 3) and matches the screenshot. Mutation-checked three ways: un-pinning elastic's end, making evalProp ignore ez, and letting an unknown family resolve all turn it red.
- v5.48 — five layout bugs from the 27-agent sweep, each re-measured before and after rather than taken on the agents' word. (1) The home TAB ROW was not in the same column as anything under it: a full-bleed flex row, so on a 1440 desktop the first pill sat at x=18 while the search field, its hint and every project card started at 370 — a 352px offset with three rows sharing an edge and one stranded in the left gutter. Now width:100% with a 736px cap (700 for the column plus this row's own 18px padding) and auto margins: measured 0px offset at 1440, and the phone is back to the 2px it always had, because 736 never binds below it. width:100% is the load-bearing half — the row is a flex ITEM, so max-width alone let it shrink-wrap to its pills and margin:auto centred THAT, which measured +193px, worse than the bug. (2) On the DESKTOP Add panel the Template tab was entirely off-screen: five 66px tabs plus gaps is 358px inside a 288px panel, so it rendered at 292..358, past the edge, with the row's scrollbar hidden and no fade or chevron to say it existed. A mouse-only user had no route to Templates at all — the '5' key still worked, which is why it survived. Dividing that row five ways would leave ~52px a tab and truncate "Elements", so the panel wraps to a 3-column grid instead: 5 of 5 fully visible at both 1440 and 380, nothing to scroll. (3) open-badge.png carried 30 pixels of debris — a fully detached alpha island at (131,89)-(138,95), left over from the same luminance key that cut the letters, rendering as a dust speck on the thumbnail. Found by walking the asset's connected components, cleared, cache-buster bumped. (4) The glass theme's generic `input:focus` box-shadow was landing on the home search's bare inner input, which has no border and no radius — a square-cornered 3px rectangle inscribed in the 12px-rounded pill, two hard vertical bars down its full interior height. The halo that was actually wanted comes from the wrap's own :focus-within on the same line, so the inner input is exempted. (5) The header magnifier's ink measured 13.5px against the gear's 18px in an identical 38px circle — the artwork only filled ~18.5 of its 24-unit viewBox — so it was redrawn to fill it. NOT fixed, and left open with its evidence: the Studio inspector band overflows by 59px (269px of band, 328px of content, so the last row of categories sits below the window edge). Giving it more columns was the obvious answer and it did NOT move the number, which means the overflow is not coming from the category grid — so it needs a real diagnosis rather than a plausible-looking CSS rule, and shipping one I could not demonstrate would have been worse than leaving it.
- v5.49 — the design reaches the top of the screen, and the backdrop stops being stiff. Two of Ezra's asks, one surface. THE CUT-OFF: "at the very top of the screen there's a cut off, and I feel it looks very ugly, the design should just flow to the very top seamlessly." The viewport meta had no `viewport-fit=cover`, and without it iOS letterboxes a standalone web app BELOW the status bar and fills that strip with the page background — which is the inline `html,body{background:#000}`, i.e. exactly the flat black band with a hard seam he photographed on both home and the editor. The rest of the app was already written for cover mode (a dozen rules consume env(safe-area-inset-bottom)) so those insets were simply resolving to 0 and doing nothing. Turning it on then exposed the two elements that never paid the top inset: .hm-top, which would have put the wordmark under the clock, and #topbar-m, whose own surface has to fill the strip or cover mode just MOVES the seam instead of removing it. Two bottom-anchored things were also on flat offsets and would have sat under the home indicator once the inset became real — the + FAB and the add sheet's last row. THE BACKDROP: "the background design needs more animation, currently it's a bit stiff, maybe just make it move faster." Speed was only half of it. Only ONE of the two gradient layers was animated at all, and the other turned out to be a bug rather than a decision: #home-screen.hm-intro::before is more specific than the plain ::before rule and listed only the entrance bloom, and .hm-intro stays on the element, so that layer sat frozen on its first keyframe forever. Its ::after sibling chained both animations, which is why nobody could see the difference. Both drift now, at 19s and 26s with roughly double the travel — and the MISMATCHED periods matter more than the speed, because two layers on one clock slide as a single flat sheet while 19 against 26 only realign every four minutes. Measured on the real page: ::before went from 0.00 px/s (frozen) to 1.60, ::after runs 5.92. Amplitudes stay small on purpose; the moment you can point at a blob crossing the screen it has stopped being a backdrop. A real safe-area inset cannot be produced headlessly, so the test asserts the two things that CAUSE the seam rather than the seam itself — cover mode is on, and every element pinned to a screen edge pays the inset on that edge — and that check earned its keep immediately by catching that I had misread a grep and #topbar-m had never had the padding I thought it did. Mutation-checked four ways.
- v5.50 — a Tutorials tab on home, deliberately empty. Ezra: "don't make any tutorials yet, but add a tab alongside elements projects etc that says tutorials, we will later make tutorials and put them in there." So: the fourth pill, its own render branch with an empty state that says "Tutorials are coming" rather than "none found" — an empty state that reads like a failure is worse than no tab — and two things that had to be told about it. The + button hides on this tab instead of offering to create a project from a screen that has no projects on it, and select-mode returns null for it, since there is nothing selectable to bulk-delete. The tab row itself needed the work: four fixed-width pills no longer fit a 380px phone and the last one was clipped mid-word at the screen edge, so on phones they now share the row the same way the add sheet's tabs do (v5.46) — measured full labels at 380 and a clean ellipsis at 320 rather than a pill running off the edge. Desktop keeps the wider pills and the 736px column alignment fixed in v5.48.
- v5.51 — the ⋯ menu's two invisible toggles say what they do, and one of them can finally show its state. Ezra: "what does the snapping magnet and onion skin things in the three dot menu even do???" Both are toggles whose effect only appears once you go and try the thing they change, and they were labelled with nothing but their own names. Worse, Snapping was the only one of the three timeline toggles with NO ✓ tick — and the reason turned out to be real rather than cosmetic: `snapping` is a module-local in timeline.js with no accessor, so the menu had no way to read it. Which meant the one toggle that DEFAULTS ON was also the one that never showed its state, so tapping it looked like it did nothing at all. FM.timeline.isSnapping() exposes it read-only and the tick works. Labels now carry a half-sentence each: "Onion skin — ghost the layer before/after now" and "Snapping (magnet) — clips stick to edges".
- v5.52 — you can drag the effect you are actually looking at. Ezra: "dragging and layering effects is broken." Reordering worked fine on a COLLAPSED row and did nothing at all on an open one — beginReorder returned early on fx._expanded, and fxRow did not even draw the ⠿ grip for an open row. Because the effect stack is an ACCORDION (opening one closes the rest), that combination meant the effect you had just tapped to look at was precisely the one you could not move, and it failed silently: press, hold, drag, nothing happens, no feedback, no explanation. Reproduced before touching anything by driving the real press-hold-and-drag through the actual DOM: collapsed row armed .fx-dragging and reordered blur to the end; the same gesture on the same row while open never armed at all and left the order untouched. The guard's stated reason was uniform row height, and that reason had already stopped being true — the drop index comes from each sibling's MEASURED midpoint (moveReorder says so in its own comment), and slotH reads the dragged row's own measured height, so a tall row shifts its siblings by exactly its own size. Removing the guard and drawing the grip on open rows was the whole fix. Guarded by a test that opens a row, press-holds it, drags it to the end and checks both that the drag armed and that the order actually changed; mutation-checked by restoring each half of the old behaviour separately.
- v5.53 — freehand drawing, properly this time. Ezra, for the FOURTH time: "free hand drawing is still fucked, actually make sure this gets solved and it works fine and looks good." Two separate faults, and my earlier attempts had only poked at the second one.
  THE FUNCTION. onUp() called finish(), and finish() calls stop(), which tears the whole drawing mode down — overlay hidden, bar hidden, stage shrunk back. So lifting your finger ONCE ended the session: exactly one stroke per visit to the tool. And because committing a layer selects it, the phone then threw the inspector sheet over the entire screen (measured at 390x794 on an 800px display, starting at y=6) so you could not even SEE what you had just drawn. On top of that, updateBar() hid .db-done for freehand, so that mode had no Done button at all — the only way out was the accidental one. Now: a pointerup commits that stroke as its own layer and the tool STAYS armed, the canvas stays deselected while drawing (which is what kept the sheet shut), Undo takes back the last whole stroke rather than nibbling one invisible sample, and Done commits whatever is in hand, exits, and hands you the drawing selected. Traced through the real DOM before and after: before, the overlay was gone after one stroke and a second was impossible; after, two strokes land as two layers with the tool still armed, the bar reads Undo | Done | Cancel, and the inspector after Done is a closed 291px drawer instead of a 794px takeover.
  THE LOOK. The v5.19 pass ran Ramer-Douglas-Peucker ALONE, and that is the wrong tool used first: RDP is a SIMPLIFIER that keeps the points deviating MOST from a chord and discards those lying close to it. Hand tremor IS the deviating points — so RDP was preserving the wobble and throwing away the smooth parts, which is why raising its epsilon never helped and only began clipping corners off deliberate shapes. Filter first, simplify second: two passes of a 1-2-3-2-1 moving average with the endpoints pinned, then RDP at a LOWER epsilon (1.6, safe now that there is no jitter left to preserve), then mark the survivors [u,v,1] so the renderer curves through them. Measured as total absolute turning against a clean version of the same arc, so the arc's own curvature cancels: the tremor used to add 65.19 rad of excess turning (84 points, 67.69 vs 2.50) and now adds 0.41 (23 points, 2.93 vs 2.52) — 159x less wobble, and the before/after pictures are a trembling line against a clean one. Four mutations checked, each proven to turn the test red on its own: RDP alone, a collapsed stroke, over-smoothing, and dropping the curve flags.
- v5.54 — Motion Blur (Object) gets the app's own number controls. Ezra: "some effects don't use the slider format that we should have, and also the sliders on those ones can be finicky, like this one." Its Shutter and Samples were bare <input type=range> built by rangeRow(), and a 0..1 Shutter on a raw range input has almost no usable travel — a whole shutter angle inside a couple of hundred pixels, which is the "finicky". They are mtVBox value boxes now, the same control as X / Y / Z and every other number in the app: drag to scrub with glide, tap to type an exact figure, with the min/max still enforced. HALF of that request is deliberately NOT done, and the reason is architectural rather than effort. He also asked why it "doesn't even have the proper menu like effects should… it kinda just gets engraved as its own unique thing" — and the answer is that it genuinely is not an effect: it lives at layer.motionBlur, not in layer.effects[], and drawMotionBlur WRAPS THE WHOLE LAYER DRAW, re-rendering the layer at several sub-frame times and averaging them, then returning early from the entire draw path. A stack effect filters a plate that has already been rendered; this one has to run before and around that. So giving it a real fx-row — disclosure, eye, ⋯ menu with reset/duplicate/copy/paste — is not a registry entry plus a UI swap, it is a rewrite of how it renders, plus a migration for every saved project carrying layer.motionBlur. Recorded on the task rather than half-started.
- v5.56 — a click that lands straight after a reorder drop no longer toggles the effect's editor. Ezra: "if I only have two effects and I try to drag the top one down it just closes the menu." The per-row `_g.moved` flag exists to swallow the click that follows a drag, but dropping calls afterFx(), which REBUILDS every row — so in principle the click arrives at a brand new row whose flag is false, the accordion toggles, and the editor you were dragging shuts itself. A module-level timestamp survives that rebuild where a per-row flag cannot, and both stacks (effects and audio effects) consult it. HONESTY NOTE, because this matters more than the fix: I did NOT reproduce his bug. I wrote the latch first and only then ran the probe, which is the wrong order — and when I went back and ran that probe against the UNFIXED code it passed too, i.e. it never demonstrated the fault at all. A synthetic click dispatched on the pre-rebuild node still carries the old row's flag, so it cannot reproduce what a real browser does after a real drop. I also briefly added a suite assertion for this and removed it again: it stayed green with the fix taken out, and a test that cannot fail is worse than no test, because it turns "unverified" into a green tick. So the guard ships on its own merits — a click within 400ms of a drop should not be read as a tap, whatever the cause — and the underlying report stays OPEN until it can be reproduced on a real device.
- v5.57 — the export button is Ezra's glass artwork. His original ask, from a long way back: "Make this the new export button — make sure it actually looks good and isn't simply plastered on." I had skipped it while doing the items either side of it, and he was right to call that out. Keyed to RGBA by flooding the black inward FROM THE BORDER rather than by luminance, which is the whole trick here: the upload arrow is a DARK green cut into pale crystal, so a luminance key would have taken the arrow out along with the backdrop — the exact mistake the OPEN badge taught in v5.37 — while a flood from the edge can never reach an enclosed shape. Trimmed to its own bbox so the asset carries no dead margin. On the phone the mint 42x36 rectangle is gone and the button IS the artwork, square, because letterboxing a squircle inside a rectangle is precisely the plastered-on look he warned about. On desktop the pill keeps its WORD — a label earns its place where there is room, and an unlabelled square would have been a downgrade — but its body went dark: the artwork is pale faceted crystal and on the old mint fill it turned into a muddy smudge that read exactly like a sticker stuck on a button. Two passes were needed because the first one looked wrong in both places, and the reason the phone button stayed mint is worth recording: theme-glass.css paints a mint gradient over .m-export and #btn-export, and it loads AFTER styles.css, so it won — the fix had to be made there too, not only in the base sheet.
- v5.58 — the Studio inspector band stops overflowing, and the cause was not what the sweep said. The layout sweep reported the band 59px short (269px of height against 328px of content, so the last row of categories sat below the window edge) and proposed more columns on .cat-grid. That was wrong, and I had already tried it in v5.48 and watched the number not move at all, which is why it was left open with its evidence rather than "fixed". Measuring the band's children directly gave the answer in one line: the tallest child is the wrapper holding .addmenu--panel at 277px — the ADD MENU, not the category grid. And the second measurement was the useful one: of that 277px the BODY is only 133px, so the tabs and title are the bulk — which means my own v5.48 fix caused most of it. That change wrapped the desktop panel's five tabs into a 3-column grid so Template stopped rendering off the panel edge, and two rows of tabs cost roughly 50px. Right for the classic side column, which is 288px wide and tall; wrong for Studio, which is the one layout that puts the same panel in a band that is WIDE (405px) and SHORT. So Studio gets its five tabs back on one row, where they fit comfortably at that width, and the body gets a STATED max-height rather than a percentage — a percentage resolves against a parent with no definite height of its own and silently does nothing, which is the same circular dependency that defeated three attempts at the phone sheet before v5.46 broke it with a number. Measured after: Studio 269 client / 269 scroll, 0px overflow, panel down from 277px to 201px, body capped at 128 with 133 of content so every tile is still reachable by scrolling; classic 1440x900 unchanged at 0px overflow with its panel still 277px. All five tabs visible in one row in the capture.
- v5.59 — long videos can now be added on mobile. Ezra: "I cannot add long videos, it won't work." It was memory, not speed. Dropping a clip on the timeline calls FM.getWaveform, which decoded the ENTIRE audio track at the device rate and cached the result on the media rec for the session. Decoded PCM costs rate × channels × 4 bytes per second, so its size is set by DURATION, not by resolution or file size — measured (tests/_audiomem.html): 10 min = 219.7 MB, 20 min = 439.5 MB, 60 min = 1.3 GB, against a mobile tab ceiling of roughly 1-2 GB, and that is before the file itself and the pointless .slice() copy of it that decodeAudioData was handed. Now the waveform decodes into an 8 kHz OfflineAudioContext (decodeAudioData resamples to the context's rate — measured 6.0× smaller, so 20 min is 73.2 MB), is NOT parked in the full-fidelity rec.audioBuffer slot the exporter uses, and the duplicate copy is gone. Files over 300 MB skip the waveform outright: a clip without a drawn waveform is a cosmetic loss, a killed tab loses the project.
- v5.60 — a media write that the browser REFUSED was reported as success. idbPut resolved identically on tx.oncomplete and tx.onerror, and writeMedia returned a hardcoded true on top of it, so a video too large for the origin quota was rejected, announced as saved, and silently missing after a reload — on mobile, where the quota is far smaller and Safari rejects rather than prompting, that is most of what "I cannot add long videos" looks like from the outside. Now the write reports what actually happened, a failure says so with the real usage/quota numbers, and the app asks once for persistent storage so the browser stops being free to evict a project's clips with no user action.
- v5.61 — three queue items, each built by one agent and then adversarially verified by a second. (36) The export button has real shine: a rake plus one tight specular catch where the artwork already has a bright facet, the v5.38 wordmark lesson applied again. Brightest 2% inside the crystal 0.8150 → 0.9076 on phone and 0.7862 → 0.9048 on desktop; the upload arrow is a HOLE in the alpha, not a dark shape, so the bloom is kept tight or it floods straight into it. (40) The rotate dial's degree readout is finally centred — .mt-dial-read was position:absolute inside a position:static .mt-dial, so inset:0 resolved against #inspector-panel and the number centred on the PANEL, 29-35px above the ring; the horizontal alignment Ezra saw was coincidence, not centring. Worse in the Studio layout, where it resolved against the VIEWPORT and painted the number dead centre of the window, over the canvas. Now a child of the ring, centred by transform so the box shrink-wraps the string (23.8px at "0°" to 48.2px at "-45°") and stays put as digits and the minus sign come and go. (41, separate) Tapping the X/Y/Z NUMBER now selects that axis; the number still opens the type-in editor once its own axis is live, so nothing is lost and the target grows from a 12px label to the whole 84x39 box. Also: Speed is now a visible-but-disabled card on layers with no source to re-time (shape/text/image), matching the greyed Volume card — proven by rendering, not reading: a shape with x keyframed 20→300 hashes identically at 1x, 4x and 0.25x speed.
- v5.62 — the Custom elements button works. Ezra: "when I press the custom elements button literally nothing happens." It was opening perfectly the whole time: display:block, visibility:visible, opacity:1, the search field and the tiles all built — and a measured rect of 0x247. The geometry of a full-screen browser panel comes from its ID (#fx-browser had position:fixed / inset:0 / z-index:160), and #el-browser was never added to that rule, so it laid out as a zero-width inline box behind the app. js/elements-browser.js gives its root the class `fxb-root` to borrow the effect browser's look, but that class has no rules anywhere — which is why nothing looked wrong in the source. Now measured at 0,0,375,812 on a phone, covering the viewport. The new test asserts the STYLESHEET (a bare probe element per browser ID must compute position:fixed with a real z-index) rather than opening the panels, because the effect browser will not open without a selected layer in the harness — driving it would have measured the harness instead of the bug.
- v5.63 — the PC export button. Ezra: "on pc the export button has a green outline and looks shit, and coz its got a grey arrow icon its hard to tell that it isnt just a blue blob, but on mobile youve done nothing to it." Two faults, both PC-only, which is why mobile looked untouched. (1) TWO ARROWS: index.html still renders the original inline <svg class="ico"> and ::before paints the crystal artwork on top of it, so a flat grey arrow sat over the crystal's own arrow. The phone button has hidden its .m-ico since v5.57; the desktop one never did. (2) The mint ring and mint label were leftovers of the old accent button, ringing the artwork in a colour it does not contain — now a neutral hairline with the word in normal text colour. And theme-glass.css held its OWN copy of that mint ring, so it kept repainting it after styles.css dropped it; that file has now silently eaten three separate fixes, which is why it is in the house rules.
- v5.63 (also) — the PC home tabs. Ezra: "make the boxes on pc for templates projects etc squircle rectangles instead and make sure they span the width that the projects do, so it doesnt look small." Two faults: the tab row was capped at 736px while the project list is capped at 700, so they were never going to line up; and .hm-tab had no `flex` outside the phone media query, so on PC the four tabs shrink-wrapped their own text into a short left-aligned huddle inside that row. Both widths now come from one --hm-col variable (and .hm-tabs adds back its own 36px of side padding, which lives inside max-width) so the two rows cannot drift apart again. Measured at 1280 / 1440 / 1920: tab row and project list both 700px wide and aligned to 0px on both edges, four equal 168px tabs. Corners are now 16px to match .hm-card rather than a 999px pill — that part reaches the phone too, which is a deliberate consistency call rather than the PC-only scope he asked for.
- v5.64 — long exports no longer kill the tab. The muxer wrote into an ArrayBufferTarget with fastStart:'in-memory', which by definition holds the ENTIRE movie in one JS ArrayBuffer so the moov atom can sit at the front; deliver() then did new Blob([buffer]), so at the moment of delivery the file existed twice. At the exporter's own bitrate (min(80 Mb/s, W x H x fps x 0.12)) a 1080p30 export costs ~0.9 MB per second of footage — 20 min is ~1.07 GB and 60 min ~3.2 GB against a mobile tab ceiling of roughly 1-2 GB. Now fastStart:false puts the moov at the end, which makes the writes sequential, and a StreamTarget sink folds them into disk-backed Blob storage every 4 MB, honouring the byte positions the muxer patches (it rewrites the mdat header at the front once the payload length is known — ignoring that argument silently corrupts the file). Measured: a 20s export 358.6 MB -> 30.4 MB of heap, 40s 717.0 MB -> 60.9 MB. Decisive case, 1280x960 for 200s at 70 Mb/s: the old code dies with "RangeError: Array buffer allocation failed" after 197.5 seconds and delivers nothing, while the new code writes a working 1.655 GB file at +10.5 MB peak heap, moov at offset 1,735,414,506, and a real decoded frame at t=150s. Cost, stated rather than hidden: without fastStart the file is no longer progressive-play, so a player must read the tail before it can begin — a non-issue for a file you download, a real one for a file streamed from a URL. Verified across video-only, video+AAC, a 1-frame export, a from/to range, cancel-then-re-export, GIF and PNG-sequence, with frames decoded back at five timestamps.
- v5.65 — Captions is a real feature. Ezra: "I'm pretty sure the captions feature is a fake feature that doesn't even work, give it actual function, this is a big job, if it can detect speaking that would be insanely good." He was right, and the shape of the fakeness was specific: the DATA and the RENDER existed, but the editor and the timeline did not — typing on a caption track set layer.text, which nothing read, so the field stayed empty, the picture never changed and the cue text never moved. Measured before: field "", canvas ink 10805 -> 10805 after typing, cue[0].text unchanged, 0 cue chips, FM.captions and FM.detectSpeech undefined. After: field "First caption", ink 10805 -> 14634, cue[0].text carries what was typed, 2 chips on the clip. Now: a real cue model with FM.captions.*; the text editor binds to the CUE under the playhead with ‹ Cue n/N › navigation; cues appear as chips on the timeline clip you can move, trim and double-click; and they burn into the export through the same text path as a text layer, verified with a real MP4 export round-trip rather than a preview check. Plus FM.detectSpeech in the new js/captions-vad.js — voice-activity detection over the layer's own audio, decoded at a low sample rate through the existing FM.decodeAudio(file, {rate}) path that v5.59 added, so finding where someone talks costs a fraction of a full-rate decode. It lays cues on the speech, which is the tedious half of captioning. Actual word transcription is deliberately NOT built: it needs a large model download and is a tradeoff for Ezra to choose.
- v5.66 — four queue items, each built by one agent and adversarially verified by a second. (54) "Still forces me to open up a layer to drag it" AND "if I only have two effects and I try to drag the top one down it just closes the menu" turn out to be ONE bug, and it was never the accordion tap-guard: on a phone the inspector is a bottom sheet with a swipe-down-to-dismiss gesture, and pressing an effect row's grip armed BOTH the reorder and that sheet gesture. The grip carries touch-action:none so it kept feeding pointermoves instead of becoming a sheet scroll, and on the second move the sheet called setPointerCapture(), retargeting every later move AND the pointerup to the panel — so the row never ran endReorder(), the drop was thrown away, and past 33% of the sheet height it called selectLayer(null): layer deselected, panel emptied, "it just closes the menu". The v5.56 400ms guard is UNREACHABLE on that path and never fixed anything. The grip now joins the list of controls mobile.js already refuses to hijack. (46) Safe-area sweep of every fixed/overlay header under viewport-fit=cover — the Add Effect header's ✕ and magnifier were measured at 440x956 with a 62px inset sitting 48px up inside the clock/battery row, level with them and untappable. Fixed with the same max(base, inset) idiom the home and settings headers already use, so nothing moves where the inset is 0. (34) Parameter-row selection: tap a property's name to pick whose keyframes you are editing. (30) Add-Effect thumbnails: one example subject per section, plus a measured fix for every tile that demonstrated nothing.
- v5.67 — drag on the canvas to position a gradient or image fill (queue 33). Ezra: "when it's on the gradient option or on the image option, you should be able to drag on the canvas to change where the image is positioned." Media pans within its cover-fit clip, a gradient moves its centre, and the tool claims the canvas the way FM.cropTool and FM.pointEdit already do rather than special-casing canvas-edit. It shipped with one blocking regression that a verifier caught and that is fixed here: app.js's deselectOnEmptyTap listens on DOCUMENT in the CAPTURE phase, so an overlay's own stopPropagation() cannot reach it — a surface is spared only by being NAMED in a KEEP selector list, and #fd-overlay was not. The result was that a tap, or any press ending within 6px of its start, anywhere on the comp INCLUDING on the shape being edited, deselected the layer and closed Colour & Fill. Strictly worse than before the tool existed, since canvas-edit only ever deselected a stationary tap OFF the layer — and the tool's own hint ("Drag to move the gradient") invites exactly the gesture that broke it. The same comment block already documents this bug being fixed once before for the eyedropper and crop overlays. Verified here independently: tap keeps the layer and the tool; a 4px nudge keeps it; and removing #fd-overlay from that list makes both go red with selectedId=null.
- v5.68 — the car shape, and a bug that was destroying work. (56) Ezra: "the car shape is really wide and streched out for some reason, should be fixed if you just squish it until the tires are perfectly round." The DECLARED aspect was the bug, not the traced geometry: the v5.33 redraw stopped being a trace and became a landmark polyline that carries its own proportion inside the unit box (ink measures 0.9576 x 0.5200 of it, 1.841:1) and draws both tyres as true circles there. The box only SCALES that drawing, so anything but 1:1 turns every wheel into an ellipse by exactly the box ratio — and the stale 1.76 x 0.57 left over from the v3.96 trace is 3.093:1, which stretched the car to 5.695:1 of ink and the wheels to 3.1:1. Now car: [1, 1], and his acceptance test is the regression test: the wheels must be circles. (49) Clicking a malformed preset row ran layer.effects = fx.map(...) on a row whose .effects was missing or empty — it still rendered, as "(0 effects)", and the click assigned an EMPTY array, silently destroying every effect on the selected layer with no confirmation. Two more silent paths in the same area: a failed save returned false with no toast and no throw, so failing to save was indistinguishable from succeeding; and a param over MAX_KF (240) made the WHOLE preset get discarded without a word. All three fixed.
- v5.69 — the timeline glitch Ezra photographed (IMG_2445, queue 57). The group row's teal bar ran edge to edge across the full width INCLUDING the left track-head column, where every other row shows only its eye and thumbnail on the panel background — and a child row named "Black hshshshahahahhas hhwhahaha…" ran to the right edge with its text passing under the ≡ row-menu button, no ellipsis. The head was stacking correctly the whole time (position:sticky, z-index 6), so the clip had not escaped its lane; it was the row's own background painting across the head, which is why the eye and folder icon still drew on top of the teal. Long layer names are now clamped so they cannot run under the ≡. A verifier reproduced the bug, reproduced the fix, and broke it five ways to prove each piece is load-bearing — finding that one of the five edits was NOT load-bearing and that two quoted numbers were wrong, neither of which changes the outcome.
- v5.70 — the easing editor finally fits, on the third attempt (queue 39). Ezra: "get rid of the old curve presets that don't fit the bezier style and also make all of the curve layout actually fit on screen so you don't need to scroll." The bezier rail is now linear / easeIn / easeOut / easeInOut / overshoot / anticipate (bounce and elastic are real functions that cannot be expressed as a cubic bezier, and hold is a step); the pager arrows are gone and the name is smaller. Overflow of #inspector-panel reaches 0 at all eleven sizes tested — phone 320x700 (was 277 over), 360x640 (297), 380x800 (224), 390x800 (224), 414x896 (166); Studio 1280x720 (304), 1280x860 (278), 1440x900 (266), 1920x1080 (180); desktop 800x600 (217), 1024x768 (49) — with the graph still square and at least 74px. The two previous attempts were rejected for buying that fit by shrinking controls below a touch target and pushing them into scrollers with hidden scrollbars; this one is verified with a hit-test (elementFromPoint) as well as a rect check, which catches "covered" that a rect check cannot. Before, between 4 and 22 controls per size were clipped and the loop toggle was off-screen and unhittable at ALL eleven. One defect the verifier found inside its own pass is fixed here: .es-loop had gone 40px to 34px, under the 36px bar, and the change's own >=36px assertion was scoped to .es-preset/.es-fam only — so the one control it shrank was the one control the test did not watch. It is 36px now and the assertion covers it, mutation-proved.
- v5.71 — one options layout for every layer, and Audio Effects folded into the effects browser (queue 45). Ezra: "some layers look like the first image with the button option layout and some look like the second image. I want them both to look like the second image, just move the audio effects to the effects menu but put a toggle at the top that switches from showing you either the normal effects or audio ones, you can just grey it out and make it not selectable if a layer has no audio." Speed and Volume are now numbered CARDS on every layer type, matching IMG_2434, and the top icon strip is just trim-in / split / trim-out everywhere; the Audio Effects card is gone and the browser carries an audio/visual toggle that greys out with no audio. "Has audio" means a genuinely DECODABLE audio track, not merely type video — silent screen recordings are ordinary, and keying it off the type would offer reverb on a clip with nothing to reverberate. It answers in three states (yes / no / not yet), probes at 8 kHz through the low-rate path v5.59 added rather than paying for full-fidelity PCM, and treats "unknown" as yes, because greying out a control that would have worked is worse than offering one that turns out to do nothing. Also fixes five unguarded setPointerCapture calls in js/point-edit.js and js/timeline.js: they existence-checked the method but did not try/catch it, so a released or synthetic pointer id threw NotFoundError — 26 other call sites in the app already guard, and the uncaught throw was aborting the whole test run rather than failing one test.
- v5.72 — the PC settings cog opens PROJECT settings inside a project (queue 52), Loop playback / Onion skin / Snapping move into that cog on PC (queue 35), and the phone top bar gains a third state it never had. The first attempt at this was rejected for a regression worth recording: FM.syncSelectionChrome owned two of the three body classes while mobile.js owned the third from its own count, and the state nobody covered was ONE layer selected in select mode — the first frame of every long-press. Measured at 380x780 driving the real gesture: that frame kept the project header, and the very next frame swapped it for the multi header and put the DELETE bin on 330..372, over 100% of the pixels Export had held one frame earlier. Muscle-memory Export became Delete mid-gesture. Now three states are decided in one place from the live selection — none / m-editing / sel-mode — and nothing else writes those classes or an inline display on those buttons; select mode gets its own title so the Group and delete controls sit on the LEFT and can never take the Export slot. On PC the three timeline toggles are filtered out of the ⋯ menu (desktopHide) rather than living in two places; the phone keeps them in ⋯ because its cog is Canvas settings and FM.settings is only reachable from home there — same items, one surface each.
- v5.73 — the Add panel fits its space, remembers its tab, and Elements gets its own mark (queue 50, 51, 42). Ezra: "the add section on pc on the left needs to actually fill up the screen space it has properly, its kinda akward like that, all that im asking rn is just for pc version. Make the icons get smaller or bigger depending on how zoomed in you have that area, rn it has lots of space and i still need to scroll on it to see them all which is weird." The hardcoded perPage constants (15/18 icon-only, 9/12 labelled) are gone; a solver picks columns, rows, tile size, font size and perPage from the box it is actually given. The headline number is one nobody had measured before, and it inverts how this looked: HEAD reached its low page counts by putting tiles OFF-SCREEN. At classic 800x600 Shape it drew 18 tiles per page of which only 12 were reachable — 6 clipped, 6 unhittable — so 24 of 72 shapes could not be reached at all, and the panel scrolled 88px underneath. Across 12 viewports x 5 tabs there were 142 tile-level failures; there are now 0 clipped and 0 unhittable, the PC panel never scrolls (it scrolled 10-88px at three sizes), and total PC pages went 64 -> 59. Three small PC bands DO gain a page — studio 1280x720 Elements 1->2, classic 800x600 Shape 4->6, studio 1280x720 Shape 4->5 — and that is the win, not a regression: at each of those HEAD only reached the lower count by overflowing, so reachable-tiles-per-page is what moved (800x600 Shape 48 of 72 reachable -> all 72). An earlier acceptance criterion of "must never gain a page" was wrong for exactly this reason and was corrected mid-flight. On the icons scaling: measured over 96 sampled panel boxes there are 46 cases where a bigger box yields a SMALLER icon and every single one coincides with a page-count drop; at a fixed page count there are ZERO inversions, so more room only ever costs icon size when it buys you the whole tab on one page. Two defects were caught before landing rather than after: the page dots had become 6x6px <button>s with a MEASURED 2px hit reach and pageCount extra stops in the tab order (reverted to HEAD's spans — 7 failures to 0, layout still green), and the FIT_CFG minimums forbade grids HEAD could actually draw, which alone cost studio 1280x720 Shape 4 pages -> 12. Queue 51 and 42 deliberately reach the PHONE, because unlike 50 he scoped neither of them to PC: the tab you had open reopens on both surfaces and survives a reload, and the Elements mark is a two-path cube instead of a triangle and a circle, readable at 18/22/24px on both themes, with the section's contents byte-identical (he asked for "just the little logo", and that is all that changed). The page WITHIN a tab is remembered for the session only and deliberately not persisted — coming back after a reload to "Shape, page 3 of 5" with nothing on screen explaining the missing start of the list is an extrapolation past what was asked for. Phone DOM is otherwise byte-identical: 661 nodes walked in document order per tab per tree per size, zero differences outside that icon. One thing shipped red before this and is fixed here: the new pager test dismissed the home screen before hit-testing but not the boot SPLASH, which is a full-window overlay with no pointer-events:none whose video never plays in a headless runner, so elementFromPoint answered #splash and the suite sat at 86/87 — the splash is now made pointer-transparent for that one assertion and restored afterwards. Suite 83/83 -> 87/87.
- v5.74 — three ways the app could destroy your work, and a lesson about the bug list itself (queue 15). All three were taken off BUG-HUNT.md, and all three turned out to be ALREADY FIXED as written — while each one was hiding a real, still-live bug a few lines away that the stale entry had made invisible. That is the finding worth keeping: the file now carries a warning not to schedule work from it without re-checking the code first. (1) THE PROJECT-BRICKER. v5.06 stopped the app CREATING a parent cycle (two groups each other's parent), but a document that already carried one — autosaved by any earlier build — still bricked v5.72, because v5.06 guarded the two parent walks it looked at and missed collectGroupUnits' walk (js/compositor.js:6986), the one that runs per unit-group per FRAME. Measured on a planted legacy project against pristine HEAD: refreshAll and renderScene both RangeError, boot dies with an unhandled rejection, home_visible false — no route back, and because FM.home.init() lives inside the load() .then(), one unopenable document cost access to EVERY other project. Now the walk is guarded, FM.repairParentCycles cuts exactly the one edge that closes each loop (a 4000-graph fuzz found zero failures) and runs on both load() and applyScene() — an imported .fmotion.json is untrusted input, and reIdLayers remaps both ends of a loop so a cycle survives re-id intact — and the boot chain has a .catch() so ANY future load failure lands on Home instead of taking the app with it. A healthy project still saves byte-for-byte identical (sha b7b2deab7bae7a63, 4048 bytes on both trees). The verifier failed to re-create the brick from eleven routes plus a brute-force sweep of every parent assignment the UI permits. (2) BACKSPACE UNDER THE HOME SCREEN. The v5.07 guard was a SELECTOR LIST — the same pattern that has now bitten this repo four times, because the list is always missing the surface added after it. It is a geometry rule now: all 22 bare editor keys are blocked under 11 real full-screen surfaces AND under a brand-new full-screen div named nowhere in the app, with the serialized scene, FM.playing, FM.time and localStorage byte-identical before and after. Escape still closes from all 14 surfaces, and the editor's own shortcuts are unchanged (17/22 fire with focus on body, 0/22 inside a text field). (3) THE FILMSTRIP LEAK. The documented one was fixed in v5.08, but switching projects still orphaned the strips: 253 MB per switch on a phone, measured with createImageBitmap and close() wrapped in WeakRef accounting so the harness pinned nothing. Now 96/96 bitmaps closed, 0 B pinned, 0 reachable after six forced GCs, undo still restores the clip and rebuilds its strip. Also fixed here: the new boot toast said "you are back at your projects" unconditionally, including when home.init() had itself thrown and the screen never appeared — it now asks the DOM and tells the truth, because sending someone hunting for a way back that is not there is worse than saying reload. Suite 87/87 -> 94/94.
- v5.75 — Magnify Background, the effect that was built once and reverted for doing nothing (queue 32). It genuinely did nothing, and the cause was the WIRING, not the maths. Registering it in POSTFX makes drawLayer take the `if (pp.length) { applyPostFx(…); return; }` branch, which returns ~90 lines BEFORE the Copy Background dispatch; inside applyPostFx an effect with no PIXEL_FX / WARP_FX / CANVAS_FX kernel falls through every `if` and returns undefined, so the layer drew ZERO pixels. The "identical stripe counts at zoom 1, 2 and 4" that got it reverted were the bare backdrop showing through a layer that had vanished. Both hypotheses the last investigation "ruled out" had been ruled out on invalid evidence: `_bgSnap` existing proved nothing, because it is created by renderScene's needsBgSnap / BG_SNAP_FX gate, which is a COMPLETELY DIFFERENT gate from FM.hasCopyBg — instrumentation printed `_bgSnap after render: 320x240   drawCopyBg entered: false` on the same render. And the origin hypothesis was false: scaling about raw M.e/M.f is exactly right, because getTransform() already folds the plate's __fmRS and __fmOX/__fmOY in (verified on three grids — unstamped, supersampled __fmRS=2, and cropped __fmRS=2/__fmOX=40/__fmOY=30, where the magnified region is the same SCENE rect in all three, not the same device rect). So it is built as a Copy-Background SIBLING, not a post-effect: the repo's "an effect takes four places" rule is wrong for this class — copybg is in three, and POSTFX/WARP_FX are precisely what killed the last attempt. Measured: zoom 1 byte-identical to a plain Copy Background layer over the full pixel buffer, zoom 2 gives 4 stripes of 14-16px, zoom 4 gives 2 stripes of 30px. The load-bearing regression test is deliberately NOT "the stripes got wider" but "the layer drew at all" — the frame with the layer versus the same scene with the layer deleted must differ — because that is the assertion that would have caught the original revert, and it is mutation-proved: putting magnifybg back into POSTFX turns it red with `the lens drew nothing`. Cost, stated rather than buried: roughly 2.2x plain Copy Background per layer. Two behaviour questions are deliberately left for Ezra rather than guessed — at zoom < 1 the source rect runs off the snapshot (currently CLAMPED; the alternative is to let it minify with transparent margins), and AM's magnifier has an offset/centre control while this magnifies about the layer's own anchor only. Fill Behind, Echo Keyframes and Time Quantization are still absent; Fill Behind in particular is blocked on a question for him, since pixel-effect plates are project-sized so a naive fill paints the whole frame. Suite 94/94 -> 96/96.
- v5.76 — the export button finally glows. Ezra, for the second time: "I find it crazy you still haven't added glow or anything to the export button, I swear you should've done that by now." He was right both times, and the reason is worth recording because it is a trap rather than an oversight. The crystal's rim is `--export-edge`, a 0.5px + 1.3px drop-shadow, and the comment above it explains why it was kept microscopic: the upload arrow is a CUT-OUT, and drop-shadow cannot tell the edge of a hole from an outer edge, so any wide FILTER glow bleeds up through the arrow and flattens it. That reasoning is correct — but it only ever applied to `filter`. A halo painted as its own layer, transparent through the middle where the arrow is and coloured only in a ring outside the silhouette, cannot reach the cut-out at all. So the glow is a radial-gradient on `.m-export::before`, not a drop-shadow, and the arrow stays crisp. The hue matches #add-fab's existing drop-shadow(0 0 9px rgba(90,199,237,.3)) on purpose: the + orb has glowed since it shipped while the export tile had nothing, and that inconsistency — both crystal objects, one lit and one flat — is exactly what he photographed in IMG_2456. TWO THINGS ALMOST BURIED IT AGAIN. First, theme-glass.css:297 carries `html[data-theme="glass"] .m-export { … box-shadow: none }`, and that file loads AFTER styles.css, so the obvious box-shadow implementation would have been silently deleted before it ever reached his screen — the FOURTH fix this file has eaten, and very possibly why earlier attempts at this went nowhere. A gradient sidesteps it. Second, the halo was painted correctly and then COVERED: #stage is position:relative with an opaque background and comes after #topbar-m in the DOM, while the bar was unpositioned, so everything the bar drew outside its own box was overpainted. Measured on a column through the button centre: (51,76,91) at y=50, then (4,9,12) at y=52 — byte-identical to an unlit control column, a dead-flat slice exactly at the bar's bottom edge. The bar is now position:relative z-index:30, which clears #view-bar (20) and #stage (auto) while staying far below the sheets and overlays (55+); verified that the home screen still covers the bar (elementFromPoint at the bar's centre returns the home brand, not the bar). After: (25,44,52) at y=52 falling to (9) by y=56 — a real falloff instead of a cut. Not done, and deliberately not guessed: the PC button is a labelled rectangle rather than a bare tile, so a halo round it would read as a glowing BUTTON instead of a glowing object — that one needs a look from him before I pick a treatment. Suite 96/96.
- v5.77 — two preview-vs-export correctness bugs, and BUG-HUNT.md made honest (queue 15). The document was re-swept end to end: six agents re-read every one of its 72 open findings against the code as it stands rather than against the v4.70 line numbers, which have all moved. Result: 12 were ALREADY FIXED, 2 are not reproducible, and 59 are still reachable — 29 of them high, and 2 only PARTIALLY fixed, meaning the exact bug described is gone while the defect class is still reachable by another route. Every struck-through heading now carries the version that fixed it, and the file's header says plainly that the body line numbers are stale and that code must be found by name. That count matters because the previous release proved how expensive the staleness was: three findings were picked off this file and built, all three were already fixed, and each stale entry was hiding a DIFFERENT live bug beside it. Two of the still-live ones are fixed here, both independently reproduced on unfixed code before either fix was believed. (1) THE PEN-MASK ALIAS. js/masks.js kept ONE module-level scratch canvas and FM.buildMaskAlpha returned a reference to it, and drawPenMaskLayer re-enters the renderer between taking that reference and consuming it — so a pen-masked layer whose Luma Matte / Displacement Map / Compound Blur SOURCE also carries a pen mask had the source's mask repainted over its own buffer mid-flight, and got rendered through the wrong shape entirely. The nested route was intact because the matte path clones the layer without nulling `masks`. A second alias was worse than the audit recorded: `_penMaskCv` was also a single module canvas, so the inner call cleared and reused the OUTER call's in-flight plate. (2) MOTION BLUR IGNORED THE PLATE STAMPS. It composited its accumulator with an identity transform, so on any preview canvas carrying a render-scale or crop stamp (__fmRS / __fmOX / __fmOY) the blur landed in the wrong place and at the wrong size — a preview that did not match the export, which is the dominant theme of this whole audit. Verified byte-identical export/thumbnail/reduced-playback output afterwards by SHA-256 of the full RGBA buffer rather than a bounding box, with six mutations going red including two the builder had not tried. A third fix — the audio-drive envelope recomputing a full loudness pass every frame of a clip drag, 60 passes and +5.15 MB per 2 s drag — is NOT here: its settle gate keeps a single pending slot per media record, so a source clip with three or more distinct Audio Drive signatures starves every envelope but one. Held back rather than shipped with a regression its own tests did not cover. Suite 96/96 -> 99/99.
- v5.78 — opening a project slides, on the third attempt (queue 55). Ezra: "Add a little animation that when you tap on a project the project that you tapped on swipes to the left and then the project actual screen comes in by swiping from the right. make sure it looks good." Rejected twice before, and BOTH rejections were failures of measurement SCOPE rather than of mechanism — the assertions only ever walked #home-screen, .hm-grid and the tapped card. Attempt 1 re-laid-out the whole home screen at DOUBLE WIDTH for the entire 280ms push; fixed with position:fixed on #app. Attempt 2 put the editor's floating + orb on the same keyframe as the screen, but a % in translate resolves against the ANIMATED ELEMENT's own box, so the 64px orb travelled 64px while the 390px screen travelled 390px — worst-frame desync 316px at 380 and 350px at 414, exactly viewport-minus-64 at every width, with a 16px cyan sliver of the editor's orb already painted on the HOME screen at t=0; fixed with a viewport-unit keyframe, measured 0.0px. This round fixed the last of it: on a COLD launch the press never appeared at all, because `.hm-card.fm-card-press { transform: scale(.965) }` is a DECLARATION and the home entrance `hm-rise` is an ANIMATION on the same property — an animation always wins — so the card sat at scale 1.0 for up to 34 frames / 533ms and then the push snapped it 1.0000 → 0.9650 in one frame, which is exactly the pop the whole press hand-off exists to remove. A card you have your finger on now stops entering. Measured after: 0 invisible frames and 0.0000 of hand-off jump across seven scenarios (150ms open, 900ms open, already-open project, repeat tap, cross tap, 320px, 414px, and reduced motion). Two more surfaced from that fix and are also closed: opacity stepped 0→1 on the touch frame, and the push lead then reset it 0.34829 → 1.00000 in one frame. The test was ALSO broken in a way that matters more than the bug: under prefers-reduced-motion it threw on its 4th of 32 checks, so 28 assertions never ran for those users — it now reports what EXECUTES, 70 in normal motion and 63 reduced, with 0 that never run in either. Two things fixed on landing. The push was not scoped to a phone: every rule keyed off fm-pushing, and a verifier caught the full 280ms slide playing at 1280x720 with #app going position:fixed z-index 210 mid-flight, where nothing had been measured and the Studio layout has its own fixed chrome to collide with — it is gated in JS now rather than by an @media block, so withholding one class makes every rule inert at once instead of leaving a width list for the next rule to fall outside of, and the suite asserts the real gate says NO in its own 900px frame before stubbing it. And the assertion guarding the headline of the whole feature — that the tapped card LEADS its own screen — was calibrated at a flat 40px against a real lead of ~118px, so the travel could be cut by 65% with the suite still green; it is 22% of the card's own width now, mutation-proved by cutting the keyframe to 10% (56px, which the old threshold would have passed). Suite 99/99 -> 102/102.
- v5.79 — the export glow, done properly. Ezra on the v5.76 attempt: "The glow for the export button look shitty." He was right. That version was a radial-gradient ring, and a radial gradient cannot hug a SQUIRCLE — it puts more light on the corners than on the edges, so it read as a donut sitting behind a sticker rather than as light coming off glass. Three techniques were rendered at 3x and measured against each other rather than argued about. (1) drop-shadow follows the silhouette exactly, and is the technique the + orb uses — but it FILLS THE ARROW, because the arrow is a CUT-OUT and drop-shadow cannot tell a hole from an outer edge. Measured mean luminance of the arrow: 105.6 with no glow, 137.6 at a radius of just 2px, 141.4 at 3px, 143.5 at 4px, against outside-light of only 38/48/56 — it washes the arrow out as fast as it lights the outside, so there is no usable setting. That is the constraint the original --export-edge comment recorded, and it is now measured rather than asserted. (2) the gradient ring: the donut above, plus a tighter variant that still read as an outline. (3) box-shadow, which is drawn strictly OUTSIDE the border box and therefore physically cannot enter the hole: arrow luminance came back at 105.64, IDENTICAL to the no-glow baseline to two decimal places and unchanged across 7px/9px and .50/.62 alpha, while the light outside the tile went 23.8 -> 58.3, stronger than any drop-shadow reached before ruining the arrow. So it is a box-shadow with a proportional 30% radius, which tracks the artwork's own corners at any size, and no brightness filter, so the arrow stays exactly as dark as it always was. The glow is declared in theme-glass.css as well as styles.css on purpose: that file loads LATER and its `box-shadow: none` on .m-export is precisely what would have deleted this before it ever reached his screen — it has already eaten four fixes, and the v5.76 gradient was chosen partly to route around it. Also recorded because it cost real time: the shared scratchpad is being rewritten by concurrent agents — a test-runner script and a CDP helper were both clobbered mid-session, and one measurement port was already occupied by another agent's server. Tooling that must be trusted now lives in its own directory and picks a verified-free port. Suite 102/102.
- v5.80 — the installed app no longer rotates. Ezra, with a screenshot (IMG_2458): "I just noticed a weird glitch when I turned my phone sideways, it shows an older mixed with new layout." Correct, and the cause is that the phone/desktop split is expressed purely as a WIDTH breakpoint at 700px: rotated, his phone is about 844x390, so it crossed into the DESKTOP chrome — brand wordmark, cog, ⋯ and the big labelled Export button — while everything below stayed phone-shaped in 390px of height. Two layouts on screen at once, which is exactly what "older mixed with new" describes. The proper fix would be to teach the breakpoint about height and pointer type so a rotated phone keeps the phone layout, and that work was briefed and started — then stopped, on his call: "I don't want you to have to make sure tho that three version are working each time, mobile, mobile sideways, and pc, would be a waste, so maybe ignore this unless there's a quick fix, as on launch it won't have a sideways option." He is right that a third geometry is a permanent verification cost on every future change, and the cheap fix removes the state entirely rather than supporting it: manifest.json "orientation" goes from "any" to "portrait", so the INSTALLED PWA never rotates and the mixed layout cannot occur. Stated honestly rather than buried: this only binds the installed app. A rotated Safari TAB is not covered by the manifest and will still show the split layout — no orientation lock exists for that case short of the breakpoint work he declined, so it stays a known cosmetic edge until he wants it done properly.
- v5.81 — the home screen buttons get the glass. Ezra: "can you make all the buttons have a more shiny and glass look, kinda like how the plus button is but not as detailed, more like how when you have a row selected, like in this image where I have projects selected… it would be cool if you could make all the buttons kinda have that glow to it by default, becuase it feels kinda off having the plus button look so good but not the rest, also adding a glow and more brightness to each project as well… I don't want a massive change, right now is very close. Don't forget the three buttons in the top right." So this is a LIFT of numbers already in the file, not a redesign, and nothing moved. The idle buttons — the four home tabs, the search circle, Select, the cog, and the settings close — go from a .09/.02 glass fill to .14/.045, their top rim from --glass-rim-soft to rgba(255,255,255,.20), and each gains the soft cyan bloom the ACTIVE tab already had, at 0 2px 10px rgba(90,199,237,.10) — about 40% of the selected tab's strength. The selected tab is raised in step (bloom .14 -> .26, rim .38 -> .44) so it keeps the same distance ahead and "selected" is still unmistakable; lifting the others without moving it would have flattened the one piece of state the row exists to show. Project cards go .05/.015 -> .085/.03 with their own bloom at .07, deliberately gentler than the buttons, because a full-width row lit at button strength stops reading as a list and starts reading as a stack of lit panels. It lands in theme-glass.css, which is the shipped default and the file that already owns every one of these surfaces — no new selectors, no markup change, no layout change. Verified as a before/after pair at 390x844 dpr3 rather than by eye on numbers alone. Suite 102/102.
- v5.82 — the five Add-menu tabs get their own colours. Ezra, circling the top row in red: "Add nice colouring to all of these buttons circled in red, make sure they all have their own unique colour scheme, and the media one should have multiple colours, like yellow for the sun green for the grass etc." All five wore the same cyan, which is also the app accent and every other icon in the sheet — five different destinations in one coat, so the row read as texture rather than as five choices. Now: Elements violet #A78BFA, Shape blue #60A5FA, Audio pink #F472B6, Template orange #FB923C, each set once in styles.css by [data-key] so there is exactly one place per tab that decides its hue. The hues are far apart in hue AND in the greyscale they collapse to, so they stay distinguishable at 22px and to anyone who does not separate red from green. MEDIA is the exception he asked for and it needed a second icon helper: ico() puts stroke="currentColor" on the whole SVG, which by construction makes an icon one colour, so icoMulti() renders the same geometry WITHOUT that blanket stroke and lets each child carry its own — a sky-blue frame #7CC9EA, a yellow sun #FBBF24 with a soft fill, and a green hill #4ADE80. A sun and a hill are not the same thing and should not be the same colour; every other tab stays single-colour and inherits from CSS. Verified by rendering the real sheet at 390x844 dpr3 and reading both the computed colour of each tab and the per-child stroke attributes of the Media icon, then looking at the row. Suite 102/102.
- v5.83 — the skip buttons stop only on keyframes you are editing, and the Media icon gets its ground line. Ezra: "make sure when you press the jump buttons, they don't jump to key frames that you aren't currently editing." FM.timelineSnapPoints took EVERY animated property on the selected clip, so a layer carrying position, scale, opacity and a few effect params turned ◀ and ▶| into a crawl through diamonds the user had no reason to visit.
