- v8.55 — **The track head stops wasting a strip on the far left (queue 295).** A follow-up to v8.50, and he was right: *"you've left a bunch of dead space on the far left side… you've got more room on the timeline even if it's only a little bit of extra room"*. v8.50 widened `--head-w` to 90 so the head could hold its contents — but 22px of those contents is the collapse chevron, which is `visibility: hidden` on any childless layer. His project has no groups at all, so every row was reserving space for an arrow that could never appear, and the red line on his screenshot runs exactly down it. **The space was reserved for a real reason** — it keeps a group row and a plain row at the same depth aligned (#191, *"the arrow pushes the ui over making it ugly"*) — so it is now held open only while the scene actually **contains a group**, because alignment with a group row is the only thing it was ever for. Measured at 375px on the same scene: with no groups the head is **72px with a 7px gap** left of the eye (was 27px) and the thumbnail keeps its full 34px with no spill; the instant a group exists the head returns to 90px and the columns line up again. **18px per row handed back to the timeline.** Mutation-checked both ways, and the "always reclaim" mutation trips #191's own test as well as the new one — which is the proof this did not just undo it.
- v8.56 — **The phone's "?" moves to the right of the version chip (queue 266).** His words: *"Question mark button is wonky and off centred and I need it to the right of the refresh button."* **Measured before touching it: the glyph is 0.00px off the centre of its own button on both axes, identical to the cog** — so "off centred" was never about the icon. The button was stranded between the project-name field and the chip, alone in the middle of the bar while the other three icons clustered to its right, which is what reads as wonky. Moving it right of the chip answers both halves at once and makes the phone match the PC row exactly (#171's order: ver · ? · notes · cog · Export). **The move broke a rule he had signed off, and the suite caught it before it shipped:** #189 spaces that run by VISIBLE ink rather than tap boxes, and dropping "?" between the chip and notes made the chip→notes gap measure straight through the new button — **64px against 18px**, the very pair the markup comment warned about. Re-spaced on real ink: **chip→? 16.8 · ?→notes 25.9 · notes→cog 24.1 · cog→Export 18.0** — edge-bounded gaps within 1.2px, icon-to-icon within 1.8px, and the 24.1 he called perfect kept untouched. The inner pair is deliberately 1.8px apart rather than exact: closing it needs the two tap targets to overlap, because the ink gap is the sum of each button's own padding before any box gap exists, and that line is drawn on purpose on a bar that also carries the delete bin. The #189 test now carries "?" in its run — omitting it would measure through the button and call an even bar broken — plus new assertions that the two icon gaps match each other and that no tap target overlaps to achieve it.
- v8.57 — **The sound-effects icon is finally white (queue 267).** His words: *"The sound effects icon needs to be the white gradient, it still is red. The rainbow and white border is good but the sound icon still isn't."* **The earlier pass had already added the right-looking rule** — `.addmenu-card--rainbow > .addmenu-ic { color: #ffffff }` — and it did nothing, because these strokes are painted with `stroke="url(#fm-ic-sfx)"`, an explicit paint server. A CSS colour cannot override an SVG gradient reference, so the icon kept using its own pink ramp (`#FBC2E7 → #E8438F`) whatever the cascade said. That is precisely why it looked fixed in the diff and was still red on his screen — worth remembering the next time a colour fix "lands" and the user says it did not. The gradient itself is white now, at falling opacity (1 → .62) rather than white into grey, which is the same language as the ring around this card and answers the same words he used for it: *"not solid white, give it some gradient"*. Verified at 375px with a screenshot. The test parses the rendered stops rather than matching the source string — so `#fff`, `#ffffff` and `white` all pass and a coloured stop cannot slip through on formatting — and separately asserts the two ends differ, since a flat fill wearing a `<linearGradient>` would otherwise satisfy it.
- v8.58 — **Template tiles show the template, not a logo (queue 268, and #210's last clause).** His words: *"I want an actual visual representation of what's in the template kind of like how each project and template in the home menu you actually has a picture to it."* **The entry told the next person to check whether the machinery already existed before building anything, and it did — more completely than expected.** `FM.templates.save` has always stored an inline `thumb` on the index entry, captured from the project at save time; that is the very picture the home screen renders. The add-menu tiles were simply not asking for it, so all nine drew the same little frame glyph. A tile with a thumb now shows it edge to edge with the name over a gradient at the foot — the same treatment the media tiles already use, so no second look had to be invented — and it **loses its colour tint**, because a colour plate under a photo is just a wash across it. That is **#210's remaining clause word for word** (*"it shouldn't even colour it should show the hero image of whatever the template is (still keeping the text)"*), so that entry closes too. A template saved before thumbs existed keeps the glyph **and** its colour, asserted separately — a fallback that quietly became a blank card would be worse than the bug being fixed. Verified at 375px against a real saved template: a 203×360 picture on the tile, no tint, name intact. *(The test stubs `FM.templates.list` rather than saving a real template — this suite runs against the app's own storage and must not leave anything in his library.)*
- v8.59 — **The Media and Audio tabs pin their own buttons above the scrolling library (queue 269 + 276).** His words for Audio: *"the three that I highlighted … should be segregated from the ones at the bottom and when you scroll to the left and right it shouldn't scroll those top buttons. It should just scroll through the ones that you've added so those are always on screen."* For Media he asked for the same plus the divider: *"those stay at the top and like a separated and like by line."* Built as **one mechanism for both**, because he asked for identical behaviour and building it twice is how two things end up subtly different. **The split is by what a card IS, not by counting:** a library tile carries `mid`, the media library's own id, so a card either came from his imports or it is one of the buttons the tab ships with — no list of names to go stale the next time a button is added. **The strip is a sibling above `.addmenu-body`, outside the pager**, which is what makes the guarantee real: it cannot scroll by construction rather than by a rule that has to be maintained. It also means none of the fit arithmetic (#50/#208, which sizes tiles from a measured box) needed to learn about it — `fitBox` measures from the body's own top down, so a strip above it leaves the tile budget automatically. The one thing that did matter was **order**: the strip must be drawn before the plan is measured, or the tiles get sized for a box the divider then eats into. Two cases handled deliberately and asserted: a tab with no imports gets no strip and no divider (the line would otherwise underline the whole tab), and Shape / Elements / Template are untouched. Verified at 375px with a screenshot — the divider falls exactly where he drew his red line.
- v8.60 — **The Media Import icon is white with a gradient (queue 270).** His words: *"keep it all looking the same. I just want the actual icon for the import button to be more white colour with a gradient instead of grey how it is right now."* Same cause as #267, so it went quickly: the arrow was grey because `ico()` strokes with `currentColor` and `.addmenu-card > .addmenu-ic` paints that from `--am-tint`, which for Import is the plain grey. A CSS rule therefore could never have whitened just the icon without taking the plate with it — the icon needed its own paint server, exactly as Sound effects has. **The plate is untouched**, which is the half he asked for twice: still `150, 160, 176`, the neutral grey #210 made a system, where Import and Import audio are the everyday action beside the things that MAKE something. The test asserts the plate is *still* a neutral grey as well as the icon being white, because a fix that whitened the whole card would satisfy the icon check and quietly break that system — and it checks **every** stroke, since one path left on `currentColor` would leave the arrow half grey, which is worse than all grey. Verified at 375px: a white arrow on the grey plate, Sample clip still pink and AI Scene still yellow beside it. *(Left alone deliberately: "Import audio" on the Audio tab is the same grey button with the same grey arrow, and #210 made those two a matching pair — whitening only one splits it, so that one waits on his word.)*
- v8.61 — **The Elements palette stops being random (queue 271).** His words: *"the colour choices are kind of poor and they kind of just like random and also similar to a lot of them. Null being red is good and you could make the custom elements button be multicoloured."* **"Random" was literally true:** the tab drew from an **eight**-hue list **cycled by index** across **nine** cards, so a button's colour came from where it sat rather than what it does — and with 8 hues over 9 cards the ninth repeated the first exactly, because the list ran out. The list also held three blues (cyan, azure, indigo), two greens and two pinks, which is the "similar to a lot of them" precisely. Hues are assigned **by name** now, the way #210 already established, and picked to sit apart on the wheel: red 0° · amber 37° · lime 100° · mint 165° · azure 211° · violet 255° · magenta 312°. Where a card's artwork already carries a colour the plate agrees with it — the pen green, the vector path amber, the camera violet — so the tab reads as chosen rather than assorted. **Measured, since "more distinct" is a claim:** the closest pair among the vivid cards is **76.5** apart against **34.1** before. **Null keeps exactly the colour it had**, pinned rather than re-picked because he said it was good. **Custom elements is multicoloured** — four marks, four hues, carried by its own paint servers because a CSS gradient cannot paint a stroke (the same wall as #267 and #270). The scheme he said to keep — vivid icon, faint plate — is untouched. *(Empty group and Custom elements sit close on purpose as the quiet pair at ~20% saturation, told apart by their icons; they were briefly identical, which is the same complaint in miniature, so they are now a steel and a lilac-grey.)* The test asserts the qualities rather than the hues — no two tints identical, vivid ones 60 apart, Null red, Custom multicoloured — so a later tune will not fail it, and it would have caught the original defect.
- v8.62 — **The phone's notepad button gets the shading the desktop one has had for ages (queue 272).** His words: *"I still want subtle shading to the notepad button just so it looks a little bit more detailed just like some subtle shading is all I need. Nothing crazy."* **The "still" was the whole diagnosis.** Queue 225 asked for exactly this and it was built — but only the **desktop** icon got it. He uses a phone, where the notepad has been the flat version the entire time, so from where he was looking nothing had changed and he was right to raise it twice. The phone icon now carries the identical treatment, copied rather than re-invented: a soft top-to-bottom gradient down the sheet, and one faint line under the top edge where a real page catches its own curl. Both are drawn **inside** the page path, so the silhouette at 24px is unchanged — the mark is the same, it just stops being flat, which is what "nothing crazy" asks for. **The test asserts the two icons MATCH**, not merely that each is shaded — one having it and the other not is exactly how this came back a second time — and that their gradient ids **differ**, since both icons live in one document and a duplicate id silently hands the paint to whichever element asked for it second.
- v8.63 — **The loading pill stops covering the + button (queue 273).** His words: *"make sure that the bar that reads out the name of the thing … doesn't go to cover over the plus create button."* Reproduced first, with a filename he would really have. The pill is pinned bottom-**left** with no right-hand limit and it **names** the clip, so its width is simply the length of a filename: measured at 375px with *"Invincible (2023) - Thragg Teaser (S04) - 1080p YT WEB-DL AV1.mp4"*, it ran the full **363px to the screen edge and lay 80px across the + button**, overlapping vertically too. With a short name it never came close, which is why this only appeared on a real import. **The cap is arithmetic rather than a guess:** the + is 64px wide sitting 16px off the right edge, so it owns the last 80px, and the pill is capped at `100vw - 100px` — clear by 8px. **The name ellipsises rather than the pill growing**, because a truncated filename still says which clip is loading, and that is the whole reason it is named (queue 201: *"Loading" on its own does not tell you WHICH of your clips is the one you cannot see*), whereas a bar across the button you are reaching for helps with nothing. Measured after at every length: a short name is 117px wide and 166px clear, a real one caps at 275px and 8px clear, a 308-character name is identical. A short label still hugs its text — asserted separately, so the cap cannot quietly become a fixed width.
- v8.64 — **A "Tutorials" button at the foot of the shortcuts sheet (queue 274).** His words: *"At the bottom of the keyboard shortcuts menu when you press the ? Icon it should show a button that takes you straight to the tutorial section."* That sheet is where someone lands when they are stuck, and all it offered was a list of keys and a Close. It reuses the home tab **button** rather than reaching into home's state — that click handler is the one thing that switches tab and re-renders, so this takes the same route a finger does and there is no second path to keep in step. **Two things nearly shipped broken.** First, it rendered **invisible while looking perfect in the stylesheet**: `theme-glass.css` restates `.shortcuts-card .btn { background: #0c1c25 }` at equal specificity in a *later* file, so it won the tie — the button kept the panel background and took the dark text meant to sit on accent, measuring **1.05:1** contrast. The selector is `.btn.shortcuts-tut` now (two classes on one element), which beats it on specificity rather than load order; contrast is **8.55:1** after. Second, the deferred tab-click used `requestAnimationFrame`, **which does not fire in a hidden tab** — home opened on Projects and the button silently did nothing. It is a timer now; the suite already records that exact trap for its own waits. Also: `.shortcuts-card .btn` was written for one full-width Close pinned to the bottom of a scrolling card, so two buttons in that rule overlapped by 38px — the row is the sticky bar now and the buttons inside it are ordinary. The test asserts the button is **readable**, not merely styled, because "it has a background colour" and "you can see it" are different claims.
- v8.65 — **The mobile shapes menu no longer scrolls up and down (queue 275).** His words: *"On mobile the shapes menu has like a thing where you can scroll up and down but it shouldn't have that because on mobile it should all just fit on the screen nice and you shouldn't need to scroll up and down. I don't even know why that happens."* Two overflows stacked, neither of them the grid being too big for the phone. (1) The sheet body states a height and the pager took `height: 100%` of it, so the page-dots row — 6px plus a 9px margin — was pushed past the bottom: exactly the 15px of scroll measured on Shape. A flex column now shares the stated height between the pager and the dots. (2) With the dots given their space, a 667px phone leaves a 198px pager and the grid's fixed **64px row floor** needs 208 for three rows — another 10px. The floor is now `clamp(50px, 8.5vh, 64px)`, so tall phones are pixel-identical and short ones shrink the tiles instead of scrolling. The PC path never hit either, because its fit solver reserves the dots and re-plans the grid — and that solver is desktop-only, so on a phone nothing ever did. Verified at 390×844, 380×800 and 375×667: every tab reports zero overflow on both the body and the pager.
- v8.66 — **The two people shapes have arms (queue 160).** His words: *"The two people shapes are good but need arms, make sure when adding arms you get other agents to verify if it's any good or not."* Three earlier attempts were thrown away, and the reason turned out to be the ruler rather than the drawing: the note on the entry said 24px was "the size the add menu actually uses", so every version was judged there. The tile draws an **SVG**, and an SVG renders at device resolution — measured in the running app it is 34 CSS px, so the figure resolves to **51 device pixels**, and about 76 on a DPR-3 phone. An armpit that is 1px at 18 is 3px at 51, which is the difference between a smudge and a drawing. Each arm is its own polygon unioned by nonzero fill, never spliced into the body path — the splice is what turned her dress into a box last time. Its top edge is a diagonal from the shoulder corner out to the arm, so the shoulder SLOPES into the arm instead of capping it; the wrist is built from the leg's own two arcs at the same radius, so the hand and the foot are the same terminal; the armpit is 0.055 of figure height, the leg-gap number, held constant so it cannot pinch shut on the way down. **Her hem widened from 0.205 to 0.258** — necessary, not cosmetic: with the arms as the widest thing on both figures the pair converged into one silhouette at picker size, and putting her skirt back outside the arms is what keeps them apart. Nine independent judges across three rounds; the last three signed off 3/3 after rejecting the first two rounds outright. Measured on the shipped shapes: armpit 3px at 51 and 8px at 128, his widest point at 0.33 of his height (his arms) against hers at 0.60 (her hem).
- v8.67 — **The add-menu drag grows upward only, and its handle is on the panel instead of the top of the screen (queue 244, reopened).** His words: *"i jsut figured out how to do ur version of the add menu dragging up and down and this is dogshit and not what i wanted at all, its completely broken. It shouldnt take up the whole screen only go up"*, and *"the button to do it shouldnt be at the top of the screen"*. Both real, both geometry, and the gesture logic was fine. The floating panel was `left: 0; right: var(--insp-w, 0)` with **`--insp-w` defined nowhere**, so it resolved to `right: 0` and spanned the whole window the moment it left the grid — swallowing the timeline. It is pinned to its own column now (left/width/bottom measured from its grid box at pointerdown and held), using `fixed` so those viewport numbers mean what they measured. And the handle was `position: absolute` inside a panel that has no `position`, so it resolved against the page: measured at **y:0, 1280×10**, a strip across the top of the window — which is also why he could not find it for weeks. Measured through a real drag: left 0, width 307 and bottom 800 unchanged at every step while the top rose 560 → 304, and the timeline's box identical throughout. The ceiling also dropped from 82% of the window to 62% so a third of the screen stays canvas — flagged in the entry as a judgement call he can overrule. The old test passed through both faults because it only ever measured HEIGHT; the new one measures the three edges that are meant to be nailed down. **And the fifth behaviour, left unbuilt at v8.30, is in:** dragging the TIMELINE up into a raised add menu now holds at its level, flashes the same divider, and re-couples past the same 9px — measured at 545 → 465 → 409 → held at 405 → both at 345. Half of it needed no code at all (connected, they are one grid row), and the design note's warning about a second `--tl-h` writer is respected: the timeline's handle only asks the menu to stop floating.
- v8.68 — **The effects browser is a phone sheet you can multi-select in (queue 277 (partial) — stage A of ten clauses).** His words: *"the menu won't cover the whole screen the menu will only go up until where the canvas is"* — and, correcting himself later in the same message, *"it covers the play buttons and all of that so it goes right up to the canvas"*; and *"when you tap on an effect it doesn't just add it selects it… you can select as many effects as you want… every time you select one it'll put a one or two on it so you know what order they are being added in"*. On a phone the browser now opens as a sheet whose top edge is the canvas's own bottom — measured on open, because that edge moves with the project's aspect — covering the transport row and timeline while the canvas stays watchable. A tap SELECTS instead of adding, badged with the order it will be added in; re-tapping removes and renumbers the rest; a bar at the foot says "Add 3 effects" and commits them in that order. The full-screen title bar is gone on the sheet, which is what he scribbled out on his screenshot. Desktop is deliberately untouched and separately asserted — one tap, one effect, closes — because he said *"all just for mobile btw"* and a multi-select leaking onto PC would make every first tap there do nothing. Clauses 5 (live preview), 7 (loop only the selected layer) and 9 (the naked/preview-values toggle) are the next stage and are ticked off honestly in REQUESTS.md rather than claimed here.
- v8.69 — **The effects sheet previews what you pick, on just that layer, looping (queue 277 (partial) — stage B, clauses 5 and 7).** His words: *"when you tap on an effect it doesn't just add it selects it and it will show the layer selected like what the layer will look like with the effect selected"*, and *"it'll just take you back to the start of that layer instead and it will only show that layer which makes sense because you're only seeing the effects for that layer anyways"*. Both are view-only overrides — the existing `FM.isolate` hides the other layers and a new `FM._fxPreview` carries the picked stack — so nothing is written to the layer, a preview can never reach history, autosave or an export, and closing the sheet is the whole undo. The stack goes in through `effectiveFx()`, already the single answer to "which effects does this layer render with at time t", so the preview cannot disagree with the real thing about ordering. The canvas loops that layer at 24fps and restarts on every tap. **Two bugs caught by looking at it rather than reading it:** the isolate restore was guarded on a null that is null in the ordinary case, so closing the sheet left every other layer invisible; and `close()` repainted before hiding, which throws on a media layer whose element is not ready and left the browser stuck on screen over the phone's settings button. Hiding happens first now, and the repaint is guarded.
- v8.70 — **A toggle for how the picked effects land (queue 277 (partial) — stage C, clause 9).** His words: *"Maybe add a button like a toggle button for when you're like done selecting all of the effects you want to add you can toggle whether they spawn in with the values that were displayed in the preview on the canvas or they are just added in as a fresh slate like they just added in naked so you can do what you want to them."* A pill on the commit bar, remembered between sessions, reading "Keep preview values" or "Add naked". Said plainly because it matters: the two settings land the same parameters in ordinary use today, since a previewed effect is built exactly the way a fresh one is and is therefore already naked — the difference only starts to bite once effects preview with something other than their defaults, which is the work he explicitly asked not to start. So it is wired to the real preview objects rather than faked, and the suite proves the wiring by changing a previewed value and checking which setting carries it through. Nine of his ten clauses for the effects rework are now done; clause 10 was an idea rather than a requirement and half of it (hold an effect for its presets) already existed.
- v8.71 — **The darkened bin group sits centred on the transport row (queue 278).** His words: *"on pc the bar with the bin on it and stuff, the darkening of the background to sginfiy that theyre seperate and not default functions is still off centred, the bar gos lower than it gos higher, and it looks off"*. "Still" earned a measurement rather than another guess, and it was two pixels from two causes. The row is a grid, and a grid lays its row track from the top of the content box — 40px row, 4px padding, so a 32px box holding 34px buttons, leaving the track hanging 2px past the bottom (4 above the pill, 2 below). `align-items: center` could not fix that because it centres items *inside* the track, and the track was what sat low; `align-content: center` centres the track itself. That left 3 and 2, because the row's `border-bottom` lives inside its 40px — so the hairline is an inset box-shadow now, same line, no layout height. Measured after: 3.0px above, 3.0px below, with play and the rest of the row unmoved.
- v8.72 — **The layer menu keeps the add menu's background (queue 279).** His words: *"keep the background of the add menu for the menu when u press on a layer"*. One selector: the gradient was written as `#inspector-panel:has(.addmenu--panel)`, so the panel wore that surface while nothing was selected and dropped to a flat fill the moment you picked a layer — the same panel changing material depending on what it happened to be showing. Without the gate it is the panel's surface, and the add menu and the layer menu read as one place. Desktop-only, as the gradient always was. The test asserts the two states paint the same thing rather than a hard-coded colour, so restyling it later cannot fail for the wrong reason.
- v8.73 — **The layer-actions button is greyed out when nothing is selected (queue 280).** His words, against a screenshot with it circled: *"make this button greyed out when a layer isnt selected"*. It opens the actions for the selected layer, so with no selection there was nothing for it to open and it looked exactly as live as every control beside it. Dimmed rather than hidden on purpose: the buttons in the group next to it do vanish, but they sit in a wrapper with its own ground that leaves with them, while this one is in the plain transport row where a hole would shuffle its neighbours sideways every time you deselected. It uses `is-off`, the app's existing word for a dead control (undo and redo already wear it), and that rule now names all three in one place so the next one needs no CSS change. The test checks the class AND the rendered opacity — a class no stylesheet acts on would satisfy the first and change nothing on screen — and that the state comes back when you deselect again.
- v8.74 — **The Custom elements card no longer counts itself (queue 281).** His words: *"get rid of the number from the custom elements section that says how many elements you have"*. It read "Custom elements (3)"; it reads "Custom elements". One knock-on went with it: the card's tint lookup carried a special case *because* of the count — the label renamed itself as you saved elements, so an exact match silently dropped it back to the index palette — and with a stable label that fallback is deleted rather than left lying around. Worth recording about the test rather than the change: the first version of it passed while the count was still there, because the suite's element library is empty and the number only appeared once you had saved one; only the mutation check caught that, so it now stubs a three-element library and tests the case the bug actually lived in.
- v8.75 — **The film grain moves faster and sits fainter (queue 282).** His words: *"Film grain background needs to move faster and be more subtle brw"*. Cross-fade .36s → .22s, offset steps .5s/.62s → .31s/.38s, strength .034 → .022. Both moved together deliberately: faster grain reads as stronger at the same alpha, because there is more change per second for the eye to catch, so speeding it up without softening it would have gone the wrong way. The two layers stay incommensurate (.31 against .38, as .5 against .62 was) — that is what stops them falling into step and re-creating the A→B flicker v7.95 existed to kill, and the suite asserts it now, since it is exactly the sort of invariant that gets tidied into a single number. One thing worth knowing for next time, learned by changing it and measuring no difference: the shared card/grain rule higher up the stylesheet declares a duration for these same pseudo-elements, and `#hm-grain` overrides it — the live numbers are the ones in its own rule, which now says so.
- v8.76 — **The selection outline stays on its layer when you drag the timeline (queue 284).** His words: *"when you drag the timeline up and down while having a layer selected the layers outline starts moving somewhere else, when you press back on a layer it goes back to normal but this shouldnt be happening"*. Reproduced by driving the real gesture at 1280×800: the preview went from 197px wide to 300 and the outline kept its old screen box, landing at 0.33 across the frame instead of centred on the layer. The gizmo lays out in screen pixels against the preview, and dragging a resizer changes that box without the WINDOW resizing, so canvas-edit's `resize` listener never heard about it. A fix for this was written and reverted once before because a ResizeObserver would not fire in the browser available — still true, measured again: an observer on `#preview` logged zero callbacks while the canvas changed size. So the resizers now tell the gizmo directly and the observer is only a belt for browsers where it works. One structural note: the first version had both paths calling `update()` independently and the mutation check reported the new test as dead — two redundant paths mean removing either changes nothing — so both go through one `stageResized` door, and with that door shut the outline drifts and the test fails, as it should. The related zoomed-preview aspect bug named in the same entry is NOT fixed and stays open.
- v8.77 — **The layer buttons shrink with the timeline band (queue 285).** His words: *"the layer edit pannels buttons should shrink if you make the timeline too small, so the button all stay fitting on screen"*. They were a fixed 101px tall, so shortening the band pushed the bottom row under the fold: 74px of scroll at a 300px band, 134 at 240, 194 at 180. They now slide 101 → 40 with the band, icon, label and gaps included, and there is **zero scroll from 240px upward** — including the default 264, which used to scroll. Sized off `--tl-h` rather than a breakpoint, because the band is draggable and any breakpoint is wrong on one side of itself. The first pass is the lesson: scaling padding and the icon made the tiles smaller and still left ~50px of scroll at every height, because the three rows share the panel with a 33px title, a 16px quick-row and ~20px of gaps — "make it smaller" was not the requirement, "make the sum fit" was, so the row height is arithmetic now. Honest limit: below about a 200px band the 40px floor bites and it scrolls the last few pixels rather than shrinking the tiles past being tappable.
- v8.78 — **The add / layer panel reacts to the cursor (queue 286).** His words: *"on the add menu and layer edit menu on pc a glow should follow ur curser… its not just a simple glow on ur curser but it makes it feel like the area around ur curser knows its there and is reacting to it"*. That distinction decided the build: nothing is drawn at the cursor. Every card's border lights on the side the cursor is near and the panel carries a wide faint wash beneath, so the light belongs to the surface. With the pointer beside "Speed", that card's ring is brightest, its neighbours catch it, and the far corners stay dark. The add menu and the layer menu are the same element, so it lands on both. Cheap by construction: the gradients are `background-attachment: fixed`, so they resolve against the viewport and every card samples ONE screen-space spotlight from the same two custom properties — no per-card coordinates, so no `getBoundingClientRect` per card per frame. Two property writes, gated to one per 16ms, and it stops entirely during playback; off under `prefers-reduced-motion`, desktop-only, and a touch pointer never arms it. Recorded because it nearly repeated the queue-284 mistake: `requestAnimationFrame` was the obvious throttle and it fires in neither browser here, so both probes reported a dead glow for working code — and CSS transitions do not tick there either, so the test reads with transitions disabled. This is also the first bit of visual identity in the app that was not modelled on Alight Motion; BEFORE-PUBLISHING.md now says so.
- v8.79 — **"Move & Transform" is "Position / Scale", and every other title's & is a / (queue 287).** His words: *"Change the name of move and transform to position / Scale. And any other title like background and opacity that has the & in it, replace the & with a /"*. Swept rather than fixing the two he named: Border / Shadow, Blending / Opacity, Distortion / Warp, Drawing / Edge, Retro / Analogue, Light / Glow, EQ / Filter, Space / Stereo, Recents / faves, Shortcuts / tips, Mouse / stage. The back button into that panel, the depth-of-field hint that points at it and the motion-blur toast that names it were updated too — a rename that leaves the app calling it the old name in three places is half a rename. Three are deliberately left and flagged in the entry for him to overrule: **Teal & Orange**, **Bass & Treble** and **Highlights & Shadows** are the names of an effect and two filters rather than section headings, and "Teal / Orange" reads as two things instead of the grade it is. Prose keeps its ampersands, since a slash mid-sentence is a grammar error rather than a rename. The existing card-order test carries the new names as its spec, so it is the guard.
- v8.80 — **The "Colour & Light" effects menu is just "Colouring" (queue 288).** His words: *"Change colour and light effect menu to just Colouring"*. It also settles a question that had sat at the top of REQUESTS.md for weeks — that the category was spelled the American way and it was his app's voice to decide — because the rename removes the American spelling and the ampersand together. Both standing notes at the top of that file are struck now: this one, and the reverted stage-resize hook, which shipped as #284 in v8.76. The comments in the compositor and the thumbnail engine that named the category were updated with it, since a rename that leaves half the codebase calling it something else is how someone later goes looking for a menu that no longer exists. Guarded by a new sweep test that walks the effects, filter and audio-effect category tables through their public accessors and fails on any ampersand in a title — so the next category added with one fails before it ships. Its first version looked for a table that does not exist and therefore checked nothing; the mutation check is what caught that.
- v8.81 — **The "Other" effects menu is gone (queue 289).** His words: *"Just put the effects from the other menu into menus that would fit them and get rid of the other menu"*. It held three things and only two were ever visible: **Channel Remap → Colouring** (every mode it has is a colour operation — Swap R/B, Hue Invert, Swap Sat/Val), **Fill Behind → Blur** (a heavy blur is the effect, and "blurred background" is what you would go looking under), and the Filter container, which is `hidden: true` and never appeared in the browser, parked in Stylize so its key resolves. All 196 effects stay reachable and none was orphaned. The real trap was not the moving but the FALLBACK: an effect with no category entry fell back to `'other'`, and with that category deleted it would have pointed at a key with no label — and since the browser lists only categories that have effects, any future effect added without one would have silently appeared nowhere at all. It falls back to a real category now, and the suite fails if any effect points at a category the browser does not list.
- v8.82 — **The signature edge-light now runs round the sound-effects and voice-record sheets (queue 291).** His words: *"Also put the our signature white line glow that move around the edges in the menu for sound effects and the menu for recording ur voice"*. "Our signature" is the requirement, so it is the same ring the open project card and the selected add-menu tab already wear — that CSS block says in as many words that it is "ONE implementation, two hosts", and this makes it four by extending the same selectors. Same keyframes, same 3.8s turn, same mask, and the suite asserts sameness by name rather than checking "is there a glow", because a lookalike second animation is the exact drift the original note warns about. One thing had to differ: the existing hosts are cards, so a spinning square 140% of their width covers them, but the sound-effects sheet is 344×688 and that square stopped ~200px short of its top and bottom — the light would have run down the sides and vanished at the ends. Sized on both axes for these two hosts instead. The test for that is pinned to a 344×700 stand-in, because the first version measured the live sheet, whose height follows the window, and passed with the sizing rule deleted. The recorder is checked through its stylesheet rather than by opening it, since `voiceRec.open()` asks for the microphone and those tests are already the flaky ones.
- v8.83 — **The Classic layout is deleted, not just unused (queue 293).** From his most urgent report: *"if someone sees it in the final version they may be able to use this against us"* — and CSS in a no-build app is plain text anyone can read, so a rule that never matches is still shipped and still legible. 45 selectors lost their `layout-studio` class (`body.layout-studio X` → `body X`, so specificity fell by exactly one class everywhere rather than unpredictably), and the Classic layout itself — the `50px minmax(0,1fr) var(--tl-h)` grid and the four placements that put the inspector down the right-hand side — is gone. Done in the order recorded in the entry, and the safety step was proving the class inert BEFORE removing it: with the Classic rules deleted, `#app` and all four regions measured byte-identical with and without it. Only then did it come off the markup, settings.js and app.js. Three tests written against the class rather than the layout were re-based (the text-editor docking test now asks whether the inspector band is big enough to dock into, which is the real condition), and the two queue-292 guards retired together exactly as their own comments instructed — replaced by a stronger one that asserts there is no old layout at all, reading the shipped stylesheet rather than the DOM. Verified at 1280×800 and 380×800: both layouts unchanged, and the body now carries no layout class.
- v8.84 — **The + button is gone; there is an Add layer on the timeline instead (queue 294 (partial) — stage A).** His words: *"the plus button inside the creator menu is kind of on the nose like… it looks very similar to what a light motion has and I was thinking of a way to get rid of it from being there while also keeping functionality… it could be a layer now… if you just tap anywhere on that line it'll open up the same admin"*. This is an identity request as much as a feature one — the second thing he has asked for to stop the app resembling Alight Motion, and the first that REMOVES a copied element rather than adding an original one. The row takes the orb's own teal light rather than a second visual language, sits in the timeline, and opens the same sheet. It says *"Tap here to start creating"* in an empty project and *"Tap to add a layer"* once there are layers — and it replaces the old "No layers yet…" sentence rather than sitting under it, because two explanations of the same thing is precisely what he told me to stop doing (*"you just, like, put an explanation for everything in every section… just don't overexplain it"*). That instruction is now a rule the suite enforces: either label over 34 characters fails. The row is drawn, never stored, so it cannot reach the export, the layer count or a saved project. Two tests that measured the + orb were re-based rather than left red. **Still to come, and it is the best part of his idea:** dragging the row to choose where new layers land.
- v8.85 — **Drag the Add layer, and what you add lands below it (queue 294 (partial) — stage B, clauses 3, 5 and 6).** His words for why this is the whole idea: *"you can move this layer up and down… and then when you press the button and add a layer out of shape, it'll actually add it where you have that so instead of just automatically adding whatever you add to the top and then drag it down you can drag that first and then add stuff and when you do add something it'll just go below the add one"*. Measured end to end: layers stacked C,B,A, row dragged to third place, add → **C,B,NEW,A** with the row still sitting above NEW. It is one number (`FM.addAt`) and one changed line — `FM.insertLayer` splices there instead of unshifting — which is why it works for shapes, text, media, captions and anything added later rather than for one add path. Edit Group is exempt deliberately: inside a group the rows on screen are a subtree, so a flat index means nothing and that path keeps the old top-insert; asserted, because getting it wrong would put a layer added while editing a group outside the group. The ≡ grip drags and the row taps — a grip that also fired the row's tap would open the add sheet every time you tried to move it. And one thing found by watching rather than reading: the row is rebuilt on every boundary the finger crosses, so the grabbed element is gone by the second move; the listeners live on `window` and the dragged look is re-applied to its replacement, or it moved correctly and never looked grabbed.
- v8.86 — **Paste lands at the Add layer too (queue 294 (partial) — clause 11).** His words: *"when you copy and paste stuff it could go there like I would go to wear that liners"* — garbled dictation, but the sentence before it settles the meaning: the Add row marks where things go, and paste is one of the things. One line, once yesterday's insertion index existed: `pasteClipboard`'s default index is now the row's rather than a hard 0, so paste and add agree. An explicit index still wins, which is what the ⧉ Paste-Layer split-button's arrow passes, so choosing a position by hand is untouched — and Edit Group falls back to the top for the same reason inserts do, because a flat index means nothing inside a subtree. Also corrected a note I had left myself: the site I had recorded as the paste path was actually duplicate, which inserts beside the original and is right to.
- v8.87 — **On PC the Add marker is a thin line between layers (queue 294 (partial) — stage C, clauses 7–10).** His words: *"instead of being like an actual full layer and like taking up all that face on the timeline instead it could just be a line between layers to signify where you're going to add… it would be a very thin but still noticeable line but when you hoover over it it will let go a bit bigger to signify click on it and then you can drag it up and down"*. Same marker and same index as the phone's Add layer — only the skin differs: 7px at rest, 24px with its label when hovered or dragged. One element serves both gestures, told apart by whether the pointer travelled 4px rather than by a timer, and a drag swallows the click — asserted, because without it repositioning the line would deselect every time. Clicking it "opens the add menu", which on PC means a deselect: the add menu IS the inspector band whenever nothing is selected. **One real breakage the suite caught:** the marker carried the `.track-row` class, harmless while it was phone-only, and the moment it appeared on desktop a grouping test counting rows found one more than there were layers. It was right — everything that walks the timeline asks for `.track-row` and this has no layer, no clip and no index — so it brings its own layout now, which also spared every other test from learning to filter it out. It is hidden inside Edit Group, where the insertion index deliberately does not apply.
- v8.88 — **⇧+Home / ⇧+End fling the Add marker to the top or bottom — and that completes queue 294, all fifteen clauses.** His words: *"we could add shortcuts on the keyboard to quickly push it up to the top… push it up to the bottom so that you don't have to go and find it and then drag it up or down"*. Paired with the plain keys deliberately: Home/End already jump the playhead to the start and end, so the shifted pair means the same for the marker — one idea rather than two arbitrary keys — and the suite asserts neither leaks into the other. Listed in the shortcuts sheet, and checked to be, because a shortcut nobody can discover is a shortcut for the person who wrote it. **The whole request now stands:** the + button that he said was "kind of on the nose… very similar to what a light motion has" is gone; a glowing Add layer sits on the phone timeline and a thin Add line on the desktop one; both drag; what you add or paste lands below the marker; the label changes with the project and is capped at one short line. Not included, because he asked for it not to be: making effects spawn with values that already look like something.
- v8.89 — **Thirteen more sound effects, and the menu is warm (queue 290).** His words: *"give the sound effects menu more sound effects and also give the menu some nice warm background colours, Specifcally warm"* — and he asked for it *"when you have time after everything else"*, which is now, because every other numbered item is finished or waiting on him. Sixteen became twenty-nine: Pass by, Slide up, Punch, Glass break, Reverse swell, Heartbeat, Success, Error, Swipe and Vinyl crackle join the existing headings, plus a new **Nature** one — Wind, Rain, Fire crackle — since those were the set's obvious gap and filing them under Texture would have buried them with the glitch and static. All synthesised like the rest; no file ships. Every one of the 29 was rendered through the app's own path to check: none failed, none silent, none too quiet. The suite renders a sample rather than all of them, and *fire* was added to that sample on purpose as the most intricate of the new ones. The warmth resolves the tension logged with the request — the button that opens this sheet is the rainbow one — by being **light rather than paint**: two soft amber pools thrown from the corners over the app's own panel colour, a warm hairline and a peach title. The test checks the tint is warm AND stays under 0.35 opacity, so a later "make it warmer" cannot quietly turn it into paint.
- v8.90 — **A zoomed preview no longer renders stretched after the stage changes size (BUG-HUNT backlog).** The entry queue 284 named as "the same root cause, so fix both together rather than twice" — and which I left open when I shipped 284. Above 1.35x the canvas wrap is pinned to hard pixels so overlays keep a comp-sized rectangle, and that inline size overrides its own `aspect-ratio`, so it cannot self-correct; nothing re-ran the measure when the stage changed, because there is no window-resize hook for it anywhere in the codebase. Recorded symptom: a 1:1 circle rendering as a 2.13x-wide ellipse, and even unzoomed a stale backing store painting 961x1709 where 367x653 was correct — 6.8x the pixels every frame, which on a phone after a rotation is pure wasted GPU. **This had been attempted and reverted twice**, both times because it was written around a ResizeObserver that fires in neither browser available here. It goes through the door queue 284 built instead: the resizers call the re-measure directly, plus a window-resize hook for rotation, and the debounce key now includes the stage box or a pure resize early-returns with an unchanged crop and zoom. Both halves are mutation-checked, and the test drives the real splitter drag and asserts the pinned box actually moved — an unzoomed preview keeps its aspect for free, so checking aspect alone would have passed with the hook deleted.
- v8.91 — **The intermittent suite failure is identified and fixed (BUG-HUNT backlog).** The file had carried it for weeks as "one desktop run in ~4 comes back 230/231, and the failing test is not yet identified", and it cost three ship-blocking re-runs in a single night. It is the voice-recorder test rig: `vrFakeMic` built a fresh `AudioContext` for every fake mic and closed it again on dispose, so under a loaded run the next context had not produced its first audio quantum inside the 4s budget — the tone never flowed, `MediaRecorder` captured an empty take, and the test correctly reported a rejected mic. The rig now uses one shared context for the whole run and never closes it: the cap that closing was written for is on LIVE contexts and there is only ever one now, while the tone and its tracks are still stopped per mic, which is what actually releases the microphone the test is about. Verified the only way a flake can be — **four consecutive green suites** — rather than by one green run.
- v8.92 — **Trimming a reversed clip now edits the end you actually grabbed (BUG-HUNT backlog).** A reversed clip is played as `trimStart + (duration*sp - adv)`, so its FIRST frame is the source window's END and its LAST is the window's START — and the trim grips had no reversed branch at all, so every edit landed on the opposite end. The documented repro: a 10s source reversed into a 5s clip, head dragged 2s right; the user cuts 2s off the head and instead loses 2s off the TAIL, with source 2s→0s silently discarded and every remaining frame shifted. Worse on the other grip, where a reversed clip with `trimStart` at 0 could never have its head extended at all even with the whole source unused. Fixed in both sites the entry named — `applyTrimAt` and `FM.extendClipTo` — with the same branch `splitLayer` and the inspector already had; this function was the odd one out, not a new idea. Measured after: the repro gives window [0,3] showing source 3s at the new head and 0s at the end, which is exactly what the inspector's "Trim start to playhead" button already produced on the identical clip, so the two UI paths agree again. A forward-clip control test is mutation-checked separately, because adding a branch is only safe if the path everyone actually uses is untouched.
- v8.93 — **The motion tracker no longer templates the wrong part of a cropped clip (BUG-HUNT backlog).** The seed arrives in CROP-BOX space — `geom()` sizes the layer with `FM.layerSize`, which returns the crop box for a cropped clip — while the frame cache is scaled from the FULL source, and the crop origin was never added. The entry's worked example: a 1080x1920 clip cropped to y=600, tapping the centre gives seed (540,540) whose true source pixel is (540,1140), so the tracker templated a point **600px away**, locked onto whatever was there, and replaced the layer's x/y keyframes with a track of it. It failed silently, because the seed box drawn on screen uses the exact inverse transform and lands correctly on the tapped feature — so nothing looked wrong until the clip drifted. The origin is added on the way into cache space and taken off on the way out, and the two conversions are now a single exported factory so the suite can assert they are inverses without building a frame cache and running a real track. Both directions mutation-checked, plus a control that an uncropped layer maps straight through as before.
- v8.94 — **A motion track no longer allocates its way to an OOM-kill on a phone (BUG-HUNT backlog).** Three things, all in the tracking path. The grayscale conversion of every frame it touched was memoised into a map that nothing ever evicted, each entry the same byte count as the ImageBitmap it came from — so peak memory was ~2x the frame-cache budget, measured at ~720MB for a 60s clip on a device whose real budget is 96–128MB. It bought nothing: the two walks visit disjoint, monotonically ordered ranges, so the only repeat read is the immediately-previous index, which one slot serves. The byte budget itself floored at 96MB and ceilinged at 360MB on **every** device, ignoring `FM.frameCacheLimits()` — which exists because of the v4.70 OOM fix and caps a 2GB phone at 128MB — so it now goes through it. And the frame cache is handed back when the track finishes, unless the layer is reversed or frame-blended and genuinely plays from one; before, a track left its frames resident for the rest of the session and later reverse-cache builds short-circuited on a cache built at tracking resolution. The budget test stubs a phone-sized cap deliberately: run against this machine's own limit the broken version passes, because a desktop allows 384MB and the old hard ceiling was 360 — the mutation check caught that first version.
- v8.95 — **Saving an effect as a preset no longer flattens the motion you authored (BUG-HUNT backlog).** The preset validator whitelisted four easing names — linear, easeIn, easeOut, easeInOut — and rewrote anything else to `linear`. The app has nine: `FM.EASES` adds bounce, elastic and hold, and `FM.EASE_PRESETS` adds overshoot and anticipate. The graph editor writes those names BARE (it sets `kf.e` and deletes `kf.bez`), so once the name was rewritten there was no bezier left to fall back on and the curve was gone for good — re-applied on every read, with no warning. The whitelist is derived from the live tables now rather than hard-coding a list that the easing menu had already outgrown, with a fallback to the old four if the tables are somehow absent, because an empty whitelist would rewrite every easing in the app and be worse than the bug. Verified with the entry's own repro: a blur radius keyed [0, 40 bounce, 5 hold, 20 overshoot] now round-trips to 40 at t=2.5 (the hold holding) and 21.31 at t=3.5 (the overshoot) — identical to the original, where the preset used to give 22.5 and 12.5. The test checks values as well as names, since a build that kept the name and lost the curve would pass a name check.
- v8.96 — **Demo mode now reaches the phone Add sheet (BUG-HUNT backlog).** Turning the setting on and going back to `+` still showed real filenames and the clips' own frames — "Holiday_Bali_2024.mp4" and its thumbnail — which is exactly the camera-roll exposure the setting exists to prevent, during the screen recording it was just turned on for. The sheet's Add menu is built once, at startup, and `demo()` is only read while a tile is being BUILT, so the setting could not reach tiles that already existed; it only started obeying once you happened to tap another tab, and nothing said so. Opening the sheet now redraws it first. Driven from `openAdd()` rather than a settings listener, because `render()` also runs for the PC inspector and a listener inside it would leak one per re-render — and the menu remembers its tab and page, so a redraw puts you back where you were. The test drives the report's exact sequence (open, close, turn it on, reopen) and asserts the thumbnail is gone as well as the name, since a frame identifies a holiday as well as its filename does.
- v8.97 — **Every radial effect finally has an aim point, and Lens Blur is finally a lens (EFFECTS-PLAN round 12).** Zoom Blur, Spin Blur, Zoom Streaks and Spin Streaks all had their centre welded to the middle of the frame, so you could not aim the rush at your subject or put the spin on the wheel that was actually turning; all four get Centre X/Y, plus a quality knob on the two blurs (their 9 fixed taps stepped into visible ghosts), a threshold on Zoom Streaks (every mid-tone used to smear, which is a grey haze over the shot rather than rays out of the light) and a trail falloff on Spin Streaks (0 gives a long comet tail, 2 a tight ghost). Lens Blur gets the one that matters: it averaged sRGB, so a specular's brightness was spread over 16 taps and divided by 16 and the highlight DISSOLVED — it was a slightly nicer Box Blur, and its own description ("bright points bloom into the aperture shape") was not true. Highlight bloom weights each tap by how bright it is, so a point of light dominates its own disc: peak luma on a blown specular goes 25 to 56. Plus a real aperture — Circle, Hexagon, Pentagon or Square, baked into the tap offsets so it costs nothing per pixel and verified as geometry (no tap escapes its polygon at full precision, with the circle as the control that does). Four things found on the way: a quality knob must not change the REACH (zoomblur's furthest tap is 8/9 of its span, not the span, so raising the tap count would have silently lengthened the streak); bloom was recomputing a whole-frame constant inside the tap loop, and hoisting it took 484ms to 348ms for identical output; a pentagon cannot be judged on the pixel grid, because the kernel's legacy truncation biases every tap the same way and only an even-sided aperture cancels it by symmetry; and the plan's proposed 64-tap maximum was rejected on measurement — 1217ms a frame against 622 at 32, for density nobody can see. Defaults are untouched and measured unchanged (170ms vs 169), so every existing project renders byte-for-byte as it did — and that is now a standing suite test rather than a one-off check against the previous commit.
- v8.98 — **The three keying effects stop being one setting each (EFFECTS-PLAN round 13).** Chroma Key was a binary cut — inside the tolerance the pixel vanished, one step outside it was untouched — so every keyed shot had a jagged aliased edge and the only control was how MUCH to cut, never how. There is no such thing as a real edge pixel under that rule, and a strand of hair or a motion-blurred hand is exactly that. Edge softness ramps the alpha across a band just outside the key. Luma Key could only ever remove the DARK end, so a subject shot against WHITE could not be keyed at all however you set the threshold — that is half the effect missing, and it now has a Removes: Dark / Bright switch; its soft edge was also welded to 28 luma units, which terminates a smoke or glow plate on a line, and is a slider now. Replace Colour's row in the plan is really a bug report: it only rotated hue and rebuilt saturation and brightness from the SOURCE pixel, so picking pale pink to replace a saturated red gave you a saturated pink. Full colour takes the target's saturation AND its brightness, scaled by how light each pixel is relative to the From colour, so the folds and shadows in the thing you recoloured survive rather than flattening. Two things worth knowing: both key functions memoise their output canvas and hand it back whenever their remembered inputs match, so a new control that is not in that cache key repaints nothing — correct maths, dead slider — and that is what the mutation check for it removes. And these two were the only effects in the app the suite could not reach at all, because they run on the media path ahead of the draw; they are on the same seam as every other render table now, and have tests for the first time. Deliberately not built: the proposed spill and colour-match controls for Chroma Key, because Chroma Key Pro already is those, and two half-answers are worse than one whole one. One thing the suite caught on the way out: featuring the two new keys in the carousel put two MEDIA-ONLY cards at the head of a row that does not filter by layer type, so on a shape or text layer the first two things you saw could only answer a tap with "That effect needs a video or image layer". Only Replace Colour is featured now, and the rule that nothing media-only or text-only may lead that row is a test rather than a comment.
- v8.99 — **Grid, Checker and Stripes stop being one look each (EFFECTS-PLAN round 14).** Grid's line weight was welded to 6% of its spacing, so the two numbers a grid is actually made of could never be set apart — ask for a wide 160px grid and you were given 10px bars with it, and there was no faint guide grid because the lines punched in at full opacity. It gets Line weight, Strength and Angle. Checker was welded to a 50/50 tint on perfect axis-aligned squares: no solid checker, no subtle one, no diagonal, no rectangles — now Strength, Cell shape and Angle. Stripes were locked to a single 45° diagonal at a 50% duty cycle mixed at a fixed 0.6, so the only thing you could change was how wide they were; they now run diagonally either way, across or down, with the bar-to-gap ratio and solidity as controls. Every default is the constant it replaced and that is asserted, not assumed — including for a freshly added instance, which carries every schema default stamped in and is the way this class of change usually breaks. One thing learned and written down: the catalogue-wide version of that check cannot be written. "Deleting a param must change nothing unless the schema says otherwise" looks like the general rule and flags 110 of 266 params, because for most effects the param is the effect's own amount, which absent quite properly means off.
- v9.00 — **The four print effects stop being one look each (EFFECTS-PLAN round 15).** Halftone Dots was locked to a 0° axis-aligned grid, which is the one angle a real print screen never uses — every actual halftone runs at 45° because a grid square with the frame beats against the subject and reads as moire rather than as ink. It gets Screen angle, Ink gain (coverage was a hardcoded 1.45) and a Dot shape of Round, Square or Diamond. Halftone Lines was permanently horizontal with 1-bit aliased edges: now an angle, an ink weight, and a softness that takes the jagged step off every line. Crosshatch fired its three hatch tiers at hardcoded brightnesses, so how much ink went down was decided entirely by how the shot happened to be exposed, and its 1px strokes are invisible at 1080p — Ink density, Stroke weight and Angle now. Pencil Sketch's Amount could only fade the drawing back toward the photo; Stroke darkness makes the lines bolder or lighter, Paper tooth gives the white some grain, and Clear smooth areas fixes a real defect — a Sobel across a smooth gradient still returns a small reading, so gentle ramps came out faintly smeared with no way to say "that is not a line". Measured: 14,400 of 14,400 pixels of a plain gradient came back smeared, and zero with the gate on. One thing caught on the way out, by the extremes sweep rather than by me: Halftone Lines' new ink weight could push its threshold past the pattern period, and once that happens every row is inked and the layer turns solid black — on a reduced preview plate only, so it would have blanked mid-playback while the export stayed perfectly fine. That is the same symptom as the v6.x halftone bug, reached through a new control, and the warning about it was in a comment three lines above the code I was editing.
- v9.01 — **Find Edges, Emboss and Bump Map stop being lit from one corner (EFFECTS-PLAN round 16).** Emboss's light was welded to the top-left and its kernel ran per channel, so you could not relight the relief, could not get a clean grey metal stamp instead of colour fringing, and could not emboss the picture rather than replace it with grey — all three are controls now. Bump Map had its key light nailed to a single vector and its depth to a constant, so every embossed surface in the app was lit from the same corner at the same depth; it gets Light angle, Relief depth and Ambient. Find Edges could only draw glowing white lines on black: it now inverts to ink on paper, gates the grey mush a Sobel invents across smooth areas, and mixes the lines back over the picture instead of replacing it. Two things worth keeping from building it. The emboss kernel GENERALISES exactly — its hardcoded weights turn out to be the dot product of each neighbour's offset with a light vector of (1,1), so the angle control turns that vector rather than swapping in a different effect, and lighting from the opposite side negates the relief at 7733 of 7733 pixels. And a correction to the plan's own notes: it flags Bump Map's default angle as needing a special case because computing it from the angle gives -0.5000000000000001 instead of -0.5. The arithmetic is right; the consequence is nil. A difference in the 16th decimal place cannot survive rounding to 8 bits — measured at zero differing bytes across three plate types and five strengths, and the mutation that deletes the special case correctly survives the suite. The branch stays because it is free, but the file now says not to budget effort for that class of thing again without measuring what actually reaches the screen.
- v9.02 — **The repeat effects stop making the same wall of clones (EFFECTS-PLAN round 17).** Grid Repeat had its rows welded to its columns, so it could only ever build a square grid, and every tile was an identical copy butt-joined to the next — which shows a hard seam at every join. It now takes rows separately, can mirror alternate tiles so they meet edge to edge, and can stagger alternate rows into a brick course. Radial Repeat's fan seam was nailed to 0° with no way to turn or animate it and its wedges butt-joined too, so a kaleidoscope came out with a visible hard edge every segment — the one thing a kaleidoscope should not have. It gets a seam angle, mirrored wedges, and a twist that bends the fan into a spiral. Mirror Tile's reflection lines were welded to the top-left corner of the frame and it always folded on both axes, but where the mirror line falls IS the composition of a mirror effect; the seam moves now, and you can fold on one axis while the other just tiles. The seam fix is measured rather than described: stepping one pixel across a join made the sampled point leap 239.9px of a 240px frame on the grid and 119.9px on the fan, and both go to exactly zero once the tiles mirror. Linear Repeat needed nothing — it was already rebuilt when you reported it squishing.
- v9.03 — **The typewriter reveals by word now, and the timer can count down (EFFECTS-PLAN round 18).** Text Progress could only run left to right one LETTER at a time, which is the wrong unit for a caption — it puts half-words on screen every frame. It reveals by Letter, Word or Line now, from the Start, the End or outward from the Middle, and it can show a caret (/, _ or a block) while it is still typing. Timecode could only count UP from zero at the clip's start: no start offset, so a broadcast 01:00:00:00 head was unreachable; no countdown, which is the single most common use of a timer on social video and simply did not exist; and no way to read project time, so a timer restarted at every cut instead of running through. All three are there. Text Spacing always OVERRODE the layer's own tracking rather than offsetting it, so an effect meant to nudge spacing silently threw away whatever the layer was set to — it can add to the layer now, with Replaces kept as the default so nothing existing moves. **Not done, and noted rather than quietly dropped:** the word-spacing and line-height two thirds of that same request. They need the text renderer itself to carry two more fields and the line-breaking loop to honour them, which is a layout change rather than a slider, and bolting it onto this round would have been the wrong place for it. Footnote worth keeping: the carousel gate added at v8.98 caught me featuring all three of these, and text effects can only answer a tap with a refusal toast on any layer that is not text. The comment I left last time did not stop me doing it again — the test did, which is the whole argument for making safeguards structural.
- v9.04 — **Starfield, Grunge, Light Leak and Blink stop being one look each (EFFECTS-PLAN round 19).** Every star was a single pixel of one flat colour — at 1080p that is sensor dirt, not a starfield, and a one-pixel highlight is exactly what a video encoder throws away, so it also shimmered to nothing on export. Stars have a size, a brightness spread and a twinkle now. Grunge was a single-pixel speckle that always dried to black, which reads as compression noise rather than grime; it gets a grain size, a darkness, and rust/sepia/soot. Light Leak always entered from the top-right at a fixed size, so it could not be placed to match where the sun actually is in the shot. Blink was one hard 50/50 square wave locked to t=0: no short flash, no dimming instead of vanishing, and two blinking layers could never alternate because every instance read the same clock — duty, dims-to and phase fix all three. **I wrote the twinkle wrong first and the test caught it**, which is worth recording: the per-star phase came from a hash with no avalanche step, so on a 96px plate every star got a phase of about 0.004 and they all dimmed in lockstep — a global flicker, not a twinkle. It was invisible at the obvious sample time too, because sin(1.5) is 0.997 and the dimming rounded clean away, so the control read as completely dead rather than wrong. Fixed to use the same mixing the line above it already used.
- v9.05 — **A tab showing an older copy of a project can no longer save it over your newer work (#306).** You reported that refreshing sometimes brings back an older version of the project, and that you had said so before. Here is what I found. Two tabs on the SAME project were completely unguarded: tab A holds an old scene, you do real work in tab B, then you switch back to tab A — and tab A's hidden-tab handler immediately writes its stale scene straight over the good one. Refresh, and you get the older version. On a phone that is not an edge case at all, because backgrounding the browser fires that handler in every open tab at once. The existing protection only ever covered a different case (a second tab open on a DIFFERENT project). Worse, nothing could even detect it: a saved project carried no notion of which of two versions was newer, so the app could not tell your project from an old copy of it. Every save now stamps a version number that only goes up, and a save that would move it BACKWARDS is refused — the tab says so and stops writing until you reload, rather than silently destroying the newer file. The same change closes a second route to the same symptom: a save that fails silently (a full store, which is much easier to hit on an iPhone) used to be indistinguishable from one that worked, so the app carried on as if saved and the next reload served whatever was last written successfully. Writes are now read back and verified. **Honest limit: I could not reproduce your particular instance** — this machine's browser allows far more storage than a phone does, so I could not make a save fail here. What I did was find the mechanisms that produce exactly the symptom you describe and close them, with a test that drives the two-tab sequence and fails if the older scene ever wins. If it happens again, tell me and it is a third cause.
- v9.06 — **Glass stops looking like TV static, Fractal Warp finally moves (EFFECTS-PLAN round 20).** Glass regenerated its distortion for every individual PIXEL, which is the one setting at which it reads as television static instead of glass — real glass distorts in facets several pixels across. It has a facet size now, a rain-streak mode that displaces along one axis only, and a seed to reroll the pattern. Fractal Warp's noise field never moved at all, and organic boiling churn is the single thing a fractal warp is for; it now has a churn speed, a feature size, and an octave count so you can have a slow smooth swell instead of fine detail. Channel Remap was completely binary — the whole swap or nothing — so it could only ever be a novelty rather than something you would leave in a finished grade; it takes a mix, and a Keep-brightness option so a swap reads as a colour shift rather than also changing how light the shot is (swapping red and blue on a sky otherwise brightens it by a third). **One high-impact effect is deliberately left: Stroke.** Its request is for an inside or centred outline and a ROUND corner, and the current one is a square-by-construction box dilation — a round stroke needs a real distance transform, which is a build rather than a slider. Doing it properly is its own job rather than something bolted onto the end of this round.
- v9.07 — **Stroke can go inside, sit centred on the edge, have round corners and a soft edge (EFFECTS-PLAN round 21).** This was the last high-impact effect in the plan and the only one that was not simply a missing slider. The outline was drawn by growing the shape in a square — which is what a box dilation does — so an inside or centred stroke and a ROUND corner were not omitted, they were impossible with that method. It now measures the real distance from every pixel to the edge of the artwork, and all four controls fall out of that one thing: outside is a band beyond the edge, inside is a band within it, centred is half of each, and softness is just where the band stops. The difference is easy to state: on a circle, the old square method bulges out to 14.25px on the diagonals when you ask for 10px, while the round one holds exactly 10px the whole way round. Inside now adds nothing at all outside the shape, which is the entire point of it — text and logos keep their silhouette instead of getting fatter. Defaults are untouched and measured unchanged (22ms against 23.8 before, because it is literally the old code path), so every existing stroke renders byte-for-byte as it did. The new options cost more: 35ms for a square inside stroke, 64ms round, and 128ms for round+centred+soft, which is the one combination that has to measure both sides of the edge. **That empties the high-impact column of the effects plan** — 33 smaller ones remain.
- v9.08 — **Sharpen stops amplifying grain, Box Blur can smear one way, and Inner Blur stops drawing a dark rim (EFFECTS-PLAN round 22).** Sharpen only ever crunched a one-pixel halo at full strength everywhere, so flat sky and skin had their grain amplified exactly as hard as a real edge; it now has a radius (wide radius is local-contrast "clarity" rather than edge sharpening), a threshold that leaves flat areas alone, and a brightness-only mode so colour noise is not multiplied along with the detail. Measured: the grain in a flat half went from 3596 changed pixels to zero with the threshold on, while the real edge beside it still sharpened. Box Blur was locked to a square kernel and one pass — it can smear purely horizontally now, and repeating the pass loses the hard boxy falloff (a straight 20px ramp becomes a soft 44px S-curve). And Inner Blur had an actual bug: it averaged colour while ignoring transparency, so near the edge of any shape it mixed in the transparent BLACK outside it and left a dark rim that had nothing to do with your artwork — on a flat disc the rim dropped from 230 to 152. "Ignore outside" fixes it, and is off by default because that rim is what your existing projects currently render. Gaussian Blur is in this family on the plan but is not done: it is a CSS filter, so laying softness over a sharp image needs a compositing path rather than a slider, and that is its own job.
- v9.09 — **The four film-look grades stop being one slider each (EFFECTS-PLAN round 23).** Each of these had its character welded into a constant, so the single Amount slider dragged several unrelated things along together. Bleach Bypass tied colour loss to contrast harshness — but keeping the bleached CONTRAST while keeping the COLOUR is the entire point of the real process, and now you can: saturation holds at 0.72 against the source's 0.70 while the tonal range still stretches from 128 to 242. Teal & Orange split warm from cool permanently at mid-grey, so whether faces landed in the orange half was decided by how the shot happened to be exposed rather than by you; the split point moves now, and the crossover can be tight or gentle. Faded Film drove the milky blacks, the contrast crush, the desaturation AND a warm cast off one control, so a COOL faded look simply did not exist — the tone control crosses zero into blue now. Cross Process had its whole look in six hardcoded numbers; the colour cast and the curve are separate, and turning the cast off leaves just the gamma.
- v9.10 — **Bend, Squeeze, Inner Pinch and Tunnel can be aimed (EFFECTS-PLAN round 24).** Bend only bowed left/right with the arc always peaking at the vertical middle; it can bow up/down now and you choose where the peak lands. Squeeze's waist was likewise nailed to the middle and always pinched horizontally. Inner Pinch's disc was hardcoded at 60% of the frame, dead centre — its size and placement being the only two things that matter for a localised pinch. Tunnel's mouth was stuck at 30% of the frame and locked to the centre, so it could be neither resized nor aimed. **And this round caught a real bug in my own work, which is worth writing down.** Squeeze's original line reads `Math.PI*y/H`, which JavaScript groups as (PI × y) ÷ H — not PI × (y ÷ H). Routing the same number through the second form changed 357 of 2030 test points at the DEFAULT settings, i.e. it would have silently re-rendered every squeeze in every existing project, with nothing visible going wrong. The plan warns that comparing new code against itself passes when both sides are equally wrong; this is that warning coming true, and the suite now carries its own written-out copy of the original arithmetic so it can never happen quietly again. It took two attempts to write that check properly — the two groupings agree on about 82% of the frame, so four sampled points all landed in the agreeing majority and proved nothing.
- v9.11 — **The app now looks like itself from the very first frame (towards #306/#292).** `theme-glass.css` — 475 lines, 75 rules — only applies when the page carries `data-theme="glass"`, and that was being set by settings.js, which loads at the BOTTOM of the page after about 1.5MB of scripts. So every load painted the bare stylesheet first and only snapped to the real appearance once that script ran. The comment in settings.js spelt out the consequence itself: with the attribute absent the app falls back to the bare stylesheet, "which is exactly the Classic look". It is in the markup now, so it is in force the moment the page parses, and settings.js merely agrees. The guard reads index.html as SHIPPED, because by the time any test runs settings.js has already set the attribute and checking the live page would pass either way. **Being straight about this: I do not think it is the bug you reported.** I rendered the page with every script stripped — which is literally first paint — before and after, and the layout was already correct both times; the fix changes colours (the inspector goes from a blue-grey to the proper dark teal) rather than the shape of the app. It is a real flash and it is gone, but it is not an old-looking version of FreeMotion.
